## Fixed in claude/cleanup-motion

The motion and rendering review at `62637f4`. Each fix has a regression test that fails on the old code. Real data (private, not committed): every cached dangle rig and graph pair (vanilla `hh_033` and `hh_107`, and five CCXL sets) compiles to byte-identical specs before and after, and 600 frames of a head sway give bit-identical solver output; a frame costs 10.5 µs (`hh_033`) and 17 µs (`hh_107`) against the 0.5 ms budget. The §5.6 vectors still pass (`tests/dangle-solver.test.ts`).

- **PREV-129 (High):** `compileDangleSpec`'s constraint walk refuses a group (`animDyngConstraintMulti`) reached a second time and counts every visited entry, empty ones included, against `DANGLE_LIMITS.constraintVisits` (8192; the vanilla `hh_033` graph visits 65, `hh_107` 53). A leaf listed twice is still read twice. The reviewer's fan-out 32 at depth 8 (about 1e12 visits) is refused at once. Tests: `tests/dangle-spec.test.ts` ("hostile and extreme graphs").
- **PREV-130 (Med):** `rangeProblem` (compile and parse) refuses mass outside 1e-3…1e3, negative or huge damping and pull, a pull spring with `(pull / mass) · h² > 1`, forces over 1000, link bounds over 10,000 %, ellipsoid scales outside 1e-3…1e3, sizes over 10 m and frames over 1 km; such a chain hangs rigid with a note. `DangleSolver.frame` resets a frame that ends non-finite to the input pose and counts it (`unstable`); `DangleRig.notes` says so. Tests: `tests/dangle-spec.test.ts`, `tests/dangle-solver.test.ts` (the ellipsoid scale 0 case).
- **PREV-133 (Low):** framing measures the head's phase-zero offset with `IdleAnimation.offsetAt` (poses the drivers, reads the composed world matrix, poses them back), so it no longer seeks 0 and back (the 72 mm tip jump). The dangles' run from a loop start is checkpointed (every 60 frames or more, at most 32 a loop) during seeks and uninterrupted playback until the seam, and a later seek resumes bit for bit from the latest checkpoint; a change to the dangles' input drops them. Reproduction (400 joints): a second seek in a 20 s loop 1.1 ms (was about 48 ms), 60 s loop 2.3 ms (was about 133 ms). The first seek to a late phase still simulates from the loop start (48 ms at 20 s, about 1.4 s at the 600 s cap), since the state there depends on it. Tests: `tests/dangle-motion.test.ts` ("seeks and framing"), `tests/idle-animation.test.ts`.
- **PREV-134 (Low):** a seek counts a face's one-shot showcase from motion zero (as turning the idle on does), so a sought pose never depends on when the idle was chosen; choosing it during playback still starts the showcase then. **No re-bake:** the bake's `(t − intro) mod D` after the showcase matches the face graph, whose second `closeup` state's `ui_closeup_shot` node has `resume: 0` and whose transitions have no `syncMethod` [resource: `player_woman_paperdoll_sermo.animgraph`], so the loop starts again on entering it; the face loop (22.07 s) and body clip (12.33 s) are separate graphs with no shared phase. The residual pop when choosing the idle mid-play is PREV-141. Test: `tests/idle-animation.test.ts`.
- **PREV-138 (Low):** a layer without a readable microblend samples `DEFAULT_MICROBLEND` (`default.xbm`: RGB 126/127/255, alpha 104/255) in the program and its CPU twin instead of alpha 1, and `stackProblems().microblends` counts masked drawn layers whose coverage depends on it, which the adapter notes. The vanilla ring layer over its 0.11 fill now takes 0 there (was 0.22). Tests: `tests/layered-material.test.ts`.
- **PREV-139 (Low):** the host's compile enforces the parse's rules (joint names ≤ 256, paths ≤ 512, notes ≤ 300 with file names clipped, integer parents, joints within 1 km) with a `DangleSpecError` and its reason, and `serveDangle` stores only a spec `parseDangleSpec` accepts. Tests: `tests/dangle-spec.test.ts`.
- **PREV-140 (Low):** Clear and eviction remove through `removeTree`: one bounded call per file and folder, and a link (a junction included) is removed, never listed. A synthetic 6,800-file Clear takes 345–385 ms (was 513–581 ms). Test: `tests/choice-prefetch.test.ts`.
- **CORE-108 (Low):** `FacialHost.clearPrepared` keeps the stopped preparation current until its files are gone (its solver is never restarted), a preparation started meanwhile waits for the clear, and a failed preparation is started again on a question after 30 s, doubling, at most three times (`PREPARE_RETRY_MS`). Tests: `tests/facial-host.test.ts`.

## Fixed in claude/cleanup-save-native

- **SAVE-11 (High):** one decoded-values budget per reading (1,000,000; per chunk 500,000; largest real object 33,114), no spare per-value objects, and the loadout decodes one owner at a time keeping only the player's: the crafted 7.6 MB package went from a 0.9 GB peak to about 95 MB, then refused.
- **SAVE-12 (High):** chunk tables are two number arrays (`chunkCount`, `chunkType()`, `chunkStart()`, `chunkEnd()`, `chunk()`), a per-save chunk budget of 131,072 (a real save holds 2,530; a package over it shows as bytes with a plain note), and the explorer keeps one status byte and one note index per object: +234 MB → +36 MB on the reproduction.
- **SAVE-13:** a check replaces only its node's row; 12 ms slices, publishing at most every 250 ms (or 4× the last publish cost): 20,000 tiny packages went from about 70 minutes of main-thread work to 1.1 s.
- **SAVE-14:** each package name is charged to the name budgets once.
- **SAVE-15:** the world-object index keeps 4-byte entry starts and builds entries on demand (`count`, `entry()`, `isFilled()`, `classHash()`): +133/+72 MB → +4/+4 MB.
- **NATIVE-65:** fixed-size arrays refuse a file count above N, a vertex layout over 32 elements is refused, only the two UV sets the GLB uses are decoded, and the estimate counts influences beyond eight.
- **NATIVE-66:** all morph targets' reads together are capped at twice their diff and mapping buffers (real targets read exactly 1×).
Real saves read identically (`readSavedV` and the explorer's views, four saves), the mesh oracle matches its baseline (630 resources), and 83,132 real resources decode with identical outcomes. Full suite 2,422 pass.

## Fixed in claude/lighting-setups

- **PREV-131 (Med):** the studio rig is gone: every setup's lights are one list in `lighting-setup-stage.ts`, whose `setShadowMapSize` returns early for an unchanged size, so a preview-quality notice that keeps the size keeps the maps; a new size changes the shadow fingerprint (map size is part of it), so the next frame draws them. `tests/lighting-preset.test.ts` "shadow maps survive a quality notice…".
- **PREV-132 (Low):** `LightingSetupStage.restore()` (called on `webglcontextrestored`, which also requests a frame) clears the shadow fingerprint, so the next frame draws the restored context's empty maps again. Same test.
- **PREV-135 (Low):** the untouched token is exactly `{intensity, cone, exposure: 0.46}` with no `shadows`; a chosen value equal to it is stored with `shadows` written out and reads back as chosen. `storedViewGraph` writes every known lights node's calibration through `storedCreatorLighting`, as `preview` does, so a second view keeps following refits. `tests/lighting-preset.test.ts` "a chosen calibration equal to the token's…".
- **PREV-136 (Low):** `shadow-filter.ts` is now Castaño's optimized PCF: a 3-, 5- or 7-texel tent from 4, 9 or 16 hardware-filtered taps (no more than the old grid's 16), positions and weights following the receiver inside its texel, so consecutive taps are at most two texels apart and every texel under the tent is read. `tests/shadow-filter-tent.test.ts` simulates the tent and the old grid across an edge: the tent falls monotonically with no flat treads; the old grid at the 1K and 2K radii had treads over a texel long. The widest tent (7 texels) caps the radius, so 2K maps draw a sharper edge than the 3 mm target (knowledge/creator-lighting.md §9).
## Fixed in claude/cleanup-mottle

- **PIPE-113 (Med):** plate reach judges a mottled layer on its mottled coverage as the verifier reads it: the 4096 reference raster's own texel values (mottle at that raster's spacing, `layerCoverageSampler` with `mottle`), bilinear at each plate sample (`plate-reach.ts` `mottledReach`); unmottled layers keep the point test. Check and Build share `preparePackageCollection`, so both omit a contact that mottles away (for a one-layer preset the test is the verifier's own `authored` count). `REFERENCE_GRID` now shares `COVERAGE_REFERENCE_GRID`. Test: `tests/plate-reach.test.ts` (PIPE-113, a 33,700-sample plate: omitted by Check and Build alike, the verifier refuses its build, a kept mottled edge passes). Offline sweep: 150 kept low-contact mottled presets all passed the verifier; every dropped one had `authored` 0. Pipeline guide updated (step 4 and the mottle paragraph); the three diagrams were rendered and inspected (unchanged; visual-review row 2026-09-27).
- **CORE-109 (Low):** the mixture cache holds 32 (a look's `MAX_LAYERS`), each seed keeping its two latest clumpings, and over the cap a seed's second clumping goes before any seed's only one (`mottle.ts`). Measured at 512² (the reviewer's script): 8 / 9 / 12 layers 2.8 / 67.6 / 68.3 ms per layer raster before, 2.7 / 2.7 / 2.6 after. Test: `tests/mottle.test.ts` (32 seeds reused; a 41-step clumping drag evicts no other layer's mixture).
- **CORE-110 (Low):** `skin` is required on `raster`, `rasterWindow` and `createRasterJob`; the tools and experiment scripts pass `EYE_MAKEUP_REGION.skin`. Test: `@ts-expect-error` calls (checked by `bun run check`) and `tools/raster-performance.ts` run over a mottled recipe.
- **CORE-111 (Low):** `readLayers` refuses a layer key outside every key a build has written (`LAYER_KEYS`, the legacy `field` included): `NewerDataError` in the current form, a plain invalid-layer error in an older recipe file. A survey of 4,849 stored layers (fixtures, evidence, local libraries and backups) found no other key. Test: `tests/recipe.test.ts` (CORE-111). The key list is a record over `keyof Layer`, so a new layer field does not compile until it is listed.

Unmottled rasters are unchanged (frozen hashes pass). The 2K Backdrop reference in Bun, 9 runs, before/after alternating twice: unmottled medians 622/600 before, 628/598 after, minima 594/593 and 599/592, SHA-256 `1dc9372a…` throughout; mottled Powder/Mascara/Cream within the same noise with unchanged hashes.

## Fixed in claude/cleanup-components

The component library and dock findings of the review at `62637f4` (UI-117..124). Each has a test that fails on the code before the fix; the headless Chrome ones (`tests/tree-view-geometry.test.ts`, `tests/dock-strip-stack-geometry.test.ts`) skip where there is no local Chrome, as the other geometry tests do. Before and after captures in light and dark at 390 and 1280 px wide, with measurements in `run.json`, are in the ignored `projects/xf-studio/authoring/evidence/screenshots/cleanup-components/` (the empty tree, the loading status scrolled, folded strips after 900 → 240 → 900 px, and the pair control's toggle).

- **UI-117 (Med):** the TreeView is now a frame the owner sizes, holding the empty message at its top, then the scrolling `role=tree`; the spacer no longer has `min-height: 100%`, which had pushed the message to the bottom edge (measured: 199 px down a 200 px tree before, 1 px after). Test: `tests/tree-view-geometry.test.ts`.
- **UI-118 (Med):** a ResizeObserver on the scrolling tree paints the rows in view whenever its size changes (after growing from 200 to 800 px: 16 of 29 rows drawn before, 29 of 29 after). Test: `tests/tree-view-geometry.test.ts`.
- **UI-119 (Med):** when the focused item leaves the list while the tree has focus (F or the star on a Favourites row, a new search result), focus moves to the item that took its place, else the one before; a row rebuilt around its focused star keeps focus on the row. Tests: `tests/ui-components.test.ts` (F, the star, the last item, a staying row) and the real-browser F case in `tests/tree-view-geometry.test.ts`.
- **UI-120 (Med):** a stack of vertical strips shares its column by each header's natural length (every label whole: `TabStrip.fullLength`, `PanelHeader.naturalLength`, set on the cell after each fit through `HeaderFitter`'s new callback) instead of its current, condensed length, so the layout at any height matches a fresh render whatever the order of past resizes, and a strip gets its labels back when the window grows (900 → 240 → 900 px: icons at 436 px before, full at 526 px after). The ResizeObserver loop warnings the old feedback raised are gone too. Test: `tests/dock-strip-stack-geometry.test.ts`.
- **UI-121 (Low):** the ratchet's scan now sees any quotes, `h` aliases, tags held in variables, any document's `createElement`, quoted `"role"` keys, `setAttribute`/`setAttr` roles, the button, grid, searchbox, textbox, scrollbar and menu-item roles, a focusable `separator`, a library class anywhere in a class list (never a longer class such as `btn-row`), `classList.add`/`toggle`, `className =`, and markup in strings; `codeOnly` treats a `/` opening the line after a `}` as a regular expression, so code after one is scanned. Its one real miss, the dock's focusable splitter, now composes a new library `Splitter` (`components/splitter.ts`, with its style-guide entry), which the Split view's gutter uses too. Tests: `tests/ui-component-ratchet.test.ts` (every case the reviewer listed, and the look-alikes it must ignore), `tests/dock-collapse-dom.test.ts` (the dock's splitter keys).
- **UI-122 (Low):** on a folded, vertical strip Up and Down switch tabs and, with Alt+Shift, reorder them, as Left and Right do on a bar (which still work); on a bar Down still moves into the panel. The key reference says so. Test: `tests/dock-collapse-dom.test.ts`.
- **UI-123 (Low):** rows and groups are keyed by kind and ID, so a row may share its ID with a group (documented; `focusItem` takes an optional kind); the loading status is pinned to the frame's corner, clear of the scrollbar, and never scrolls; the status and the empty message sit outside `role=tree`, which holds only its items. Tests: `tests/ui-components.test.ts`, `tests/tree-view-geometry.test.ts`.
- **UI-124 (Low):** PairControl's toggle is named "Link sides" whatever its state, which `aria-pressed` carries (its icon shows the link whole or broken, a new `unlink` icon), and uses the unavailable pattern (`aria-disabled` with its reason, focusable) instead of native `disabled`; GroupSection leaves a group the person folded during a search alone for the rest of that search; Combobox shows the owner's value when closed, focused or not, so a refused choice reverts at once; FolderSetting turns a rejected promise into a plain refusal on the note line ("Couldn't change the folder. Try again.") with the controls back. Tests: `tests/ui-components.test.ts`.

## Fixed in claude/scroll-on-resize

- **UI-135 (Med, maintainer report):** every `ScrollMemory` container observes its own size. Its anchor is recaptured on every scroll (and after a settled restore or hold), so it is never stale when a resize arrives; a height-only change keeps the scroll position; a width change restores the pre-resize anchor to minus its clip offset, and a correction's own scroll event keeps that anchor, so a drag back returns exactly. Skipped while the person is scrolling (wheel, touch or key within 500 ms, or a pointer held in the container). Re-rendered dock bodies (float, dock, layout commits) already restore from the anchor recorded at teardown. Tests: `tests/view-state-persistence.test.ts` "resizing keeps the place".

## Fixed in claude/ui-state-persistence

- **UI-133 (Med, maintainer report):** one generic remembered view state in the library (`studio-ui/view-state.ts`, `studio-ui/scroll-anchor.ts`): every fold (GroupSection, the Character panel's groups, sections, rows and author groups, a tree owner's `RememberedSet`) and every panel's and TreeView's scroll position persist in the workspace's UI preferences (`expanded`, `scroll`; bounded, least recently set first; the older `folded` list migrated). A scroll position is the element at the top edge plus its clip offset, restored once async or virtualised content arrives, falling back to the nearest earlier element then the plain position, and dropped when the person scrolls first. Workspace state, not layout state; never Undo. Tests: `tests/view-state-persistence.test.ts`, the Character panel's reload test.
- **UI-134 (Med, maintainer report):** a person's choice never moves their view: choosing holds the container's anchor for a moment (`holdScroll`: rebuilds, focus and layout moving above are corrected), a rebuild only unfolds the group holding the V's choice without scrolling, focus comes back with `preventScroll`, and the V's choice is brought into view only when the person opens the row, within the panel only and never during a restore (`revealInView`). Test: clicking a picture leaves the panel's scroll position unchanged through a rebuild, a line appearing above and a programmatic jump.

The brow swatch now reads a decal's diffuse texture at 512 px (`DECAL_TEXTURE_SIDE`, `swatchTextureSide`) and weights the brow's tone by coverage squared, as the program squares the filtered primary alpha; swatch rules v2 recompute every cached swatch. Measured in [hair shading §6.1](../../knowledge/hair-shading.md#61-what-a-brow-colour-looks-like-for-a-swatch); tests in `tests/cc-swatch.test.ts`.

## Fixed in claude/fix-plate-seam

- **PREV-145 (Med):** a garment's entry carries its own components, so a skinned part looks up its dangle among them. Found while loading the reference save for the plate-seam diagnosis (host error `character_failed`). Test: `clothing-dressing.test.ts` plans a skinned garment part.
- **PREV-146 (Med, maintainer report):** diagnosed by elimination with `tools/plate-seam-look.ts` (reference V, creator and studio lights, scatter on, wrap and bare, shadows off, a bare frame as the plate-hidden reference): the ratio of each frame to the bare one steps exactly along the plate's jagged top outline, and the scatter adds only a smooth 0.2–0.7 % brightening just outside it. The maintainer chose to warn, not fade: output is unchanged, and `layerSurfaceEdge` (`engines/layered-makeup/surface-edge.ts`) finds the plate's outer-edge segments where a layer still has 2/255 coverage (about 1 ms per changed layer). The Layers row shows a muted info flag that says to pull the shape in or soften the edge less, and the UV map draws the plate outline faintly with the cut part in orange. ## Fixed in claude/fix-expression-drift

- **PREV-144 (Med, maintainer report):** `composePreviewMotion.attach` binds the owner of the bones last (the idle while enabled, else a held expression, else the blink), so every motion captures the neutral pose the bones arrive in and only then does the owner pose them. Morph weights were ruled out: a detail's are copied from the head's as absolute values on every swap (`setCharacterDetails`, `syncFollowers`); only the captured neutral pose accumulated (world-matrix elements drifted by up to 1.9 after 21 swaps before the fix, below 1e-9 after). In a `?verify=1` workspace with Mild surprise held, ten alternations of Hairstyle 01 and 02 visibly changed the lids and brows before the fix; after it, the 3D view's frame is pixel-identical after 10 and 12 alternations. Tests: `game-blink-composition.test.ts` alternates hairstyles 20 times with an expression held (idle off, and idle playing over it) and with a held closure, and checks the pose matches one swap and that releasing restores rest exactly.

## Fixed in claude/fix-eye-drag

- **UI-116 (Med):** picking tests the visibility a depth-tested handle is drawn with: head and eyes clear of its drawn position within 1 mm, and a camera-facing plate triangle, with grace for an edge-on margin. There is no re-hit of the plate. `SurfaceMap` can bridge interior UV holes (`uvHoleBridges`: the rim loop triangulated on its best-fit plane), and active drags and Shift gestures fall back to the bridge under the pointer. Only the head hides a bridge, so the eyeball never stops a drag, while fresh presses on the eye still orbit. Controls inside the opening draw on the bridge without depth, and a dragged one stays pinned where it was dropped. Outlines and tangent frames stay on plate triangles, because the slit is only about 0.002 V wide in UV. Contract: [editor invariants, eye openings](editor-invariants.md#surface-editing-and-viewport). Tests: `tests/surface-eye-drag.test.ts` (synthetic rolled-rim opening; the real plate's lash-line point near the outer corner dragged to the lower lid), and the pinched-hole case in `tests/surface-eye-handles.test.ts`. Browser capture: `tools/eye-drag-look.ts`, run in `?verify=1` on the default V. It finds the point pickable, follows it over the eye to the lower lid, and records one Undo step ("Move point").

## Fixed in claude/hair-physics

- **UI-115 (Low):** a reserved note line that was empty took the empty-state box's padding and border (the global `.empty` class shares the name), so every control with a reserved note (switches, sliders, segmented controls) left a gap about 26 px tall instead of one text line (15.6 px), and grew or shrank by 10 px as a note came and went. `.control-note.empty` now drops them (measured in `?verify=1`: 26 → 15.6 px). Found converting the Motion panel's Body source to the library `Segmented` (now with `setOptions`, the even-tile `wrap` layout, a group disabled state and a reserved note; `tests/segmented-choices.test.ts`).

- **PREV-110 (Med):** a part skinned to a dangle component now carries that component's spec (`dangle-host.ts`), and `IdleAnimation.bind` binds each of its chain joints to the part's own driver for that joint (`DangleRig.driverFor`), which poses it from its rig parent: with physics off the whole chain follows `Head` rigidly, as the game would with `alpha` 0; the joints V has (`Head`, `Neck1`) still bind to V's by name, and `nearestDriver` no longer reaches any chain joint. Tests: `tests/dangle-motion.test.ts` (a flat-exported strand sheared by a head turn under the old binding, rigid on the head under the new one, exact restore), `tests/dangle-spec.test.ts`, `tests/dangle-solver.test.ts` (the knowledge page's §5.6 vectors), `tests/hair-physics-surfaces.test.ts`.

## Fixed in claude/cleanup-save-mesh

The Save Explorer and native mesh reader review at `9f71a56` (SAVE-01..09, NATIVE-58..63). Each High and Medium has a regression test built from the reviewer's reproduction, scaled down: `tests/save-hardening.test.ts` (SAVE-01..05, and SAVE-09's fuzz), `tests/save-explorer.test.ts` (SAVE-06, SAVE-08), `tests/save-host.test.ts` (SAVE-07), `tests/save-explorer-panel-dom.test.ts` (SAVE-03's panel), `tests/native-mesh-budgets.test.ts` (NATIVE-58, NATIVE-59), `tests/native-mesh.test.ts` (NATIVE-60, 62, 63) and `tests/choice-prefetch.test.ts` (NATIVE-60's manifests). Real data, private and not committed: `readSavedV` gives the same output on a 9 MB save before and after, and the explorer's summary, tree and mod data are unchanged; the mesh oracle over the Studio's export cache (556 resources) gives the same report, resource for resource, as the run just before the change (every vertex component bit-identical). The reproductions afterwards: eight SAVE-01 packages 418 MB → 13 MB; an 8 MB SAVE-02 entry 231 MB → 1 MB; a SAVE-04 bundle of 100,000 repeated offsets 132 MB → 1 MB; the NATIVE-58 and NATIVE-59 meshes are refused before decoding.

- **SAVE-01 (High):** a save package's names must lie inside its name data (`package.ts` `nameSpan`), and what they decode to is capped at 1 MB a package (`MAX_PACKAGE_NAME_BYTES`) and 8 MB a reading of a save (`NameBudget`, shared by every package the explorer opens). Names stay lazy (CORE-92): a frame decodes one when a chunk or value uses it, and the oracle's candidate names are read one at a time (`packageNames` is a generator over `peekName`) without the frames keeping them. A 2.31 save's ten packages spell out 19 KB of names.
- **SAVE-02 (High):** a world-object entry decodes to at most 50,000 values (`MAX_ENTRY_VALUES`; the largest of a 2.31 save's 99,697 entries: 458), an array past it refused before it is allocated, and every decode reports its `values`. The explorer's decoded entries are bounded by count (4,096) and by values (200,000), oldest first (`cacheStats`).
- **SAVE-03 (Med):** an inspection builds at most 5,000 fields (`MAX_FIELDS`; each level takes its share before its children, the rest counted as `more`), and the panel builds a field's children when it is first opened (`fieldView`, on `toggle`).
- **SAVE-04 (Med):** `readScriptBundleNames` decodes each distinct offset once and refuses more than 32 MB of names (a 2.31 install's 96,452 CNames take 2.2 MB; the same set as before).
- **NATIVE-58 (Med):** what the LOD 1 chunks read stays in proportion to the vertex buffer (`MeshChunk.vertexSpans`, `checkWritable`): their distinct stream ranges must fit it and all their reads together four times it. Chunks do share streams, so a plain sum was refused by real files: the vanilla earring morph targets' instanced copies share their colours and second UVs, and 52 hair meshes give LOD 2 the range of LOD 1; over 718 reference meshes and morph targets the LOD 1 reads come to at most 1.14 times the buffer. The output budget counts the decode's real peak: 640 bytes a LOD 1 vertex (540–640 measured) and 8 an index, plus 128 a morph delta row and 1 KB a target on a chunk (`estimateBytes`), against the unchanged 512 MB (the largest reference mesh is about 330 MB).
- **NATIVE-59 (Med):** targets times chunks is capped at 16,384 (`MeshLimits.maxTargetChunks`; the reference set's largest is 105 × 23) before any header table is walked, the empty accessors count against the output budget, and each target's per-chunk diff and mapping starts are running sums (`diffStarts`, `mappingStarts`), so `chunkDeltas` no longer re-sums them.
- **SAVE-05 (Low):** every declared chunk size is checked against its stored bytes before the stream is allocated (`lz4CanExpand`: an LZ4 block expands at most 255 times), and blocks expand straight into the stream (`decodeLz4Into`).
- **SAVE-06 (Low):** the world-object walk runs in steps of about 50,000 decoded values (`checkStep`), the service yielding between them; inspecting the node before the walk ends takes one step and shows the counts so far (`complete: false`). On a 9 MB save the longest walk step is about 40 ms. The package checks stay one step each (SAVE-10).
- **SAVE-07 (Low):** the host resolves the saves folder once per request with a link or junction at it followed (`resolveSavesRoot`); links below it are still refused.
- **SAVE-08 (Low):** "Open a save file" gets the file's name and size first (`PickedSave`), and a file over 128 MB is refused before its bytes are read.
- **SAVE-09 (Low):** a 2,400-case mutation fuzz over saves (the container and every explorer answer), packages, world-object streams, type databases and script bundles, allowing only the engines' typed errors and the bounded reader's plain ones (30,000 cases passed once locally). It found that a failed node's hex preview threw for a node outside the save's data (now empty, `bytesOf`), and a save string that doesn't decode now raises the reader's plain error rather than the decoder's `TypeError`.
- **NATIVE-60 (Low):** the exporter's readiness check takes a morph target's base (`GameAssetExporter.has(..., base)`) and answers only for the base the cached skin came from, as `cached` does; a choice manifest's morph export names its base mesh and archive (`ManifestExport`, 3 or 5 entries), which must still win.
- **NATIVE-61 (Low):** the mesh and texture readers' cache identities carry the resource reader's version and data hash (`NATIVE_READER_DATA`), so native GLBs and PNGs made by an older resource reader are made again.
- **NATIVE-62 (Low):** a joint count taken from the bone names is capped by `maxBones` like the stored positions.
- **NATIVE-63 (Low):** a LOD 1 chunk without vertices or triangles, or one left without triangles, is refused as unsupported (glTF accessors hold at least one element), so WolvenKit exports it.

## Fixed in claude/ui-components

UI-110..114: the maintainer's dock reports and the Save Explorer layout, fixed through the new component library ([component library](ui-component-library.md)). Tests:
- `tests/dock-summon.test.ts`: the three summon cases, remembered places, focus, and header actions in a collapsed group;
- `tests/tab-strip.test.ts`: the staging rule and fit;
- `tests/ui-components.test.ts`: the components;
- `tests/ui-component-ratchet.test.ts`, `tests/ui-component-library.test.ts`.

The summon test and the header test failed before their fixes. Isolated `?verify=1` captures, with measurements, are in the ignored `authoring/evidence/screenshots/ui-components/`.

- **UI-110 (Med):** a summoned panel landed in an arbitrary group, even a collapsed one.
  - In the default layout, with the right column's lower group collapsed, Help was added as a tab to that group, because its `opensBeside` named the inspectors ("I summoned help, where is it?").
  - Every route now goes through `summonPanel`: the palette, the Panels menu, the Help button, tours and reveals.
    - A panel in an expanded group becomes the active tab.
    - A panel in a collapsed group expands it.
    - A panel not in the layout returns to where it was closed from (`lastPlace`, saved with the layout per size class: its group and tab position, its neighbour, or its floating window) or to its factory group. Otherwise it opens floating.
  - Focus moves to its tab, or into the panel when the opener focuses there (Help's search).
  - `opensBeside` and the catalogue's `homes` are removed, and the Panels menu's row for a collapsed panel now summons it.
- **UI-111 (Med):** a group header's action buttons shrank with the header, and a crowded strip scrolled.
  - A 24 px icon button measured 16 px, so on a narrow or collapsed header the expand button could be squeezed out of reach.
  - Panel headers (`PanelHeader`) now keep their actions whole (`flex: none`).
  - Tab strips (`TabStrip`, `planTabs`) condense in stages: full labels, then truncated labels, then inactive tabs icon-only (the active one last), then an overflow menu. The active tab always stays. Every tab is named by its label and titled by its label and context. They refit on every header resize.
  - This supersedes the UI-96 decision (never bare icons), at the maintainer's request.
- **UI-112 (Med):** controls were built ad hoc across panels.
  - Hand-built `.btn` elements skipped the unavailable guard: toast actions, Character's Details and Show more, the empty dock, and the viewport tool toggles.
  - Hand-built `.icon-btn` elements lacked titles and `aria-disabled` styling.
  - Menu triggers lacked `aria-haspopup`.
  - Three progress bar builds existed, one without `role=progressbar`.
  - The History panel's Undo and Redo used native `disabled`, and blocked history rows had no `data-reason`.
  - All of these are now composed from the library.
  - The remaining 88 ad hoc controls in 18 files (after merging main) are the ratchet's allowlist, which may only shrink. The plan is in the library record.
- **UI-113 (Low):** the style guide documented patterns but not a component library. Section 05 now documents every component with its full contract and a live specimen, and a test fails when an export has no complete entry. It adds "Summoning a panel" (d-summon) to the panel system.
- **UI-114 (Low):** the Save Explorer's sections sat against each other. The title overlapped the All saves button (−8 px), the meta line touched the title, the tree met the inspector, and a node's title and hex dump had no space around them.
  - It is recomposed from `PageHeader`, `SplitView` (with a 16 px gutter, stacking below 560 px), `blockSection`, `propertyList` and `codeBlock`.
  - The gaps are now 13, 13, 16, 8 and 8 px. The hex dump still scrolls horizontally, and behaviour is unchanged.

## Fixed in claude/fix-multilayer-eyes

- **PREV-127 (Med, maintainer report):** "Multilayer ring", "ring black", "target", "target black" and "arasaka" eyes drew wrongly against in-game captures. `microblendContrastFactor` is now `1 / max(contrast, 0.001)` ([multilayered evidence](../materials/multilayered-shader-evidence.md#the-ring-eye-designs-against-the-game-27-september-2026)): the Blender add-on's curve, the wiki's in-game contrast grid and the ring captures agree on it. ## Fixed in claude/perf-incremental

Measured first (`tools/measure-character-page.ts`, page marks in `src/character-timing.ts`; numbers in [mod loading §6](../../knowledge/mod-loading.md#6-implementation-and-reproduction)).

- **PREV-118 (High):** the host takes turns with background work: `src/event-loop.ts` (`timeSlicer`: a macrotask once 8 ms are used); `manifestProblemSliced` (`choice-manifest.ts`) checks a manifest an entry at a time, reading each archive's identity once per manifest (`withArchiveFingerprints`, `resolver-host.ts`); the prefetcher takes a turn between choices (`PrefetchDeps.slicer`) and awaits an asynchronous readiness check; the host's background turn waits while a person's change is asked for, prepared or read and until the page has been quiet for `QUIET_MS` (`CharacterDetailHost.noteAsk`, called by the endpoint and the asset route), and every request stops the batch in progress (`ChoicePrefetcher.pause`); the .NET registry is asked synchronously once, then in the background at most once a minute (`dotnet-runtime.ts`, `forgetDotNetRegistry` on `recheck`); the prepared-files walk keeps 16 calls in flight. The log line gives the wait before starting and the total. On the page: parts are kept for the next change (`DetailPartPool`, keyed by `partKeyFor`), a superseded change's parts are finished and kept, a new load waits for a superseded one to settle, the poll backs off from 25 ms, and another V, a slot no longer drawn (PREV-108) or no V releases kept parts; `NativeAnswerFiles.add` writes its marker off the event loop. Tests: `tests/host-responsiveness.test.ts` (a waiting request is answered within a bound while a row's checks run, and was not before; sliced manifests; a request pre-empts a batch and the next waits for quiet), the part pool in `tests/character-detail-geometry.test.ts`, and GPU memory with kept parts in `tests/webgl-scene-host.test.ts`.
- **PREV-119 (Med):** `platform/scene/camera-settle.ts` watches the camera and its target each frame; the scene host keeps drawing while the last step moved more than 1e-12 m² (what remains is under a hundredth of a millimetre). One per camera node, so views sharing a camera keep drawing together. Tests: `tests/camera-settle.test.ts` (damped frames with no pointer event settle fully; without the watch the loop stopped with over a millimetre left; a still camera draws once).
## Fixed in claude/camera-zoom

- **PREV-114 (Med):** the orbit limits come from the scene ([camera zoom design](camera-zoom-design.md#orbit-limits-come-from-the-scene)). The farthest fits the head, and the body while it shows, around the target at the current lens and aspect with a margin, and always reaches Front view and Whole body; the closest stops 1.5 cm in front of the head's surface on the orbit line. Both are recomputed before every controls update, never move the camera, and lie within `CAMERA_DISTANCE_RANGE` (0.02–100), which `validCameraPose` accepts for the camera codec and `parseWorkspace` alike. Whole body now fits the frame's near face, so wide lenses keep the feet in view. Files: `camera-framing.ts`, `platform/scene/orbit-limits.ts` (new), `platform/scene/scene-host.ts` (limits and framing only), `camera-navigation.ts`, `preview-actions.ts`, `browser-scene-preview-ports.ts`, `preview-view-graph.ts`. Tests: `tests/camera-framing.test.ts`, `tests/view-graph.test.ts`, `tests/application-api-gaps.test.ts`; the isolated `?verify=1` run at 15° in 550×888 and 330×888 panes zoomed out past the whole body and framed it.
## Fixed in claude/head-fixes

PREV-109 (Med, Character plan): a multi-part hairstyle resolved but was never drawn, because the plan picked hair by the vanilla creator slot `hair_color`. Hair is now everything the hairstyle controller draws: every active head option listed in the `hairs` group, whatever its creator slot (`HAIR_GROUP`, `detailSlotOf` in `character-detail-plan.ts`), and the Character panel's coverage uses the same rule (`cc-render-coverage.ts`). The hair label names each part by its own creator label when there are several (`hairLabels`: "hair base: brown liquorice, face frame: ash brown"). "Apply to my V" is still a design (hair colour study §4.4), so there was no code to change; the part-aware apply belongs to that feature. Checked on the diagnostic MO2 profile with "SOPHIE MCH": both parts draw (`evidence/screenshots/head-fixes/after-mch-sophie`, private).

PREV-115 (Med, Materials): the teeth were lit as if outside the head (the preview draws no shadows or ambient occlusion), so the idle's slight lip parting showed bright, forward-looking teeth that the game does not show. The jaw was checked and is not the cause: the lower teeth are skinned to `mid_J_jaw_JNT`, which the idle drives (every teeth bone bound, none unmapped), but the idle opens the jaw by at most 1.45° (about 2.4 mm at the incisors) while it turns the lip joints by up to 11°; and the teeth sit where the game's meshes put them, about 13 mm behind the lip surface with a closed bite. The teeth slot's chunks now take a mouth-interior occlusion whatever their template (`mouth-occlusion.ts`, applied in `materialAdapter`): each vertex keeps the share of the outside it sees through a 10 mm lip parting from its depth behind the drawn head, at least 0.3 (front teeth about 0.36, the back of the mouth 0.3), on every light term. The constants are a Studio choice [hypothesis] until head CC test ask 15.

PREV-116 (Med, Materials): a hairstyle's built-in accessories were resolved but never drawn: the earrings and hair pin of a CCXL hairstyle (a fourth part mesh on `metal_base.remt`) had no adapter, so the component was dropped. `metal_base` is now a render template with its own adapter (`metal-base-material.ts`, rank 1 of the [metal and glass reference](../materials/shader-metal-glass.md#7-recommended-preview-adapters-ranked)): colour × `BaseColorScale`, red-channel metalness and roughness through scale and bias, the RG normal, `LayerTile`. The host now reads `.remt` templates' own names and defaults like `.mt` ones, and a template's `Vector4` inputs reach the record as four scalars (`BaseColorScale.x` …). The same adapter draws the teeth's unreached `default` appearance and `metal_base` garments. Alpha test: PREV-117.

## Fixed in claude/body-fidelity

Three rendering faults the maintainer reported on the reference V in the whole-body view (the torso's sides pinched under the ribs, crushed toes, floating nails), each found offline with measurements ([evidence](../animation/deformation-rig-evaluator.md)):

- **PREV-111 (High):** helper joints bound to the nearest rig segment (`IdleAnimation.nearestDriver`) turned the latissimus, scapula and chest joints with the upper arm, pulling the torso's sides in by 38 mm on average (up to 54 mm) in the close-up idle. The game solves them with the puppet's deformation rig; the Studio now compiles and evaluates it (`deformation-rig.ts`; A-pose check 179/181 within 0.5 mm and 1°): 11 mm on average. Tests: `deformation-rig.test.ts` (every node kind, refusals, program parse), `idle-animation.test.ts` (a helper follows its rig joint, and its segment again without a rig), `native-rig.test.ts`, `render-detail-record.test.ts`.
- **PREV-112 (Med):** a morph target exported without the game folder came out without its skin, so a nails morph mod's nails were bound whole to one bone and floated beside the fingers. The export now runs a skin route (the raw copy exported with the game folder; used only with a skin and the same vertices; once, by a note in the cache entry). Tests: `morph-skin-export.test.ts`.
- **PREV-113 (Med):** the creator's close-up clip, authored for the creator puppet's lifted feet, played on the flat feet a barefoot V wears, sinking the heels 5 cm through the floor and bending the toes. The creator's idles now ask for the creator puppet's feet (`puppet: "creator"`, the feet group its `character_creation` group lists); the inventory's idle, now offered with the game's other preview idles, stands on flat feet. Tests: `idle-catalogue.test.ts`, `uncensored-mode.test.ts`.

## Fixed in claude/fix-dock-collapse

UI-107, UI-103, UI-105 and part of UI-104: the collapse feature of the view graph merge (`635b0d7`). The Character panel v2 merge (`e7eaacb`) had already made the expanded groups of a split share all of its space (`openShares`: grow factors that sum to 1). This branch adds one folding rule for every split, nested split and floating window (view-graph-design.md §4.4). Tests: `tests/dock-layout.test.ts` (the folding rule, splitter pairs, the blank-dock guard, reveal and maximize, parked shares) and `tests/dock-collapse-dom.test.ts` (the rendered cells, fold marks, strip, tab click, reveal, repaired layout). The isolated `?verify=1` run on 27 September reproduced the maintainer's three-group stack: with two groups collapsed the third filled the column (676 of 752 px, header rows the column's full 581 px), with all three collapsed the column became a 34 px vertical strip, and the sizes came back exactly on expanding. Floating composites behaved the same.

- **UI-107 (Med):** a collapsed group in a vertical stack kept `flex: none` inside its row-direction cell, so its header was a short strip of tabs rather than spanning its column. A column holding one group collapsed to a vertical strip, but a column of stacked groups never did: its groups became header rows over an empty column. A collapsed group now folds along its split's axis (a full-width header row in a stack, a vertical strip in a row), a split whose groups are all collapsed folds as one along its parent's axis (`foldAxes`, `splitShares`), and a stack of strips sizes each by its tabs, condensing and scrolling them when short. Splitters resize the nearest expanded groups (`splitterPair`) and are inert beside a folded edge, so dragging never gives a folded cell a size. A tab of a collapsed group expands it.
- **UI-103 (Med):** every reveal goes through `revealPanel`, which expands a collapsed group holding the panel; so do the Show tab submenu and a tab click on a collapsed group.
- **UI-105 (Low):** `setMaximized` expands a collapsed group it maximizes. The last expanded docked group offers no collapse (`lastExpandedDocked`), and a layout with every docked group collapsed (saved earlier, or left so by parking) expands the docked group with the largest share (`keepDockExpanded`), so the dock is never blank.
- **UI-104 (Low, part):** a parked group comes back at its share of the split it rejoins, the siblings giving up theirs (`insertNode`'s whole-split share), so `[.3,.3,.4]` comes back exactly; a floating group comes back collapsed if it was. Composite members still return to their factory home (open).

## Fixed in claude/cleanup-view-graph

UI-102, UI-106, CORE-94..98 and the three unnumbered Lows of the view graph P1+P2 review. Tests: `tests/view-graph.test.ts` (history, codecs, persistence, reserved IDs, kept kinds, camera jumps), `tests/view-modules.test.ts` (registry completeness, tool resolution, readiness, withdrawn tools, `view.endEdit`) and `tests/input-bindings.test.ts` (hints without an editing tool); each new case failed before its fix. [View graph design §6.5](view-graph-design.md#65-phase-status).

- **UI-102 (Med):** tools the presentation doesn't offer are withdrawn, not just hidden. `studio-ui/app.ts` passes `port.views.withdraw(ids)` the tools of hidden modules (and research tools while hidden) whenever the filter changes; the graph keeps each view's choice (saved, in force again once offered) and `PreviewActions` applies `ViewGraph.activeTools`, so Surface controls stops drawing and editing. Tool IDs only reach the application, never module visibility (rule 6 holds). The hint strip follows the view tool marked `editing`: a 3D view without one hints camera input only (`CAMERA_MODIFIER_SUMMARIES`), with no note pointing at Layers or Surface controls.
- **UI-106 (Low):** `view.setTool` is refused `not_ready` ("The 3D view is still loading.") until the preview is attached, so no toggle is made that nothing shows or saves.
- **CORE-94 (Low; High at P4):** every structure edit records a step (default names: New view, Close view, Link/Unlink *slot*); one made without recording (a restore) clears the history; and a replay is checked as a whole before it applies (main view present, unique IDs, every reference resolves to its slot, camera and rig fit), dropping a failing step and the older steps behind it.
- **CORE-95 (Low):** coalescing is by edit, not time: a run of same-key edits is one step until it is sealed by `view.endEdit` (the Camera & light sliders send it on release or at the end of a keyboard burst), a different edit, Undo or Redo. `COALESCE_MS` is gone.
- **CORE-96 (Low):** the node codecs and `parseWorkspace` share one set of ranges (`EYE_SHAPE_RANGE`, `validCameraPose`, the studio exposure and key-angle ranges in `preview-view-graph.ts`); the eye shape is rounded to its index in both.
- **CORE-97 (Low):** a main view whose tools node holds anything but the mirrored Surface controls and Plate wireframe is not the default graph, so it is stored; on read the legacy fields still win for those two and every other tool is kept.
- **CORE-98 (Low):** `uv`, `surface` and `head` (the viewport port's alias for the main view) are reserved view IDs (`RESERVED_VIEW_IDS`).
- **Lows:** each view tool names the action it dispatches (`ViewToolContribution.dispatches`; completeness checks it) and `resolveTool` switches on that action, not on `motion.idle`. `parseViewGraph` keeps views and nodes of unknown kinds (`KeptView`: a newer build's view kind, or a 3D view over an unknown scene, camera or rig kind) and writes them back unchanged, in their stored place, with the nodes they reference; only damage or an unshowable main view falls back to the default graph. A camera jump that left the camera where it was records nothing and leaves no Back step.
## Fixed in claude/bridge-batch4

The runtime-bridge findings of the batch 3 review at `024d960` (RB-42..50), before the next in-game session. Each fix has a test (`xfb_selftest --unit`, the self-test host or `bun test tools`); the same branch adds the CET panel with re-arm and the live-pose write path (both under "New subsystems").

- **RB-42 (Med):** `CancelOpen` now reports what became of the request from the registry (`TakeCreatorOpen` and `OnXFBridgeOpenCreator` record "taken", "switched", "refused" or "expired" with the request's cid): `withdrawn` when nothing can open (still waiting, refused or expired), `taken` when the menu switched for it. `writes::CreatorOpen` answers `creator_open_timeout` ("nothing opened") only on `withdrawn`; after `taken` it polls two more seconds and reports a late screen as opened, otherwise `creator_open_uncertain` (check `game.status`, `cc.back`); a withdrawal that fails is uncertain too. Unit checks for all three.
- **RB-43 (Low):** anything that throws once `open()` has started (the open call itself, a phase poll) withdraws the request before the error goes back, and the message says whether the withdrawal worked. Unit checks for a failing poll and a failing open; the self-test host's polls go through the game-thread queue as the plugin's do.
- **RB-44 (Med):** `FindValue` matches exactly (a whole number as a number); the partial match moved to `FindPartialValue`, which never matches a number and is reported as `matched_by: "partial"`. Card check D7.
- **RB-45 (Low):** `OpenRefusal` refuses when the player isn't V (`PlayerPuppet.IsReplacer`, with `IsJohnnyReplacer` named; `not_v`), and `Open` asks only once the game's `SaveLocksManager` holds the bridge's own reason (`XFBridgeHolds`, an added method reading its private list; linted against the 2.31 bundle; in game unverified). The Johnny refusal: a self-host test (`selftest.phase {player: "johnny"}`) and a unit check that it stops `cc.open` before anything settles or is asked.
- **RB-46 (Low):** every refusal after the first step (settle, open, a poll, the timeout) ends "saving stays locked until a save is loaded (the bridge took its save lock first)"; the tools' wording for `save_lock_not_held`, `creator_open_timeout` and `creator_open_uncertain` says the same.
- **RB-47 (Low):** the CET layer leaves out values over 256 characters, and values past a 60,000-byte answer, naming them under `skipped`; the plugin keeps an overlong value as null and names it (`too_long`) instead of refusing the answer; a refused answer (too large, not JSON, the wrong shape, an unasked name, a non-text value) ends the pending request at once with its reason, which `game.options.read` reports instead of "didn't answer within 3 s" (`OptionsOutcome`, `RenderOptionsResult`, shared with the self-test host). Unit checks.
- **RB-48 (Low):** documented: in-process trust in `core/OptionsExchange.hpp`, the README's security boundary and the design's safety model (a row covering every global native, including the new `XFBridge_Rearm` and `XFBridge_PauseWrites`).
- **RB-49 (Low):** offline coverage of `cc.open`'s refusals (unit: stage 2, a failing poll, not V; self-host: a Johnny section) and of the kill switch during `cc.open` (`tools/test/batch4.test.ts`: the request withdrawn by the kill switch's restore, nothing opened after reconnecting); card checks D2 (a refusal: V in a vehicle) and D3 (a kill right after `cc.open`).
- **RB-50 (Low):** version 0.3.0, packages rebuilt from this branch; hashes in the [test card's build record](../runtime/runtime-bridge-test-card.md#build-record-batch-4-branch-build-not-staged).

## Fixed in claude/fix-ci-linux

CORE-93: the Authoring workflow's Linux job (run 36278774172) failed 13 tests from the settings isolation, repair route and optimistic selection merges; the Windows job passed. The code under test was already correct on each OS (settings follow `localSettingsDirectory`'s per-OS folder; the repair route splits depot paths on `\`); the tests assumed Windows or depended on file order. Each failure was reproduced under WSL Ubuntu with Bun and passes there after the fix.

- **Settings tests** (`tests/host-isolation.test.ts`, `desktop/tests/server.test.ts`): the saved game folders are built with `resolve`, so they are absolute on the OS running the test. The isolated server is started with `process.execPath` rather than `bun` from `PATH`. The host-state test now also checks the Linux (`XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, else `~/.config`) and macOS settings folders.
- **Repair route** (`tests/export-substitution.test.ts`): the fake WolvenKit names the repaired mesh by the depot path's last `\`-separated part, as the code does.
- **Test order** (`tests/light-dom.ts`, `tests/ui-polish-dom.test.ts`): the light DOM now installs and restores `requestAnimationFrame` and `cancelAnimationFrame` itself, and its `document` is an event target whose listeners are cleared on each install, so no DOM test depends on another file's globals. Bun runs files in a different order on Linux, which is why only CI saw it.

## Fixed in claude/fix-texture-fallback

PIPE-108, PIPE-110, PIPE-111, UI-101: no hairstyle drawn on the maintainer's server at `7f7aa3b` ("Part hair_2 … none of its chunk(s) could be drawn"), and a Character-panel click that showed nothing for seconds. Reproduced by preparing the reference MO2 route's saved V with hair colour `24_purple_ombre` through the character-detail host (hair_2 left out on `7f7aa3b`'s reader, drawn after). Tests: `tests/native-hardening.test.ts` (PIPE-110), `tests/character-detail-service.test.ts` (PIPE-108, PIPE-110), `tests/native-texture.test.ts` (PIPE-111), `tests/character-context.test.ts` and `tests/character-panel-dom.test.ts` (UI-101); each failed before its fix.

- **PIPE-110 (High):** vanilla `purple_ombre.hp` and `liliac.hp` (and a hair-colour mod's copies) omit `CHairProfile.sampleCount`, whose class default is 64. No sampled file omitted it, so the learned defaults lacked it and the native reader wrote 0, which `hairProfileStops` refuses: every hair chunk drawn with those colours was dropped as "HairProfile could not be read". The reader now falls back to the class defaults of plain-valued properties (`src/native/rtti-class-defaults.json`, generated by `tools/native-class-defaults.ts` from WolvenKit's generated classes: 375 defaults in 139 classes, none disagreeing with a learned value) before the type's zero; this also covers `CSkinProfile`'s scales, `Quaternion.r` and `entMeshComponent.isEnabled`. `hairProfileStops` and `skinProfileValues` read omitted fields as those defaults. Reader version 4 with the class defaults in its identity, so answers and choice manifests of version 3 are not reused and a restart recovers without Clear.
- **PIPE-108 (Low):** not what dropped the hair (the ALICE DISHEVELED BOB drop carried the same "input couldn't be read" line as the purple ombre profile; PIPE-108 words a part as "WolvenKit couldn't read" its archive), but on the same path: a failed answer from the exporter (a refused texture's WolvenKit fallback launch failing) discarded the archive's geometry and natively decoded textures. What the archive's launches answered is now kept, and the failure still marks the archive so the preparation is degraded and prepared again.
- **PIPE-111 (Med):** MELUMINARY's CCXL cap mask lists a 1024² BC7 chain (1.4 MB) under a 4096² `sizeInfo` while its data holds the whole 4096² chain (22.4 MB); the texture reader refused it as malformed and WolvenKit's fallback was needed. When the table doesn't fit and the data is exactly `sizeInfo`'s chain, the mips are now computed from `sizeInfo` (`TextureLayout.mipTable: "derived"`); the PNG is bit-identical to WolvenKit 9.0.1's. Native PNGs of other textures are unchanged, so `NATIVE_TEXTURE_VERSION` stays 1.
- **UI-101 (Med):** a Character-panel choice was highlighted only when the host's view of the new state arrived. `CharacterContextActions.view` now shows the current state's choices (or the V's own after Reset) on the last view until the host's view of that state arrives and replaces it; `ChoiceList` marks the clicked item chosen in the click, and the chosen item shows "being prepared" while the V is prepared. Measured in an isolated browser workspace with a row being prepared ahead: the highlight moved in the click (1–2 ms) where it took 430 ms with a quiet host before.

## Fixed in claude/native-textures

PIPE-104. Tests: `tests/native-texture.test.ts`; real data: `tools/native-texture-oracle.ts` (59 of 59 comparable textures of the default V bit-identical to WolvenKit's PNGs) and the isolated server's preparation log.

- **PIPE-104 (Med):** the character details' textures are decoded by XF Studio's own reader (native reader phase 3) in a texture worker per game folder, WolvenKit per texture it refuses, and only the served mip is decoded: a body texture mod's 8192² maps are served from their own 4096² mips, so WolvenKit's full-size conversion (7.9 GB) and the host's halving (25.5 s) no longer run. A cold preparation of the default V with the body went from 64 s to 23.1–23.5 s (WolvenKit's geometry export, 17 s, is now the longest stage); the process peaks at 221 MiB for a 4096² map and 300 MiB for an 8192² one. What remains: meshes (phase 4) and `.mlmask` layers still go through WolvenKit.

## Fixed in claude/fix-prepare-loop

PIPE-103, found on the maintainer's server at `ec01c30` and reproduced on `255d01e`. Tests: `tests/character-detail-pages.test.ts` (two pages following different Vs, each through `CharacterDetailActions` onto one host, timed out on the old host and now finish one after the other; a page re-asking every few seconds while its V waits for WolvenKit, NATIVE-47, leaves another page's V to finish; a page still supersedes its own earlier V; two pages on the same V share one run; the device names its page and the endpoint passes it on).

- **PIPE-103 (High):** each open page names itself (`X-XFS-Page`, a random name per page load, `browser-character-detail-device.ts`), the endpoint passes it on (`character-detail-server.ts`; a missing or malformed name is one anonymous page, as before), and `CharacterDetailHost` keeps one run per page: a page's request supersedes only that page's earlier one, another page's waits its turn behind every earlier run (they still run one at a time on the shared cache), and a V another page is already having prepared is shared. `settled`, `cancel`, Clear and `foregroundIdle` cover every page's run. Each preparation's log line now says where its time went, stage by stage, with the native reads, WolvenKit reads and WolvenKit launches each stage made and the exports the export cache answered (`prepareOnce`, `gatherParts`); measurements are in [mod loading](../../knowledge/mod-loading.md).

## Fixed in claude/cleanup-native-catalogue

The native catalogue review at `17d2585` (NATIVE-46..53). Tests: `tests/native-catalogue-texts.test.ts` (the reviewer's reproduction: a worker that briefly can't start with no WolvenKit; a reader that refuses the texts for good; a language no archive holds; the text cache's pruning), `cc-panel-review.test.ts` (the service's rule, Try again, the context's line and next step, the language key), `character-detail-service.test.ts` (the host's early need), `character-switch.test.ts` (the page waiting for WolvenKit), `choice-prefetch.test.ts` (the `setup` stop), `preview-setup.test.ts` (the WolvenKit step with the head ready), `native-resolver-integration.test.ts` (per-kind log room, separate counters).

- **NATIVE-46 (Med):** each text read has an outcome (`readTexts`: read, absent, unreadable, transient). A read that may pass leaves the catalogue's `labels.next` at `retry`: the service serves it with one plain line and Try again but doesn't keep it as final (a question after the PIPE-78 backoff builds it again, a failed rebuild keeps serving it, and Try again builds at once, shown as preparing; the new panel's identity differs, so the page takes it up). Only a language whose game texts no archive holds is "not installed". Texts only WolvenKit could read while WolvenKit isn't set up, with the reader off for good, leave `labels.next` at `wolvenkit`, said plainly with the "Set up WolvenKit" step in the Character panel's status line (a reader off only for a while is `retry`: the registry opens it again and the catalogue with it).
- **NATIVE-47 (Med):** the host's failed state names the need (`need: "wolvenkit"`), and the "WolvenKit missing" line no longer points at the card. The setup service offers WolvenKit's own next step (`wolvenKitStep`: its download consent, the copy found on this computer, .NET, or where it is set; `Open Game & tools` when WolvenKit's own state doesn't know it is missing), which the head viewport's detail line and the Character panel's status line show as a button, working while the card is hidden. Game & tools is only opened, never changed. The page asks the host again every 4 s while it waits, so the V is prepared once WolvenKit is set up.
- **NATIVE-48 (Low):** without WolvenKit the host answers at once, as a need, without resolving the V or decoding the clothing preset, and logs one plain line instead of an error-level failure; a preparation that meets a missing or unrunnable WolvenKit is a need too. Preparing ahead stops as `setup` before its first batch (no `prefetch_failed`); the panel's legend says choices are prepared once WolvenKit is set up.
- **NATIVE-49 (Low):** the fallback log's room is per kind (`LOGGED_FALLBACKS_PER_KIND`, 12 each per session), so a worker outage never keeps a later `native_internal` reader bug out of the log.
- **NATIVE-50 (Low):** `fetchJsonResource`'s null answers that may not repeat count in `jsonTransientNulls`; only the resolver's reads count in `transientNulls`, which the preparation's degraded check reads.
- **NATIVE-51 (Low):** the catalogue's key includes the game's on-screen language (`currentGameLanguage`, read again only when the settings file changes), so a language changed in the game's settings builds it again.
- **NATIVE-52 (Low):** parsed texts another reader wrote are removed in the background, once per cache folder and set of readers per session, only while the native reader is on.
- **NATIVE-53 (Low):** `presetOf`'s comment says what happens: a failure is remembered per archive identity, for the session or for `PRESET_RETRY_MS`.

## Fixed in claude/cleanup-install

The install review at `536bff3` (INSTALL-01..12, UI-98..100). Tests: `tests/mod-install-host.test.ts`, `mod-install-transport.test.ts`, `mo2-modlist-text.test.ts`, `host-isolation.test.ts` (an isolated `server.ts` started with a stand-in `LOCALAPPDATA`), `ui-polish-dom.test.ts`, `desktop/tests/server.test.ts`. Pipeline guide: [Adding a built mod to the mod manager](studio-to-mod-pipeline.md#adding-a-built-mod-to-the-mod-manager).

- **INSTALL-01 (High) and UI-98 (Med):** settings follow the data folder (`src/host-state.ts`): a localhost server with `XFAS_DATA_DIR` or `XFS_SETTINGS_DIR` keeps its settings and receipts there and never reads or writes `%LOCALAPPDATA%\XF Studio`, and doesn't add mods unless `XFS_MOD_INSTALL=on`. A verification workspace uses `/api/verification/local-settings` (its own store in `<data>/verification-settings/`, seeded read-only from the host's settings, `LocalSettingsStore`'s `seed`) and `/api/verification/mod-install` (plans read only; every change refused with a plain reason, `ModInstallPorts.readOnly`; an interrupted install is left for a normal session), on both hosts. `tools/cdp.ts` starts its servers with their own copy of the settings and adding switched off. Tests prove that an isolated server and `?verify` leave the stand-in real settings folder untouched and that their install requests change nothing in the MO2 tree or game folder.
- **INSTALL-02 (Med):** the journal carries the receipt the install was about to write. The next plan recovers: finished when every new file is in place, undone from the verified backup when only some are, forgotten when all are gone; a changed file is named and left alone. The plan says what happened in a note. Finishing a recovery or replacing an `.archive` waits while Cyberpunk 2077 runs, with "close the game" as the step.
- **INSTALL-03 (Med):** a recorded install with a file missing (removed in MO2, or deleted) counts as uninstalled: the plan offers a fresh install and says so. A file of it still present is replaced only if it is byte for byte the recorded one; any other file, or other files in the MO2 folder, still refuse. MO2's own `meta.ini` doesn't make a folder foreign.
- **INSTALL-04 (Med):** receipts are per user on this computer, beside localhost's settings (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\XF Studio\install-receipts`; the desktop app passes the same folder, `DesktopHostOptions.installReceipts`), so the two hosts recognise each other's installs and share one lock. A record in a host's earlier own folder (`<data>/install-receipts`) is adopted once (without its rollback) and set aside there (`*.json.adopted-<time>`). Chosen over a marker file in the mod folder because the direct route shares the game's `archive\pc\mod` with every other mod, and a marker there would be a stray file in the game.
- **INSTALL-05 (Med):** `modlist.txt` is read as bytes (`src/mo2-modlist-text.ts`): UTF-8 with or without a BOM, otherwise the system's single-byte ANSI code page (`GetACP`), used only when encoding the text again gives back the same bytes; otherwise refused with MO2's own re-save as the step. `applyMo2Placement` keeps each row's line ending and trailing spaces and only flips the prefix of an existing row.
- **INSTALL-06 (Med):** the duplicate scan reads only what the game loads: the profile's switched-on mods, MO2's Overwrite and the game folder. The advice fits the place (switch off in MO2, move out of Overwrite, remove a hand-copied file, disable in Vortex and deploy).
- **INSTALL-07 (Low):** the token covers the placement and every change line shown, besides the list's exact bytes.
- **INSTALL-08 (Low):** the new list is written and flushed beside the old one before any mod file is copied, then moved into place; a read-only, hard-linked or missing list is refused in the plan in plain words.
- **INSTALL-09 (Low):** a first install that fails before any file is in place removes the empty folders it created.
- **INSTALL-10 (Low):** one asynchronous `tasklist` run with a 10 s timeout reads MO2 and the game; an unreadable list blocks (fails closed). The `.xl` scan is bounded (enabled mods only, 64 files a folder, 1 MB a file); the plan and install are asynchronous.
- **INSTALL-11 (Low):** a name ending in `_separator` is a rename step on the MO2 route.
- **INSTALL-12 (Low):** the fixture comment now says frameworks are at the top of the pane.
- **UI-99 (Low):** `ModInstallPlan.next` (`setup`, `rename`, `retry` or none); the sheet shows that button (Open Game & tools, Rename the mod, Check again).
- **UI-100 (Low):** the legacy `XF Studio` folder is refused as an early test copy with its action (remove that mod in MO2); a conflicting or duplicate file Vortex deployed (by `vortex.deployment.json`) is to be removed or disabled in Vortex, never renamed.

## Fixed in claude/native-catalogue

The native reader reviews at `e09391e` (NATIVE-26..34, PIPE-102) and `36b5c87` (NATIVE-40..45, NATIVE-44 left open), and PIPE-50 from the creator catalogue review. Tests: `tests/native-resolver-integration.test.ts`, `native-hardening.test.ts`, `native-catalogue-texts.test.ts`, `clothing-host.test.ts`, `character-detail-plan.test.ts`, `cc-catalogue-service.test.ts`.

- **PIPE-50 (Med):** the catalogue's texts go through the installation's fetch port (`ResolverFetcher.fetchJsonResource`): natively first, asking for the `JsonResource` root and the on-screen payload class at `background` priority, so a V's resolution never waits behind them; otherwise WolvenKit's JSON-resource mode, batched with the resolver's other reads, whose output walk keeps a raw `.json` resource apart from its `.json.json` companion. The catalogue host's own WolvenKit batching is gone. Parsed entries are cached per depot hash, archive fingerprint and the reader that answered. The native documents equal WolvenKit's leaf for leaf on all 1,113 text resources of the reference route in 19 languages, after the RTTI slice gained `JsonResource` and the on-screen classes (`tools/native-rtti-subset.ts` roots). A cold female catalogue on the reference route: 5.3 s, 927 texts read natively, no WolvenKit launch (before: 24 launches cold). The route and the catalogue now open without WolvenKit; the character details ask for it only to export (`TOOL_MISSING`, in plain words) and say so on a part only WolvenKit could read (`PlanReaders`).
- **NATIVE-26 (Med):** a decoder that couldn't be opened for a reason that may pass (a signature check timing out, a library in use) is tried again after `NATIVE_RETRY_MS`; a lasting reason (not Windows x64, no library, an unrecognised library) is not (`OodleUnavailableError.permanent`).
- **NATIVE-40 (Med):** `ResolverFetcher.transientNulls` counts null answers whose failure may not repeat, whichever reader failed, and the preparation's degraded check reads it: a native failure that may pass followed by a lasting WolvenKit refusal now marks the V degraded, so it is prepared again and the choice isn't shown prepared. Fixed with NATIVE-29's rule (a decoder off for the session doesn't count).
- **NATIVE-41 (Med):** checked. A CR2W file omits a property equal to its class default, and `rendChunk`'s default `renderMask` is no flags [source: WolvenKit's generated `rendChunk` class, built from the game's RTTI defaults, sets non-zero defaults for its other fields and none for the mask]. So an omitted mask is "no flags", not drawn, as WolvenKit's "0" reads. R12 no longer removes it (`forgetDefaulted` is gone); the note says the chunk was read as its class default (graded [source]).
- **NATIVE-27 (Low):** a marker removed by Clear is written again; markers of another reader identity are pruned once per cache folder and session.
- **NATIVE-28 (Low):** a closed decoder answers `unavailable` (read again later), not `internal`; routes reopen with the new library's decoder.
- **NATIVE-29 (Low):** a decoder off for the session (`lasting` `unavailable`) doesn't keep a failed read retryable.
- **NATIVE-30 (Low):** the reader identity hashes the verified roots and payloads and the default budgets.
- **NATIVE-31 (Low) and PIPE-102 (Low):** the clothing preset decodes in a worker of its own, opened for the read and closed after it, so it never holds up the route's queue; failures are remembered per archive identity. The character-detail service no longer reaches for the route's decoder.
- **NATIVE-32 (Low):** a reader bug is logged with the worker's stack, and a fallback line says WolvenKit *will* read it, or that WolvenKit isn't set up.
- **NATIVE-33 (Low):** decoders of game folders no route uses any more are closed.
- **NATIVE-34 (Low):** `resource-document.ts` records version 3.
- **NATIVE-42 (Low):** a decode worker idle for 60 s is told to close: it releases the game's Oodle library and exits (terminated after 5 s if not), and the next request starts another. Bun 1.4.2's `close` on a `bun:ffi` library does not unload it on Windows, so `oodle.ts` releases the library itself with one `FreeLibrary` for the one load and never calls Bun's `close` [resource: the library copy stayed locked (`EBUSY` on open for writing) after Bun's `close` and became writable after one `FreeLibrary`; with the change, in-process and in a worker after its idle close, and a real decode worked again after reloading]. A worker ended any other way (over its time budget, a crash) still keeps it loaded until exit.
- **NATIVE-43 (Low):** `NativeDecoder.canAnswer` (not closed, not waiting after a failed start); an earlier native answer counts as ready only while it holds.
- **NATIVE-45 (Low):** fallback warnings are logged once per resource and kind, at most 24 per session across every fetcher; the whole V's notes (textures over budget, unread mod files) come before any part's notes under the 32-note cap.
