## Fixed in claude/cleanup-body-clothing

The body and clothing review at `47f581c` (PIPE-97..101, PREV-106..108, CORE-92, NATIVE-25, UI-78..79). PIPE-97 first: the provisional policy is that the Studio never shows more than the game's own uncensored mode, with underwear as the floor ([clothing render backlog](../backlog/clothing-render.md)), so every path that could lose the cover now fails closed, each with its own test.

- **PIPE-97 (High):** `censorRole` (`character-detail-plan.ts`) reads each body option's part in the policy from the creator's own rules: a `cover` (activate-only, no twin: `underpants`), the `uncensored` skin (whose twin is another appearance option on the same slot and link with the opposite action), its `censored` twin, `hidden` parts and `unknown` descriptors. The plan marks covers `cover` and the uncensored skin `covered`, draws the uncensored skin only while every cover the body's consumer groups list is planned (a creator resource with no cover never shows it), otherwise the game's censored skin, otherwise no body with one plain line; the censored twin is planned beside the covered skin (`CharacterPlan.censoredBody`). The host serves covers first (so the texture budget never cuts one) and, when one can't be served, serves the censored twin in place of the covered skin, or withdraws the body (`CENSORED_BODY`, `UNCOVERED_BODY`). The record (`xfs/render-detail-10`) carries `censor` on body parts; its reader keeps covers within the part cap and withdraws the body if a covered part outlives a cover (`withdrawUncoveredBody`, shared with the loader). The loader withdraws and releases the body when a cover fails or is over the texture budget, and reports it in plain words (its earlier dropping of the body's problem line is gone with it). Fail-closed tests, one per path: plan (`character-detail-plan.test.ts` "PIPE-97": markers and order; no `underpants` descriptor; the cover's appearance missing; neither cover nor twin), host (`character-detail-service.test.ts` "PIPE-97": markers served; cover texture unread → censored skin; no twin → body withdrawn), record (`render-detail-record.test.ts` "the underwear floor in the record": a dropped cover; no cover; the part cap; bad markers) and loader (`character-detail-geometry.test.ts` "a cover that doesn't load": the cover's file fails; the cover over the texture budget).
- **PIPE-98 (Med):** an option the merged creator resource doesn't define never draws (`unknown`); a twin must be an appearance option on the same slot and link; covers sort after every other body option; a male V's body is refused in plain words (`BodyScope` `male`) and not resolved. Tests in the same describe.
- **PIPE-99 (Med):** `playerBodyType` (`clothing-resolver.ts`) derives ArchiveXL's body type as `GetBodyType` does: `base_body` unless a body type declared in an `.xl` (`player.bodyTypes`, new in `archivexl-config.ts`) has its tag `Body:<name>` in the player entity's (or an ArchiveXL patch source's) entity or visual tags, or on a component, the last first; declared but unreadable, or ambiguous, stays `base_body` with a gap. Each item evaluates only the suffixes its record lists. Tests: `clothing-resolver.test.ts` (suffix list, body type from a patch, unreadable entity).
- **PIPE-100 (Med):** `records` answers null when the TweakDB can't be read (each item's gap `records-unreadable`, worded as such); a failed resolution is a `ClothingFailure` the plan words as unavailable; a failed preset read is not kept, so the next preparation tries again.
- **PIPE-101 (Med):** `tests/clothing-host.test.ts` (item records with every field and suffix names, the save's record IDs, the preset table, `presetOf`'s failure, retry, shared in-flight read and disk cache, a missing TweakDB), TweakDB Int32 (negative), Bool, Float and `array:CName` (empty, latin-1, UTF-16, back to back, an impossible count), and hostile save packages (below).
- **NATIVE-25 (Med):** the preset is decoded in the native reader's worker (`workerPresetDecoder`: `openNativeDecoderAsync`, the library checked asynchronously; the document decoded off the event loop; one read shared by concurrent callers); only a worker that can't start falls back to an in-process decode after `openGameOodle`. The desktop bundles the worker (`prepare-static.ts` → `native-decode-worker.js`, checked by `verify-canary.ts`) and passes its path to the character host.
- **PREV-106 (Med):** `VertexGrid` (`decal-underlay.ts`) keys cells by one number within the sources' bounds and skips targets beyond them; the loader builds the body's shape field and grid once per load; garments are reused while the body's shape (meshes and applied shape keys) is unchanged, whatever its chunk masks. Tests: grid equivalence and bounds (`face-decal-material.test.ts`), and on a real GPU a re-masking clothing change keeps both garments (`webgl-clothing.test.ts`).
- **CORE-92 (Med):** `readSavePackage` refuses more than 65,536 names or chunks before decoding anything and decodes names lazily. Tests in `save-loadout.test.ts`.
- **PREV-107 (Low):** scaling streams (`decodePngHalved`: rows unfiltered and halved as zlib inflates on its thread pool; byte-identical to `halveImage`) and compresses on the thread pool (`encodePngAsync`, byte-identical to `encodePng`), before the record is written (`scaleTextures`). Tests: every channel layout, odd sizes and one-texel sides against whole-image halving; a record served from a scaled map.
- **PREV-108 (Low):** with **Body** off the character context's request carries `body: false` (`xfs/character-request-6`), so the host neither dresses nor resolves the body and the scene releases it; back on, it is asked for again.
- **UI-78 (Low):** area Undo labels use `CLOTHING_AREA_LABELS` ("Hide inner torso").
- **UI-79 (Low):** the Clothing snapshot offers only states that dress V differently (the current one always; **Choose areas** only with an area worn) and the capability refuses the others with the reason; `camera.body` is refused while the body is off.

## Fixed in claude/bridge-batch2

The open runtime-bridge findings from the review at `fa74f98` (RB-32..33) and the bridge autonomy review at `d144704` (RB-34..41), before the next in-game session. Each fix has a test (`xfb_selftest --unit`, the self-test host or `bun test tools`); none changes what the game is asked to do in a passing case, only what is refused or restored. The same branch adds the expression design's two bridge commands for R1/R2, `face.rig.read` (read) and `photo.expression.index` (write-photo), described in the [runtime bridge design](../runtime/runtime-bridge-design.md#32-protocol-1); they extend the bridge subsystem rather than add one.

- **RB-32 (Med):** `ResolveScriptCallAddresses` (`native/src/plugin/ScriptCall.cpp`) runs in `Load` and resolves, through RED4ext's own `RED4ext_ResolveAddress`, every engine address the call path reaches: the internal execute, and what making the `$XFBridge` caller and the static context use (`CGlobalFunction_ctor`, `CClass_CreateInstance`, `Handle_ctor`). If RED4ext's resolver is missing or any address is 0, it logs `evt=script.addresses_missing … script_calls=off` and `CallFunction` refuses every game and script call with `script_calls_unavailable` (plain wording in `tools/api/errors.ts`); the internal execute is called through the address resolved at load, never the SDK's lazy relocation. The address check is a pure function (`script::MissingAddresses`, `core/ScriptFrame.cpp`) with unit checks for all present, one missing and no resolver.
- **RB-33 (Low):** `putBackLock` (`tools/session-lock.ts`) puts a moved-aside lock back with a hard link, which fails when a lock exists, falling back to an exclusive (`wx`) copy; the aside file is removed either way. Test: a third runner's lock written in between is kept, and with nothing there the lock is restored (`mcp.test.ts`).
- **RB-34 (Med):** the cursor wrap substitutes Hide only while photo mode is active; `XFBridgeActions.Status` clears the flag whenever it sees another phase (so every `game.status` and `game.wait` poll cleans up after a photo mode that closed without `OnHide`); and a client the pipe drops for idleness (`idle_disconnect_seconds`) sets a flag the next Running tick turns into `XFBridgeActions.ReleaseCursor` (only after a write, never after the kill switch's restore). The self-test host does the same on its simulated tick; test: `--idle-seconds 1`, a hidden cursor stays hidden while the client talks and comes back after the idle drop (`mcp.test.ts`).
- **RB-35 (Med):** `CommandApi.keyTarget` honours the test-only window override only while `XFB_NO_INPUT=1` (when nothing can be sent), and the real sender checks the target process's image is `Cyberpunk2077.exe` besides the window's owner. Tests: the override is ignored without `XFB_NO_INPUT`; `isGameImage` (`framing.test.ts`).
- **RB-36 (Low):** `photo.open` sends only when `game.status` says `photo_mode_can_open: true`; a missing answer refuses with `photo_not_allowed`. Test with a fake transport answering without the field (`framing.test.ts`).
- **RB-37 (Low):** `KeySender` takes a `beforeSend` check, run after the window came to the front and just before the key (both routes); `photo.open` re-reads `game.status` there and refuses if the game left normal play or stopped allowing photo mode. Test: the phase changes while the window "comes forward" and nothing is sent.
- **RB-38 (Low):** `readPhotoModeBinding` uses the default `IK_N` only when the settings file or the `photoMode` entry doesn't exist; an entry that is unbound (`IK_None`, empty), not a key name, or a settings file that can't be parsed is refused (`key_unbound`, `key_binding_unreadable`).
- **RB-39 (Low):** navigation-cluster and arrow keys are sent with `KEYEVENTF_EXTENDEDKEY` (and the extended bit in the posted route's `lParam`); punctuation keys, whose virtual key depends on the keyboard layout, are no longer mapped, so such a binding is refused and the player presses the key.
- **RB-40 (Low):** `native/config/config.ini`, `Config.hpp`, `Params.hpp` and the `creator_leave_disabled` message now say that only the test profile's `-writes` package turns `allow_creator_leave` on.
- **RB-41 (Low):** `bunfig.toml` preloads `tools/test/preload.ts`, which sets `XFB_NO_INPUT=1` before any test file loads; `tools/test/package.test.ts` imports nothing from `helpers.ts` and checks the preload ran. Packaging's staging moved into `tools/packaging.ts` (`package.ts` keeps the provenance gate and the zips), so the same test stages all three variants with a stand-in DLL: the default package has no camera presets file and keeps `enabled`, `allow_writes` and `allow_creator_leave` off; the diagnostic package is read-only with the presets; only the writes package allows writes and leaving the creator.

## Fixed in claude/cleanup-batch2

The review at `fa74f98` of the bridge script-call fix, faster first-time choices and native reader hardening (PREV-101..105, PIPE-96, NATIVE-18..23; RB-32..33 stay open), the step 8 review at `fa74f98` (CORE-91, PIPE-88..95), and the real-GPU harness's start-up flake. Each fix has a test; the prefetch, cache and priority tests (PREV-101..105, PIPE-96) were run against the code before them and failed there. Package bytes are unchanged: nothing here touches a feature's plan, the resource builder, the pack or the verifier, and the ordinary-package golden (finish-board maps included) still matches. What changes in packaging is reporting (omissions decided once and each manifest's own share, PIPE-88), the identities of a saved copy's split-off mods (PIPE-89) and refusals ([step 8 cleanup notes](feature-module-platform.md#cleanup-after-step-8)).

**Choices prepared ahead** ([knowledge §6](../../knowledge/mod-loading.md#6-implementation-and-reproduction)):

- **PREV-101 (Med):** `ChoicePrefetcher.run` catches its own failures: an unexpected one (a dependency throwing) stops the job (`stopped: "failed"`, which the page shows without a reason), leaves its remaining choices "not prepared" and is reported once through `failed`; `start` adds a last-resort catch. Readiness is wrapped per choice (an unreadable check queues the choices, a throwing manifest check queues only its choice, a failed catalogue lookup is "not prepared"). `readChoiceManifest` validates every entry (`parseChoiceManifest`: decimal 64-bit hashes, export kinds, strings), so a damaged file means "not prepared" instead of a later throw in `refFromHash`. Both entries (`server.ts`, `desktop/main.ts`; not `createDesktopServer`, which tests import) call `logUnhandledRejections`, which records any other unhandled rejection as a `host/unhandled_rejection` failure with a plain message instead of exiting. Tests: `choice-prefetch.test.ts` (no unhandled rejection with a throwing dependency; per-choice readiness; eight damaged manifests).
- **PREV-102 (Med):** the preparation cache's maps are `RunMap`s and each preparation and prefetch batch runs as a `CacheRun` (`AsyncLocalStorage`): `forget(run)` removes only entries that run added and that still hold its value, never one another run added meanwhile. `warmCharacters` checks cancellation after opening (before `cache.reset`) and after `gatherParts` (before forgetting or writing manifests). The prefetcher has `idle` and `preparing`, and nothing awaits between its foreground check and a batch's start. The host's `warm` answers a stopped batch at once (`untilStopped`) and tracks the batch's own settlement (`warming`); a person's `request` starts once the prefetcher has let go (`idle`), and Clear waits for the running preparation, `idle` and every settling batch before removing files. Tests: `character-detail-service.test.ts` (attribution with an interleaved run; a stopped batch lets the change start at once and Clear waits for the batch), `choice-prefetch.test.ts` (`idle` waits for a stopped batch).
- **PREV-103 (Low):** `RunMap` records which run added each entry and which runs another run was served from; a run's manifest reads are its own reads and creator files plus `CacheRun.inherited()` (every run it was served from, transitively). Test: V B prepared after V A on a shared cache (sharing A's lashes) has a manifest holding every read of V B prepared on a fresh cache; before, the lash `.app` was missing (`character-detail-service.test.ts`).
- **PREV-104 (Low):** `ChoicePrefetcher.resetBudget`, called by `clearPreparedFiles` once the files are removed, forgets `startBytes` and `spent`. Tests: the prefetcher, and the host (a row stopped for disk, Clear, a new row not stopped).
- **PREV-105 (Low):** `PrefetchLimits.unpolledMs` (default 90 s: the page asks every 0.8–4 s and a hidden tab may get one timer a minute). Every `update` stamps the job; a watchdog (an unref'd interval while a job runs) and the loop stop a job nobody asked about, aborting its batch, and the next question starts afresh. Test: `choice-prefetch.test.ts`.
- **PIPE-96 (Low):** a resolver launch decides its priority as it starts (`WolvenKitFetcher.low`: below normal only while no foreground work runs on the lane). `ProcessTreeOptions.lowPriority` accepts a function asked at spawn (`LowPriority`, `lowPriorityNow`), which prefetch exports get from `backgroundPriority(cacheDir)`, and by-hash exports take the batch's priority. Tests: a queued background batch that a foreground change then waits on starts at normal priority; a by-hash export runs at the batch's priority (`choice-prefetch.test.ts`).

**Native reader** ([knowledge §8](../../knowledge/archive-format.md#8-reading-untrusted-files-safely), [budgets](../backlog/native-archive-reader.md#budgets); still not wired into the app):

- **NATIVE-18 (Med):** the CR2W string pool is capped (`maxStringPoolBytes` 16 MiB; the largest measured is 2.7 MiB, in a `JsonResource`), its terminators are counted first and charged to the decoded-bytes budget (4 bytes each) before a `Uint32Array` of exactly that count is allocated, and decoded names are counted (`maxNames` 2 M; 314 K measured in one mesh). Test: `native-hardening.test.ts`.
- **NATIVE-19 (Med):** `WorkerDecoder` hears only its current worker (every message, error and exit is checked against it), and each decode message carries a sequence id the worker echoes (`WorkerDecodeMessage`, `WorkerReply`), so a late or stale answer is dropped. The worker is injectable (`createWorker`). Test: `native-hardening.test.ts`.
- **NATIVE-20 (Low):** a worker must report ready within `startTimeoutMs` (15 s); after a failed start, resources are answered with the new failure kind `unavailable` (so they fall back to WolvenKit) for `restartDelayMs` (60 s) before one retry, and after 3 failed starts in a row for the session. Test: `native-hardening.test.ts`.
- **NATIVE-21 (Low):** the bytes hashed are read through the held handle (`holdFile(...).read`) and the library is loaded by the handle's final path, so a junction repointed during the check swaps nothing; the folders above the held file can't be renamed either. The residual window (a drive letter redefined for the process's user) is documented. Test: `native-oodle.test.ts` (a repointed junction).
- **NATIVE-22 (Low):** `authenticodeSignature` is asynchronous (`execFile`); hosts open the reader with `openGameOodle`, `openNativeReaderAsync` and `openNativeDecoderAsync`, and the synchronous twins stay for the three command-line tools. Test: `native-oodle.test.ts` (both forms agree).
- **NATIVE-23 (Low):** `maxIndexBytes` (128 MiB; 28.6 MiB measured, the largest game 2.31 archive) is checked before an index is read, and a pool keeps at most `maxPooledIndexBytes` (512 MiB; all 1,157 indexes of the reference setup are 96.2 MiB) of index blocks, least recently used dropped first. Test: `native-hardening.test.ts`. Documents of the cached resources and the native oracles are unchanged.

**Export host** ([step 8 cleanup notes](feature-module-platform.md#cleanup-after-step-8), [pipeline guide](studio-to-mod-pipeline.md)):

- **CORE-91 (Med):** `readPackagePlan` classifies a stored plan without throwing: a later `xfs/package-plan-N` schema, or a `-1` plan with a field this build doesn't know (such as the planned `selectorLabels`), is `newer`; anything else unreadable is `damaged` (the rule is deliberately "unknown field means newer", so such a plan is kept whole, never stripped). The codec keeps it opaque (`KeptPackagePlan`), the library form and workspace drafts write it back unchanged, and the collection opens and saves. `packagePlanIssue` carries the plain wording ("made with a newer version" or damaged) to the Mod package panel; every `package.*` action and the package request refuse with `unavailable`, and the export host with `package_plan_newer` or `package_plan_damaged` (422). Tests: `product-plan.test.ts`, `package-plan-actions.test.ts`, `product-export.test.ts`.
- **PIPE-88 (Med):** `checkProducts` decides whole-look omissions once, across every feature: a look is left out whole only when no feature packages anything of it (with the reporting feature's reason, else `NOTHING_PACKAGED_REASON`), and eye makeup no longer reports a look without eye makeup. Each feature's list keeps its layers and its part of a look another feature packages, labelled with the feature when several export (`resultOmissions`). `ProductCheck.omissions` is each product's own share (one no built product owns goes to the first), the manifest's top-level `omissions` is that share instead of the collection's copy, and the result gate compares it. Tests: `product-export.test.ts`, `product-builder.test.ts`, `package-omitted-looks.test.ts`, `package-server.test.ts`, desktop `package-check.test.ts`.
- **PIPE-89 (Med):** `copyPackagePlan` replaces `renameCollectionId`: on Save as copy the default mod takes the new collection ID and every split-off mod a fresh UUID, names and moves kept, so no archive of the copy shares a name with one of the original's. Tests: `product-plan.test.ts`, `package-plan-actions.test.ts`.
- **PIPE-90 (Low):** the candidate's mod name flows through the install transport (checked as a folder name), the diagnostic stage (its plan records `modName`) and promotion; the transport refuses an MO2 folder of that name it didn't create; staging and promotion refuse a build any installed mod already holds part of, found from the resources each installed `.archive.xl` declares rather than one stage's receipts. Tests: `mod-install-transport.test.ts`, `runtime-diagnostic-promotion.test.ts`.
- **PIPE-91 (Low):** the planner's clash suffix is validated as a mod name (a number when "(collection name)" isn't a valid folder name), and `package.rename` to another mod's name of the collection is refused (`invalid_value`) instead of silently suffixed. Tests: `product-plan.test.ts`.
- **PIPE-92 (Low):** the builder takes back promoted products when one can't be moved into place, and the host checks every destination in the candidate store before moving any and removes the moved ones on a failure. Test: `product-host.test.ts`.
- **PIPE-93 (Low):** `PackagePlanConflict` names the feature by its label, and `package_conflict` messages name features by label, with the namespace, depot path or ArchiveXL detail in `ExportRefusal.detail` (Check worker, builder log line and host log only). Tests: `product-plan.test.ts`, `product-export.test.ts`.
- **PIPE-94 (Low):** with unreadable Local setup (settings and backup) localhost Check plans with the defaults, as desktop does, and Build answers a JSON refusal (`package_setup_unreadable`, 503) in plain words. Test: `package-server.test.ts`.
- **PIPE-95 (Low):** the real eye-makeup verifier's merged branch is tested beside a second feature's members and declaration (`mod-verifier.test.ts`); export fixtures hold single-feature looks; the result gate checks each feature's `planSha256` against the host's plan (`product-export.test.ts`); and `tools/compare-package-candidates.ts` is the re-runnable byte-identity gate (members after WolvenKit unbundling, `.archive.xl` bytes, manifests, and intermediate trees with `--tree`; `compare-package-candidates.test.ts`).

**Real-GPU harness start-up (no ID):** `tools/cdp.ts` `launch` starts Chrome with `--remote-debugging-port=0` and reads its `DevToolsActivePort` (the harness no longer derives ports that could meet a Chrome still closing), waits for the page target with a bounded backoff (`waitForPageTarget`: 100 ms growing to 1 s, 30 s per start, stopping early when Chrome exits), tries a start three times with a fresh profile, then fails naming every attempt; `close` waits for Chrome to exit and removes its profile. Tests: `tests/cdp-launch.test.ts` (new, not a before/after case); the four `webgl-*` suites pass.

## Fixed in claude/cleanup-vortex-diag

The Vortex and diagnostics review's VORTEX-01..08 and DIAG-19..24 (review at `8857137`), the export repair and installer review's PIPE-84..87 and REL-04..06 (review at `972ee62`; PIPE-85 was fixed in claude/choice-prefetch), and the step 7 cleanup review's CORE-90 and PREV-99..100 (review at `8857137`). Each fix has a test that fails on the code before it. Package output is unchanged: nothing here touches the recipe, compiler, resource builder or verifier, and every golden (the ordinary-package golden with the finish board's baked maps included) is unchanged; the installer (REL-04) is the one output meant to change.

- **VORTEX-01 (Med):** `stateFromPairs` builds its tree from null-prototype objects, skips (and reports) any key segment `__proto__`, `prototype` or `constructor`, merges a JSON leaf into a tree without its unsafe keys, and every read (`at`, the profile and mod walks, JSON backups included) takes own properties only. Test: hostile keys, a leaf carrying `__proto__`, an inherited name as the active profile and a backup's `__proto__` mod leave `Object.prototype` untouched (`vortex-setup.test.ts`).
- **VORTEX-02 (Med):** `leveldb-read.ts` checks a Snappy block's claimed length against 22 times its compressed bytes and a 64 MB limit before allocating, refuses a literal past the claimed length, decodes a block the index lists again only once, and counts every allocation (blocks, expanded keys) against one `DecodeBudget` per read (16 times the input, 64 MB to 1 GB); a file past it is a gap and the rest still reads. Tests: a megabyte claimed by five bytes, 2,000 handles to one block, and quadratically growing prefix keys (`leveldb-read.test.ts`).
- **VORTEX-03 (Med):** a game-folder file whose time no longer matches the manifest's keeps "Installed game" as its provider in source discovery (its `deployedBy` says `changed`), and the report's mod identity credits an archive to a Vortex mod only while the game-folder file's time matches the manifest's (`attributeVortexFile`), whether the provider arrives as the game folder or as the staging name. Tests: `vortex-setup.test.ts`, `diagnostics-report.test.ts`.
- **VORTEX-04 (Low):** Vortex entries have their own key space in the report; on the Mod Organizer 2 route, an MO2 mod whose folder holds the archive stays an MO2 mod (its `meta.ini` source and version) even when a leftover manifest names a Vortex mod of the same name. Test: `diagnostics-report.test.ts`.
- **VORTEX-05 (Low):** `inspectVortexSetup` and `readVortexStateFolder` are asynchronous: state files are read without blocking, at most 256 MB each and 512 MB together, never past the caller's deadline (the report's one time budget now starts before the Vortex read, which counts against it), with gaps naming why (running, too large, out of time); a complete read is kept for the same files, a partial one is not. Tests: `vortex-setup.test.ts`.
- **VORTEX-06 (Low):** the staging folder's marker is read only from a folder on a local drive (`isLocalFolder`): a UNC, `//`, `\\?\` or `\\.\` path from the manifest (or Vortex's settings) is reported, never opened. Test: `vortex-setup.test.ts`.
- **VORTEX-07 (Low):** `tools/vortex-check.ts` prints `profileFound`, not the profile's name. Test: the tool's output for a profile with a personal name (`vortex-setup.test.ts`).
- **VORTEX-08 (Low):** when the database read has gaps, the newest backup is used if the database lists no mods or the backup is newer than every database file read. Test: `vortex-setup.test.ts`.
- **DIAG-19 (Low):** the host builds and redacts the issue link on every host: without `openExternal` (the dev server) it answers `{ code: "open_in_page", url }`, and the page opens only a link the host built (on the project's issue page); the page never builds one. Tests: `diagnostics-report.test.ts`, `diagnostics-device.test.ts`.
- **DIAG-20 (Low):** a diagnostics-only pass (`redactProfileFolders`) removes a user-profile folder's whole name up to the next separator, quote or line end (any number of words, parentheses, trailing, percent-encoded, `/mnt/c/Users`, `/home`, `/Users`), keeping placeholders and shared profiles; known folders match their percent-encoded, `+`-for-space and `\u005c` forms. The shared detectors (`tools/private-data.json`) are unchanged. Tests: `diagnostics-report.test.ts`, with the shared vectors still passing in `diagnostics.test.ts`.
- **DIAG-21 (Low):** `redactValue` redacts identifier keys without the account-name rule (`Redactor.forKeys`), numbers a key that redacts to an existing one instead of overwriting it, and defines keys so `__proto__` stays data; the MO2 profile is hidden only in quotes or as a path segment (`hideProfileName`). Tests: `diagnostics-report.test.ts`.
- **DIAG-22 (Low):** the host's one-a-minute repeat key includes the entry's reference. Test: two references for the same failure are both logged and a report finds the second (`diagnostics-report.test.ts`).
- **DIAG-23 (Low):** mod-file ZIP entry names are redacted with the report's roots (`modFileEntryName`). Test: `diagnostics-report.test.ts`.
- **DIAG-24 (Low):** hashing streams with a stop check per chunk: a hash still running when the budget ends stops, is not recorded, and is finished in the background. Test: an 8 MB archive stopped part-way, then hashed on the next report (`diagnostics-report.test.ts`).
- **PIPE-84 (Med):** drop notes go ahead of informational ones under the record's 32-note cap (`recordNotes`), into the `character/prepared` trace event (`dropped`), and a shown slot drawn in part carries the host limit `part-unread` on the record (`HOST_SLOT_LIMITS` in `render-detail.ts`, so engines reach nothing new), which the page's device keeps beside the scene's limits and the preview panel words. Tests: `character-detail-service.test.ts` (record, parse, trace, device), `detail-limits.test.ts`.
- **PIPE-85 (Low, fixed in claude/choice-prefetch):** a repaired GLB is recorded under the repair route's identity (`MESH_EXPORT_REPAIR_VERSION`) and reused only under it.
- **PIPE-86 (Low):** `GeometryRepair` returns a typed outcome (`repaired`, `not-applicable`, or `failed` with its step: `serialize`, `deserialize`, `pack`, `uncook`, `tool`); the exporter reports each (`onRepair`, by default a `wolvenkit/repair` trace event and a warning for a failure) and rethrows anything that isn't a tool failure (a full disk, a parse bug) instead of blaming WolvenKit. Tests: `dynamic-hair.test.ts`, `choice-prefetch.test.ts`.
- **PIPE-87 (Low):** `tests/mesh-repair-adapter.test.ts` drives `createWolvenKitMeshRepair` over a fake WolvenKit runner (now injectable): the four launches' exact arguments, the work-folder layout, the packed resource at its depot path, `pack.archive`, each missing output as a failed step, invalid serialized JSON, a mesh the repair doesn't fit, the non-invertible matrix wording, and tool, cancel, missing-tool and disk errors.
- **REL-04 (Med):** the installer script sets `InternalCompressLevel=none`; `singleInstallerWrapper` refuses a setup whose script strings (`WRAPPER_PLAIN_TEXT`) don't read as text, the budget is the measured 5,241,942-byte wrapper plus about 58 KB (`WRAPPER_BUDGET` 5,300,000, from 3,000,000 over 2.05 MB), and the log and README say what the scan covers (strings, not program code). A local canary build (Hutch 0.26.0, Inno Setup 6.7.3) passed `verify-canary.ts`; before the change the same strings were unreadable. The download grows by about 3.2 MB. Tests: `desktop/tests/single-installer.test.ts`.
- **REL-05 (Low):** custom exit codes are 100 (Electrobun's setup couldn't start) and 101 (it reported a failure), documented in the script and the desktop README. Test: the script's codes and their documentation.
- **REL-06 (Low):** the marker error names the 6.7.0 data format; `quadVersion` gives each pre-release its own Windows file version (`alpha.N` 1000+N, `beta.N` 2000+N, `rc.N` 3000+N, release 65535; others refused), and the local build carried 0.1.0.1001; `SetupMutex=dev.axefrog.xf-studio.setup`. Tests: `single-installer.test.ts`.
- **CORE-90 (Low):** the import scan resolves `.js`, `.mjs` and other script extensions of relative specifiers, and records modules loaded by URL (`new URL("./x", import.meta.url)`, which `new Worker` and `new SharedWorker` take, and `new Worker("./x")`; a served root path is left alone) as value uses. Must-fail probes: a feature core importing a device by `.js`, loading a worker by URL, and reaching a browser device through a `.js` helper's worker; a view reaching platform internals by `.js`. `native-decode` now shows its worker as a dependency. Tests: `architecture-import-boundary.test.ts`.
- **PREV-99 (Low):** a rig attachment re-syncs its bones before each drawn frame: bones added under it later join the idle and blink (posed at once, world matrices current), removed ones leave, and disposal detaches the current set. Test: `scene-feature-renderers.test.ts`.
- **PREV-100 (Low):** `makeup-stack`'s `dispose` takes the anchor's index, attributes and morph targets off its geometry and then disposes it, so the renderer frees only the stack's own (the underlay attributes, vertex-array states, wireframe index, morph texture). Test: `makeup-stack.test.ts` checks what the geometry holds when its dispose fires, and that the anchor keeps every buffer.
- **Flaky skin oracle:** the resolved brow, lash and hair GLB check (`tests/skin.test.ts`) samples at most four distinct GLBs per slot and morph kind (the local cache held 56 files, 279 MB) and has an explicit 30 s timeout, justified in the test, instead of the default 5 s.
- **Observed, not changed:** `character-detail-service.test.ts`'s by-hash timing check (PREV-55, "six 20 ms launches … less than 120 ms") failed once while a type check ran beside it and passed on five reruns; it is timing-sensitive under load.

## Fixed in claude/native-hardening

The native reader review at `5e64894` ([backlog page](../backlog/native-archive-reader.md), [knowledge §8](../../knowledge/archive-format.md#8-reading-untrusted-files-safely)). All five Highs are closed, so this track no longer counts against the budget. The reader is still not wired into the app. Tests: `tests/native-hardening.test.ts` (one test per reproduced case), `native-fuzz.test.ts`, `native-oodle.test.ts`, `native-boundary.test.ts` and `native-reader-fuzz-oracle.test.ts` (new), and `native-fetch-port.test.ts` (rewritten). Documents of all 1,722 distinct cached resources are byte-identical to the unhardened reader's, and the differential harness and the WolvenKit oracle are unchanged.

- **NATIVE-01 (High):** string lengths are built arithmetically, take at most four continuation bytes and must fit in the bytes left. `Cursor.take` refuses negative or non-integer sizes, a cursor window must lie inside its bytes, and every array or element count must be at most the bytes left. The reviewer's 2-million-element `array:String` is refused in milliseconds.
- **NATIVE-02 (High):** the name table finds terminators through one pass over the string pool, decodes each offset once and caps one entry at 1 KiB. Decoded name bytes count against the resource's budget.
- **NATIVE-03 (High) and NATIVE-07 (Med):** package values must be contiguous and in field-table order. The first starts right after the table, each ends exactly where the next begins, and chunks follow in order, so each byte decodes once. Values, JSON values and nesting are budgeted. Only version 4 decodes. A type name the RTTI doesn't know as a class is refused, not read as a struct: the reader now holds FNV-1a hashes of all 15,715 class names in the RTTI dump (`rtti-class-hashes.json`, `tools/native-rtti-subset.ts --hashes-only`), so a class outside the slice still reads.
- **NATIVE-04 (High):** the embedded-file table must lie inside the file, name distinct exports other than the root and valid imports, and hold no more records than exports.
- **NATIVE-05 (High):** per-resource caps, from `tools/native-limits.ts` measurements times a margin ([budgets](../backlog/native-archive-reader.md#budgets)), cover the resource read, the body, each buffer, decoded bytes, values (8 M), JSON values (16 M, and at most 8 per decoded value beyond 2^20), nesting and the name list, which keeps one decode near 1 GB at worst (the few largest world meshes fall back; the resolver reads none). Each is checked before allocation. Segments are checked against the real file size, and `customDataLength` is capped and must fit the file. Decoding can run in a worker (`WorkerDecoder`, 10 s per resource) that is terminated when over time, and the resource then falls back to WolvenKit. Memory is bounded by the caps, since Bun ignores worker heap limits.
- **NATIVE-06 (Med):** package references are hashes in `appearanceAppearanceDefinition.compiledData` and path text elsewhere, decided by the owner. An `.app` reference of a size other than 8 is refused.
- **NATIVE-08 (Med):** a property stored with a type the RTTI disagrees with (the `castShadows` Bool) is decoded by its stored type and noted. `readResource` and the port's `NativeFetchedResource.notes` carry the note.
- **NATIVE-09 (Med):** typed failures (`native-errors.ts`: malformed, unsupported, over-budget, decompress; io from system error codes; everything else internal). The port counts fallbacks by kind, not-indexed and not-verified included, keeps up to 32 messages and up to 8 internal failures with stacks, and has `onFallback` and a `strict` mode that rethrows reader bugs.
- **NATIVE-10 (Med):** the pool holds parsed indexes, not file handles. A read opens, checks the file's size, time and id with `fstat`, reads and closes. A replaced archive is re-indexed and read again. The test replaces an archive by rename, on Windows, while the pool holds its index.
- **NATIVE-11 (Med):** the Oodle library loads only when its SHA-256 is on the known list (game 2.31) or its Authenticode signature is valid and names CD PROJEKT S.A. The check uses Windows PowerShell with a hidden window, ties the verdict to the same bytes by hash, and holds the file without write or delete sharing from check to load. The identity comes from those bytes.
- **NATIVE-12 (Med):** malformed-input regressions for NATIVE-01..05, 10 and 13, and a deterministic mutation fuzz in the normal suite: 1.5 s, about 100,000 cases over synthetic resources and archives. No case may fail internally or take 500 ms. An opt-in real-data fuzz runs under tight caps, a case limit and a 1.5 GB resident-memory stop. `XFS_NATIVE_FUZZ_MS`/`_SEED` allow longer runs (3.3 million synthetic cases clean).
- **NATIVE-13 (Low):** nesting is capped at 128 levels (the deepest real document has 11) in decoding, deriving and writing. Writing needs its own cap, since key order can walk a chain that file order decodes shallowly.
- **NATIVE-14 (Low):** the compiled-decoder cache and the learned-keys memo are bounded at 4,096 entries. The RTTI memos and the new base-class memo keep only classes in the slice.
- **NATIVE-15 (Low):** the diff harness excuses a hash-only path only when the reference's path hashes to it (all 41,904 in the cache do). Otherwise it reports `ResourcePath: different hash`.
- **NATIVE-16 (Low):** watched properties written as defaults are reported with their JSON paths (`defaulted`; watched: `rendChunk.renderMask`). No cached resource omits `renderMask`.
- **NATIVE-17 (Low):** `tests/native-boundary.test.ts` requires the pure modules to import no host module and read no host global. Only `oodle` may start a process or load native code, and only `native-decode` may start a worker. Nothing outside `src/native` imports it (the resolver host will be allow-listed at integration).

## Fixed in claude/cleanup-diagnostics

The diagnostics review at `972ee62` ([docs/diagnostics.md](../../docs/diagnostics.md)). DIAG-05 and DIAG-11 came in a second delivery, on top of claude/rnd-vortex's Vortex attribution. Tests: `tests/diagnostics-report.test.ts` and `tests/diagnostics-device.test.ts` (new), `tests/diagnostics.test.ts`, the diagnostics rule in `tests/architecture-import-boundary.test.ts`, and the desktop's `tests/startup.test.ts`.

- **DIAG-01 (High):** `README.md` and `report.json` are built from the ticked items only, by `reportSummary`/`reportIndex` in `report.ts`, which the review also uses to preview both ("Always in the report"). Versions, the window's facts, the problem's entries and the log lines appear only when their parts are ticked; `leftOut` is a count and the fixed item IDs, never a label. A test saves a report with one part ticked and finds no problem text, stack, log line, version, mod name or profile name in the ZIP.
- **DIAG-02 (High):** the shared `userPaths` rules match a name of up to four words when a separator or quote follows, and a new `onedrive` rule matches an OneDrive-for-work folder's organisation; space-bearing vectors are added (all three scanners pass). The host also names the person's profile, OneDrive folders and account name literally (`host-roots.ts`) in the log, the window, reports and the desktop's "Copy diagnostics".
- **DIAG-03 (High):** the log and the window redact the value tree string by string, never serialised JSON; `DiagnosticLog.write`, `failure` and `hostFailure` never throw; the forwarding endpoint skips an entry it can't record and still answers 204; the page drops a batch the host refuses instead of resending it.
- **DIAG-04 (High):** `character/resolved` writes each distinct resource decision once in a `resources` table (a synthetic V of the reference's inline size keeps under 100 rows), has its own 2 MB bound and keeps the table when still too large; the report falls back to `character/prepared`'s parts. Tested with a 1.5 MB-plus resolution.
- **DIAG-05 (High):** "Installed game" is split in `mod-identity.ts`: `content`/`ep1` archives are one base-game entry, never located or hashed; an archive in Vortex's deployment manifest joins its Vortex mod; every other game-folder archive is its own entry. Game and manual folders are searched only where the game loads archives (`archive/pc/mod` two levels deep, REDmod's `mods/`), never walked whole. Tested with base-game, loose, Vortex-deployed, REDmod and out-of-place archives.
- **DIAG-06 (Med):** the endpoint names the configured folders (MO2 instance folders wherever `ModOrganizer.ini` puts them included) to the log and window as they write, and redacts page items (labels and details too), page facts, the description and the issue title and body with them. The test asserts `game-jdoe` absent from the ZIP, the issue link, `log.jsonl` and `trace/`.
- **DIAG-07 (Med):** the page records a repeat once per ten seconds whatever its reference (showing the recorded reference), bounds its waiting queue at 100 with one "dropped" line, the host keeps the same page entry once a minute, and "This problem" searches the whole log plus the last 50 host failures kept apart from it.
- **DIAG-08 (Med):** "Frameworks and full mod list" is unticked by default; the MO2 profile name becomes `<profile>` in the framework check's wording.
- **DIAG-09 (Med):** mod files are offered only for an MO2 mod or the manual folder with no source, never game-folder (or Vortex-deployed) files; the wording is "XF Studio couldn't tell where this mod came from"; the host refuses mod files without `sharingConfirmed`.
- **DIAG-10 (Med):** `tests/architecture-import-boundary.test.ts` holds exception 13 (pure half loads no `node:` module, host half or device and reads no global; nothing in `src/` imports `host-endpoint`; only `studio-startup` imports `browser-device`), with probes that show it fails; exception 13 names `browser-device.ts` and `host-roots.ts`.
- **DIAG-11 (Med):** hashing runs smallest first within a 10 s budget (`HASH_BUDGET_MS`), the rest identified by size and date (`identifiedBy`) and hashed afterwards in the background, one at a time, so the next report has them; progress reaches the review through `preparing` in the state endpoint. Locating is bounded (DIAG-05). No explicit cancel: the budget bounds a request.
- **DIAG-12 (Low):** references are six characters (about a billion values; four-character ones still read); a page failure links to at most three host failures of the last 15 s, none when it carries a host reference; a notice claims a host reference only within 3 s.
- **DIAG-13 (Low):** "Show all of it" loads a long part's whole text (`POST /api/diagnostics/item`, `fullText`).
- **DIAG-14 (Low):** an expired report turns the review to **Prepare again**; "couldn't reach its diagnostics" and "Full JSON" are reworded.
- **DIAG-15 (Low):** documented rather than changed: a process keeps one window per data folder, so `ResourceGraph.trace` is always the host's own window; exception 13 and the field's comment now say so.
- **DIAG-16 (Low):** `consoleEcho` shows control characters as `?`.
- **DIAG-17 (Low):** the view settings a report names are chosen in `createStudioPresentation` (`DiagnosticsActions.setViewState`); the startup file passes only the workspace flag.
- **DIAG-18 (Low):** resource tables and the full resource item say they come partly from mods' own files and to include them only when they help.

## Fixed in claude/cleanup-step7

The step 7 review's findings PREV-89..98, CORE-86..89 and UI-76..77 (review at `023cbf7`) and the open Low CORE-82, before module #2. The review's injected leaks are boundary-test probes that must fail. Appearance is unchanged: in makeup screenshot parity (`tools/scene-parity.ts`, 20 frames, own port, `?verify=1`) every frame of five branch captures (one after the scene-port commit, four on the final rendering code) is byte-identical to one of five captures of the base (`972ee62`). The base's own run-to-run noise is a second state of the bare head's first 1K creator frame (4 pixels, one step), which one base capture and two branch captures show; the other captures match in all 20 frames. No console errors. Every golden is unchanged, and the rebuilt style guide differs only in its bundled script (the binding catalogue lost its type imports).

- **PREV-89:** a feature says what it replaces through the port, `supersede(parts)`, whenever that changes (a brow feature only while it has brows). A part is a whole slot or only the components of named creator options (`{ slot: "face", options: [...] }`, the game's option names), so lips can replace the lips decal and leave the other face decals. A superseded part draws as if the V had none there: hidden and not baked; a superseded skin leaves the core head with its default skin in both placements (never a headless V), and superseded eyes bring back the core eye. The registry forgets a renderer's list on disposal and tells the host at once. Tests: `tests/character-renderer.test.ts` (per option, whole slot, changes, skin in both placements, eyes) and `tests/scene-feature-renderers.test.ts`.
- **PREV-90:** `attach(object, { rig: true })` joins the bones under the object to the rig motion by name (blink first, then idle, as the V's details), and the detach or disposal takes them out again.
- **PREV-91:** a renderer factory asks for `renderSlots` and its port carries a `renderBand`; the registry gives each the next free run of the feature-plate range (10 to 98) in composition order and refuses a list that overflows it. `makeup-stack` takes its slots' draw order from its placement; eye makeup asks for 32 (`MAX_LAYERS`) and keeps 10 to 41, so the next layered feature starts at 42 (the GPU probe draws both).
- **PREV-92:** a renderer that throws during creation releases what it made through its port (attachments, rig bones, frame, skin, character and lighting subscriptions, its supersede list); the port tracks those subscriptions, so disposal ends any the renderer forgot.
- **PREV-93:** meshes added later under a morph-following attachment take the V's current shape on the next drawn frame (before the renderers prepare) and follow it; removed ones stop.
- **PREV-94:** each renderer's `beforeDraw`, display toggles, restore listeners and evidence run guarded: a throw is reported once per failing streak (`onRendererError`, default the console) and the host and the other features draw on.
- **PREV-95:** the character renderer applies the drawn skin once per change, after the new V is in place: listeners hear once per V switch (none when a kept skin stays), with the new identity.
- **PREV-96:** `CharacterView.drawn` lists the slots with a part shown now: a skin drawn on the core head included, hidden and superseded slots left out; its listeners hear once per change, visibility changes included.
- **PREV-97:** the head rig makes every record surface an anchor it never draws (hidden, its own double-sided material, skinned with all influences for picking). `makeup-stack` only reads its anchor: its layers and lit plate draw a geometry of their own that shares the anchor's buffers (no second upload) and carries the skin underlay; disposal frees nothing the anchor uses. Tests: the plate's material (not disposed), visibility and attributes are unchanged after eye makeup's renderer ran and was disposed.
- **PREV-98:** behavioural character-renderer tests replace the skin and eye source-text checks; the GPU probe (`tests/webgl-scene-host.test.ts`) adds a resolved skin placed on the core head that lights eye makeup's plate, a renderer whose methods throw, a lost and restored WebGL context (the composite redraws), and the draw-order bands; the rendering-boundary list (`tests/render-detail-record.test.ts`) finds every module under `platform/scene/` and each engine's and feature's `render/` on disk; `scene-parity compare <after> <base> [<base> …]` passes a frame that matches any base capture.
- **CORE-86:** the scene host rule and the feature renderer rule are transitive, types included. Everything the host reaches is a listed device module or a listed support module (materials, lights, grading, the binding catalogue and pure record readers under them), never a feature, engine, `compose/`, studio-ui or an application service; a renderer reaches, beyond its feature's core, only `platform/api`, engines, Three and the scene's materials. The one leak (the binding catalogue named `StudioAction`, `CollectionRequest` and the UV command types) is cut: `input-bindings.ts` imports nothing and its action and request IDs are strings checked against the registry by `tests/input-bindings.test.ts`. Probes: a device module or scene material reaching an application service, an engine or the authoring core by type, and an engine renderer reaching the host.
- **CORE-87:** the import scan (`tests/fixtures/import-scan.ts`) reads template-literal specifiers in `import()`, `require()` and `import.meta.require()`, and records any computed call (a variable, an expression, an interpolated template or a comment as the argument) as `<computed>`, which every allowlist refuses; no source module has one (a local helper named `require` in `eye-plate-wolvenkit.ts` is now `requireFile`). Probes: a feature core loading a device by template literal and anything by a computed import.
- **CORE-88:** `platform/api` may name Three by type only: a value import, and the api index re-exporting the scene port, are violations (probes).
- **CORE-89:** the core record's surfaces come from every geometry node but the head and the eyes (`LoadedCoreDetail.surfaces`); the head rig no longer names the plate.
- **UI-76:** the composition lists the renderers that draw a layered surface by feature (`STUDIO_LAYERED_SURFACES` in `compose/renderers.ts`); the startup wires the live feature's to the preview device and the on-head editor and names no feature. `attachBrowserHead` takes a list of layered surfaces (each with its preview device, one with the editor), connects and presents every one and releases them together. The preview devices read their layers through a narrow source (`PreviewLayerSource`: the stack, the active layer and the change intents). What still limits a second layered feature is the core, not the wiring: the authoring core edits one live feature, so only its surface has a layer source (recorded in the [design doc](feature-module-platform.md#cleanup-after-step-7)).
- **UI-77:** `scene.feature(factory)` returns the renderer that factory made, typed by it (a different factory for the same feature finds nothing); eye makeup's unused `pick` and `diagnostics` and the port's unimplemented `FeatureRenderer.pick` are gone (picking joins the port with its dispatch).
- **CORE-82:** the UV editor, the on-head editor and the UV view's fitting and selection checks draw, pick and drag a symmetric layer's mirrored instance across the region's mirror (`reflectUV(p, mirrored, mirror)`, the engine's `mirrored`), and the diagnostic Glitter route mirrors a catalogue across a u or a v line. At u = ½ every value is the same as before. Tests: `tests/editor-mirror.test.ts` (mirrors at u = 0.4 and v = 0.3).
- **Slow scans:** the whole-repo scan tests share one cached, pruned scan (`tests/fixtures/source-files.ts`): the branding scan no longer walks desktop build output, dependencies or junctions, and the presentation and architecture boundaries read each source once per process. The resolved brow, lash and hair check reads each distinct GLB once and asserts once per mesh (6.3 million `expect` calls to 501; 1.8 s to 0.3 s).

## Fixed in claude/platform-step8

Platform step 8 ([step 8 status](feature-module-platform.md#step-8-status)): the exporter and verifier contract, the product planner and package plan, the export host with `runProductBuild` for both hosts, `xfs/local-package-2`, eye makeup's exporter and verifier. A default Build of the finish board is byte-identical to `main`'s (21 unbundled members, `.archive.xl`, `plan.json`, `compiled.json`, rasters and DDS inputs).

- **PIPE-12:** Check and Build plan products (one merged mod by default, split by the collection's package plan; archive `xfs_c<collection>` for the default product, `xfs_m<product>` otherwise). Every exporting feature builds into its product's one staging tree; the host packs once, the product verifier requires the unpacked set to equal the union of the features' files and the `.xl` to equal the merged fragments, and each feature's verifier checks its subset. A two-product synthetic test with a stub exporter, and namespace-duplication refusals in the plan, at Check and at install (`tests/product-export.test.ts`, `tests/mod-install-transport.test.ts`).
- **PIPE-09:** one typed manifest (`xfs/local-package-2`, `platform/export/manifest.ts`) and one reader for versions 1 and 2 (`readPackageManifest`), used by the hosts' result gate and the install transport.
- **PIPE-03:** `PackageHostService`/`runProductBuild` (`platform/export/product-host.ts`) is the one host service: request normalisation, Check in a fresh worker with one deadline (15 s; localhost had 2 minutes through the CLI), Build prerequisites, one bounded builder run (40 min), cancellation, one error-code table with HTTP statuses, the stale-prerequisite retry, and the result gate against the host's own plan. `package-server.ts` and `desktop/package.ts` are adapters only.

## Fixed in claude/platform-step7

Platform step 7 ([step 7 status](feature-module-platform.md#step-7-status)): the scene port and eye makeup's renderer, then `scene.ts` moved to `platform/scene/` and split into the scene host, head rig and character renderer, then real-GPU tests. Makeup screenshot parity (`tools/scene-parity.ts`, 20 frames: Boards 1 to 3, Shimmer over Glossy and the bare head, both lighting presets, 1K and 2K) is byte-identical to the base after each code commit, within the base's own run-to-run noise (4 pixels by one step on the first creator frame).

- **UI-11:** `scene.ts` (758 lines) is gone. The scene host (`platform/scene/scene-host.ts`, 340 lines) owns the renderer, camera, lights, display, frame scheduling and disposal; the head rig (255) the core head, surfaces, facial shapes and motion; the character renderer (307) the resolved V through the host's `DetailLoader`; the registry (120) each feature's `SceneHostPort`. Nothing eye-specific is left in the host.
- **UI-02 (architecture):** features draw through `FeatureRenderer`s that import only `platform/api`, engines, Three and their own core (boundary-tested); eye makeup's wraps `makeup-stack` through the port; a synthetic second renderer adds its own plate beside it (`tests/scene-feature-renderers.test.ts`). A feature that authors a character part names the slot it replaces in `supersedes`, and the character renderer hides it.
- **PIPE-11 (preview):** the character-detail device loads through the host's detail loader (`scene.details`), and the rendering-boundary test covers `platform/scene/` and eye makeup's renderer.
- **Tests:** `tests/webgl-scene-host.test.ts` runs the real host on a synthetic head in headless Chrome: an idle viewport draws nothing, each request or burst draws one frame, the idle draws only while playing, and `renderer.info.memory` returns to its baseline after V switches through the detail loader and after makeup layers are cleared.

## Fixed in claude/cleanup-engine

The step-5 moves review's findings CORE-75..81, UI-73..75 and PREV-88 (review at `81bcac9`), with the reviewer's injected violations turned into boundary-test cases that must fail. Behaviour and bytes are unchanged: every golden is unchanged, and a Build of the finish board (experiment 016, no diagnostic knobs, WolvenKit 9.0.1, the built-in plate) before and after gives byte-identical baked rasters, DDS inputs, `plan.json`, `compiled.json`, `.archive.xl` and unbundled archive members (only WolvenKit's JSON headers, the archive container and build paths differ, as between any two runs). The rebuilt style guide is byte-identical.

- **CORE-75:** the engine holds no region of its own. `engines/layered-makeup/region.ts` defines `LayeredMakeupRegion`: the layer models, starter and new-layer contours, the mirror of symmetric layers (axis and centre), fine Glitter's scope (ID and regions, in the exact cache key), the texture grids and the area's wording. `editLayers` and the layer actions, recipe actions and gestures, shape transforms and hit tests, the raster (`coverage`, `raster`, `createRasterJob`, `rasterWindow`, `layerCoverageSampler`), the raster worker (each request carries `rasterRegion(region)`), the optical keys, the finish catalogues, the compiler, `plate-reach`, `glitter-route` and `package-bake` all take it; the fast paired raster path runs only for a mirror at u = ½. Eye makeup supplies `EYE_MAKEUP_REGION` from `features/eye-makeup/region.ts` (with the historical `initialRecipe` sample); the composition passes it to the trusted core (`STUDIO_COMPOSITION.region`, checked against the live feature), the startup to the preview and viewport devices (the editors take its models and mirror), eye makeup's renderer passes its fine-Glitter scope to `makeup-stack` (merged after platform step 7), and the export roots (the build tool, the localhost package route, the desktop build and Check worker, `bake_collection`, `validate_collection_build`) to the pipeline. `finish-export.ts` keeps only the route rules. The design doc's claim that the region config stays in `src/` is corrected. Tests: `tests/layered-makeup-region.test.ts` (eye makeup's region reproduces the old constants and wording exactly; raster equals scalar coverage for mirrors at u = ½, u = 0.4 and v = 0.3, job and window rasters included; a region's contour and models decide new layers; two scopes never share a fine-Glitter key; the compiler rasters with the region given). Engine and export tests reach the eye region through `tests/fixtures/eye-region.ts` and `eye-exporter.ts`.
- **CORE-76:** the engine reads only the in-memory recipe (`parseRecipe(value, models)`, `readLayers` with its structural forms). Eye makeup's recipe-file lineage (`eye-artistry/recipe-1`, `xfs/recipe-2`…`11`, each schema's forms and model gates, `RecipeFile`, `RECIPE_FILE_MESSAGE`, `readRecipe`, `parseRecipeFile`, `parseRecipePart`) and its registry (`LAYER_MODELS`, a `RecipeModelRegistry` adding each model's oldest schema) are in `recipe-schema.ts`; the engine's `layer-models.ts` defines the models and a lineage-free `LayerModelRegistry` whose `check` takes an older form's gate. Every engine read and edit takes the feature's registry (through its region). `RecipeActions` (the live document's publisher) is in the application's `authoring-eye-makeup.ts`. Engine text is neutral ("Expected an XF Studio recipe with up to 32 layers."); eye makeup's readers keep their message, and its region supplies "the eye UV area" and "the lid".
- **CORE-77:** a feature's core imports only `platform/api`, engines (not their renderers), the pure helpers, its own folder and a legacy list whose four entries (`recipe-schema`, `history-labels`, `eye-makeup-model`, `eye-makeup-descriptors`) each name their removal step; a test fails when an entry is no longer imported. No feature reaches `platform/core`, `scene` or `export` transitively (a core by any import, a view by value imports), a core reaches no device, Node, Three, studio-ui or `compose/`, and its code reads no page or host global. `editor-actions` keeps only the history owner; the pure layer actions (`LayerAction`, `layerCapability`, `applyLayerAction`) are the engine's (`layer-stack.ts`), so eye makeup's core no longer reaches `platform/core/look-history`. Eye makeup's descriptors could not move into the feature yet (the application, presentation and studio-ui runtime read every kind from `studio-action-descriptors.ts`): they are in `eye-makeup-descriptors.ts` (over the shared builders in `action-descriptor-kit.ts`), which the feature imports and the table spreads in place, so the registry golden is unchanged; it moves into the feature in step 9.
- **CORE-78:** no pure engine module reaches an engine renderer or Three, even through other modules. The `window` allowance is gone: `plate-uv-window.ts` and `rasterWindow` name their locals `uvWindow`/`area`, and pure engine code is scanned with comments and literals blanked (`tests/fixtures/code-scan.ts`) for the window in any use but a property name, `self.`, `document.`, `globalThis`, storage, the navigator, `fetch`, `process.env` and `Bun.env`. The boundary rules run over a source tree; the reviewer's probes A, B and D (and a legacy module reaching the look history by type, and a view importing platform internals) appended to the real tree must each fail.
- **UI-73:** feature views receive a `FeatureViewContext` (`studio-ui/views/feature-view.ts`: the facade, the feature's own dispatch and ranges, `platform` for platform actions, which refuses the feature's kinds by type and at run time, the toast Undo, feedback, anchors, `reveal`, links, target menus and the `uv` slot), never the runtime or the port; panel factories are typed over it (`featureView`). The layer menu and the palette's eye-makeup entries are the view's contributions (`view/layer-menu.ts`, `view/commands.ts`), with identical entries, labels, order, icons, checked states and availability. `tests/studio-ui-boundary.test.ts` holds `@ts-expect-error` type probes and a text backstop with the review's probes E and F; `tests/feature-view-context.test.ts` covers the context.
- **UI-74:** the view import scan uses the shared `tests/fixtures/import-scan.ts` (`IMPORT_FORMS`, `resolveFrom`, `importUses`), which also sees `require` and `import.meta.require` (both boundary tests use it), counts value re-exports and `export *` as value imports, and limits a view's value imports from the shell to an explicit list.
- **UI-75:** the shell's remaining eye-makeup couplings are recorded in the [boundary assessment](ui-architecture-boundary.md#open-work) (item 11), each with an owner and a removal criterion (shared end state: the shell mounts with no features). Fixed now: `Frame.canUndo` reads `port.authoring.history()`; `rt.addLayerCapability`, `rt.glitterModels` and `rt.finishOf` are gone; the palette entries and layer menu belong to the view.
- **CORE-79:** the design's "what stays in `src/`" list is a [table](feature-module-platform.md#file-moves) of each module's destination, blocking importers and step.
- **CORE-80:** `bun run check` also typechecks `tools/` (`tools/tsconfig.json`) and the experiments' scripts (`tsconfig.experiments.json`); CI runs it (`authoring.yml` also triggers on experiment scripts). Fixed: `tools/cc-catalogue.ts` (coverage from `catalogueCoverage()`), `tools/runtime-diagnostic-promotion.ts` (a `Verb` type), experiments 015 and 019, and experiments 008–010, which read the recipe through `readRecipeFile` so each still records its schema (008 and 009 reproduce their committed records byte for byte; 010 matches its shape, normal and surface hashes and schema, its recipe hash differing only because today's sample serialises differently).
- **CORE-81:** current-truth pages name current `src/` paths (`knowledge/cc-file-chain.md`, `knowledge/head-cc-rendering.md`, experiment 002, this ledger, the design doc and the boundary assessment); retired files are named without a path. `tools/check_links.py` now requires every Studio source path in inline code (`src/…ts`, `authoring/src/…`, `projects/xf-studio/authoring/src/…`) to exist; a dated record can wrap lines in `<!-- historical-paths -->` … `<!-- /historical-paths -->` (four research integration tables, the UI overhaul record and one backlog line do).
- **PREV-88:** `tools/glitter-fine-metrics.py` imports the engine and eye makeup's region from their current paths.

## Fixed in claude/cleanup-glitter

Findings PIPE-68 to PIPE-77 from the diagnostic Glitter route review at `368f70b`, with the reviewer's probes (`strength.ts`, `cost.ts`, `vol.ts`, `spread.ts`) turned into tests. Ordinary packages are unchanged: a new golden (`tests/golden/ordinary-package.json`, captured on the code before this branch) pins every committed ordinary collection's plan, plate materials, appearances, morph links and `.archive.xl`, and the finish board's and session 2's baked maps, and the [experiment 020](../../experiments/020-session-2/README.md) candidate rebuilt with this code gave all 41 members byte-identical to the staged build. [Experiment 021](../../experiments/021-glitter-board/README.md) was rebuilt: its 31 textures changed (PIPE-72 widens every region by half the feather, so each catalogue is larger and redrawn) and nothing else did; three builds gave the same 35 members. The [pipeline guide](studio-to-mod-pipeline.md#the-diagnostic-glitter-route) describes the new rules.

- **PIPE-68:** the verifier reads the flakes' contents. On every texel whose mask is 255 inside exactly one region (nested regions on every level, BOX regions at level 0), roughness and metalness must be within one byte of the region's flake values, the diffuse must be the flake colour, most texels must be tilted (spread ≥ 1°), and at least 99 % must carry the tangent of a nearby flake of the verifier's own restatement of the seeded catalogue (xoshiro128**, draw order, mirror), which pins the tangent frame's signs and the flakes' places. At level 0, fully covered texels without flakes must carry a pigment within the active layers' colours and the base surface, moved by the region's restated sheen share. On the 021 build every flake texel matches its catalogue flake (25,500–51,100 per preset) and 98.7–99.6 % are tilted. Tests (`tests/glitter-checks.test.ts`, from `strength.ts`): all-flat normals, flake roughness/metalness reset to the base, negated normal Y, flakes and normals V-flipped, a grey pigment everywhere and a grey pigment between flakes each fail.
- **PIPE-69:** Build clips a region to the plate window before sizing its catalogue, and a region may hold at most 200,000 flakes (`glitter-region.ts`). Planning (`checkRegionPlan` in `planCollection`) applies the rule over the head's UV square, whose count bounds the window's, so Check and Build refuse the same knob with a plain reason; the verifier's restated rules refuse it too. The `Math.min(...)` spreads are loops. Tests: `cost.ts`'s whole-lid knob at 0.05 mm and 60 % cover is refused by Check and by the verifier; a layer reaching past the window gets exactly the clipped rectangle's catalogue.
- **PIPE-70:** the builder CLI honours diagnostic knobs only with an explicit `--diagnostics`; without it a file carrying `diagnostics` is refused with that reason. Neither host passes the flag. Tests: a posted collection with a valid glitter knob gives no glitter route and no diagnostics through localhost's Check, the file localhost hands the builder carries no knob, the desktop builder gets neither the knob nor the flag, and the desktop Check worker returns flat routes (`package-server.test.ts`, `desktop/tests/build.test.ts`, `desktop/tests/check-runner.test.ts`); the CLI refuses the file without the flag and builds the glitter route with it (`package-build-cli.test.ts`). The 017, 019 and 021 build commands and the 020 build record now pass `--diagnostics`.
- **PIPE-71:** the symmetry refusal is a planning rule (`layerOutlineBounds`, via `checkRegionPlan`), so Check refuses a mirrored region layer as Build does. Test: `preflightPackageCollection` refuses it with the plain reason.
- **PIPE-72:** a region is its layer's outline bounds: the convex hull of its control polygon (each knot and both tangent endpoints on a Bézier outline, or each Catmull–Rom knot with the segment's equivalent Bézier controls, which covers overshoot), plus half the widest softness width and the summed warp displacement. The verifier restates the rule (`restatedOutline`) and uses the same rectangle. Tests: for a curved Bézier lid, a Catmull–Rom outline, per-knot softness and a warp field, the builder's and verifier's bounds are identical and every covered texel of a 1024² raster lies inside them; the curved lid's bulge above its knots gets flakes and passes the verifier.
- **PIPE-73:** the verifier's knob rules add `tiltMaxDeg ≥ σ/4` and "no surface override or head UV beside the knob", plus the region rules above. Tests: each is refused.
- **PIPE-74:** the verifier decodes the accent's stored BC4 level itself and requires the exported level with its rows reversed, and samples the accent mask at the packaged plate's own UVs on a dense barycentric grid (91 points per triangle, 551,060 on the built-in plate) against its layer's authored head-UV coverage, which Build now bakes beside the preset's reference (`accentReference`): at most 2 % of the drawn mass may fall where the layer has none. The on-flakes statistic is now taken over the accent components away from the feathered edge, where both masks fade (85 % of 160 on the 021 build; over all 291 it would be 77 %, near the 75 % gate). Tests: an accent stored in natural row order and an accent sampled against another layer's coverage both fail (`tests/glitter-route.test.ts`).
- **PIPE-75:** the canvas planes, clip mask and sheen planes are allocated once at level-0 size and reused as views for every level; the accent canvas holds the mask only; the normal owner is a `Uint32Array` of global stamping ranks (key order, ties to the flake stamped first) rather than the suggested Float32 keys, which could not keep every key distinct and would have changed ownership; on its own the refactor left every channel's bytes unchanged (board presets A and E, before PIPE-72 changed the regions). The verifier builds its per-texel messages only on failure (`ensure` takes a function; hot loops test first). Peak RSS of one 4096 × 1024 board preset's compile (fresh process, `cost.ts`): *A* 1,163 → 895 MB, *B* 1,280 → 1,092, *C* 944 → 871, *D* 931 → 873, *E* (accent) 1,077 → 1,003, *F* 951 → 881; compile time 2.2–3.5 → 2.0–3.0 s. The remaining floor is the shared flat pigment compile at 4096 × 1024 (`compileFlatPreset` and `flatMipChain`, about 750 MB peak on their own), left unchanged because ordinary builds use it. Verifying one preset's chains takes 0.2–0.25 s (1.0 s with a BOX region), 20–65 ms more than before with the new content checks.
- **PIPE-76:** two bakes of the glitter test collection are identical in every channel, the accent, both references and the records (`tests/glitter-route.test.ts`); the ordinary-package golden above; negative checks for tilt beyond the maximum, a region without flakes (density), a flake-free level's sheen, accent placement and flake contents; the Check test for a symmetric region (PIPE-71).
- **PIPE-77:** `volume-info.ts` takes its platform and volume API as seams (`createFolderStampMode`) and caches the answer per scan root as well as per volume. Tests (`tests/volume-info.test.ts`, from `vol.ts`): outside Windows folder times are used and no API is loaded; a kernel32 that can't load, or a failing query, means listing; NTFS and ReFS use times, FAT32, exFAT, network shares and unknown volumes listings; a repeated root asks nothing; `C:\` gives `"time"`.

## Fixed in claude/cleanup-blink

Findings PREV-80 to PREV-85, UI-61, UI-62 and CORE-65 from the `e1219fd` game blink review. The reviewer's probe cases are now tests (`tests/game-blink-composition.test.ts`, 7 tests). Checked in the browser (`?verify=1`, own port, disposable library data, the rebaked local blink) with the default V; private captures stayed in the scratch folder.

- **PREV-80:** at Closure 0 with Play blink stopped, `setShape` and `attach` update only the seat and the bindings and write no bone (`GameBlink.reapply`); a held closure or Play blink is applied again as before. Test: a paused idle, then an eye-shape change and a detail joining, leave every bone's pose bit-identical, and the seat taken meanwhile applies when the lids next close. In the browser, a paused idle with eye shape 1 → 10 → 1 kept the head and neck exactly where the idle held them (the capture after returning to shape 1 is pixel-identical to the one before).
- **PREV-81:** `GameBlink.animating` is true only while Play blink is inside the clip; the scene's scheduler uses it. When the clip's last frame has been drawn the loop stops and one timer (`BlinkTimer`, injectable) wakes the viewport for the next blink; frames drawn meanwhile for other reasons advance the cycle and a stale wake-up only asks for a frame. Stopping, a closure, reset and dispose clear it. Test on a fake clock: 30 to 33 frames per 0.5 s clip, none during the hold, one pending timer, fewer than 34 frames per cycle over six cycles. In the browser: 62 frames over 4.9 s of Play blink (two cycles), where the whole cycle used to redraw (about 294).
- **PREV-82:** the constructor compares each bound target's world bind with its rig joint's rest and refuses the blink with `GAME_BLINK_OTHER_HEAD` beyond 0.1 mm (`BLINK_BIND_TOLERANCE`); the derived female head matches to 0.0003 mm. Details attached later are not held to it, since they follow the checked rig in world space and three cached hair meshes bind eye and mouth joints 5 to 8 mm away. The bake records `asset.extras.rig` (skeleton, setup, body gender from the `pwa`/`pma` name), which the parser validates (optional for older bakes) and the scene evidence reports. Tests: a head 2 mm off is refused, 0.05 mm is accepted, a hair bone 5 mm off follows the lid.
- **PREV-83:** `setFaceMorphs` ends with `blink?.setShape(activeEyeShape(head))`.
- **PREV-84:** `--setup` without `--output` and `--evidence` stops with a plain argparse error; the report names the setup actually used with its SHA-256 and the rig (`rig`, `setup.used`, `setup.sha256`, `setup.role`); an identical report is not rewritten. The bake is reproducible: rebaked with the pinned solver, the asset differs from the old one only by the 136-byte `rig` entry (old SHA-256 `f95ba7e7…`, new `10bbd5d8…`), and a second run left the report unchanged.
- **PREV-85:** the knowledge page states the per-eye-shape seat as a hypothesis chosen on the sum, lists the measured shapes with the `h011` regression (10.2 / 5.5 % re-seated against 3.6 / 0 % about the base centre), says `h111` was seen only in the browser, and adds `h011` to open question 3 and the in-game ask; the research page and CHANGELOG say the same.
- **UI-61:** `loadGameBlink` maps an unfetchable asset to `GAME_BLINK_MISSING`, any GLB parse failure to `GAME_BLINK_DAMAGED` ("The prepared blink is damaged; prepare it again."), and no matching joints to its own `GAME_BLINK_NO_JOINTS`; the scene's duplicate check is gone. The messages live in the Three-free `game-blink-messages.ts`, and `MotionActions` uses the constant for its fallback reason and snapshot (`blinkError` is always set while the blink is unavailable). Tests: 404, network failure, an HTML page, random bytes, GLBs cut in the header and in the data, another schema version, no joints and another head, through a GLB built from the miniature rig. In the browser, a truncated local asset showed "The prepared blink is damaged; prepare it again." in the Motion panel and the refusals.
- **UI-62:** the Motion note carries a "How to prepare the blink" link (a new `blink-guide` project link to the idle guide's blink section, opened by the host like the Help links) while the blink is unavailable. The repeat text is built from the snapshot's `blinkRepeatSeconds` (the scene's `BLINK_REPEAT_SECONDS`) as "a Studio choice: the idle's average blink spacing" (`blinkNoteLine`, tested). A one-click, consent-gated "prepare idle and blink" flow for the desktop app is queued in the [backlog](../backlog/prepare-idle-and-blink.md).
- **CORE-65:** the scene's idle/blink rules moved into `preview-motion.ts` (`composePreviewMotion`: `animating`, `advance`, `setIdle`, `attach`, `detach`, `connect`), which `scene.ts` calls in place of the inline code. The source-text test is replaced by a scene double driving the real `IdleAnimation` and `GameBlink` through it with `createRenderScheduler` on a fake clock: the idle takes over from a closed blink and hands back the exact editing pose, a detail joins the blink before the idle, frames stop between blinks, and disconnecting disposes the wake-up. Added: damaged assets (above), no drift over 5,000 scrubs, shape switches and played frames (below 1e-12, exact restore), and restoring a saved Closure and Play blink through `MotionActions` onto a real blink. `tests/render-scheduler.test.ts` checks the scene's one `rigMotion.connect(invalidate)` line instead of the idle's own wiring.

## Fixed in claude/cleanup-ccpanel

Findings PIPE-78 to PIPE-83, PREV-86, PREV-87, UI-67 to UI-72 and CORE-70 to CORE-74 from the Character panel review at `6a1ff15`, with the reviewer's probes turned into tests (`tests/cc-panel-review.test.ts`, `tests/character-panel-dom.test.ts` over a new light DOM harness, `tests/light-dom.ts`). The registry and spec-fields goldens were recaptured: the diff is `character.hideOwnMakeup`, `character.retry`, `setOption`'s optional `activates` and the shared 255-character name bound. The look-history parity golden and the 0.1.0-alpha.1 reader tests are unchanged. The character record is now `xfs/render-detail-7`, so every V is prepared once more after the update. Checked in headless Chrome (`?verify=1`, own port, this worktree's copy of the caches, reference MO2 profile) with the reference save, save B, the default V and a preset at the limits; a failed preparation was simulated in the page (the host's answer replaced), since the real host prepared every change. Timings (warm): Hide my V's own makeup (2 rows on this save) 0.23–1.07 s to settled; a keyboard-only Eye Color change 0.23 s with focus kept on the same swatch, and Ctrl+Z restored the old choice with focus kept; Default V 1.4 s and Undo back to the save 1.3 s; save B 1.7 s (32 s the first time, WolvenKit exports); a 3,001-entry preset 26 ms to the view and 0.6–1.2 s to the prepared V. The status line (24 px), the quick-action section and the search field kept their positions in every frame of the run. The reference installation has no same-named switcher choices (ArchiveXL merges them by name), so CORE-70 is covered by the fixture tests only.

- **PIPE-78 (High):** a failed catalogue build is kept per fingerprint with its plain line; `state()` starts only the first build for an installation and never builds again; a question that needs the catalogue builds again only after a backoff (10 s, doubling, at most 10 minutes), and `retry` (`POST {kind:"retry"}`, the panel's **Try again**, `character.retry`) builds at once. The context stops at a failure (no polling) and offers Try again; while the build runs it polls every 0.8 s only while the panel is being looked at, else every 4 s. Tests: a build that always fails is built once over 20 state reads, then once more after the backoff; the endpoint answers `failed` (503) with a plain line; Try again follows the catalogue again.
- **PIPE-79:** one name and limit rule (`src/creator-names.ts`): a creator name is at most 255 characters without control characters or a backslash (`/`, quotes and angle brackets are legal CNames); mod names 255 and never a path; 2,048 choices per V; one request limit (8 MiB) for both endpoints.
