The preset reader, `characterChoiceOf`, `savedDescriptorsOf`, the request parser, the page's option IDs and the action descriptors all use it. A preset entry whose name is only too long is kept verbatim (`unknownEntries` with `unusable`) and written back; entries past 2,048 are reported (`carryPreset`, reason `not-carried`) and never sent, and stay in the kept entries for Save preset. Tests: preset → request → host → stored round-trips names at 255 characters with quotes, slashes and angle brackets; a 3,001-entry preset carries 2,048 and reports the rest.
- **PIPE-80:** the preparation derives a V with choices from a structural catalogue of the merged creator resource it already loads (`structuralInput`: options, choices, links and activation, no texts or TweakDB, memoised per merged resource), so a stored choice no longer waits for the panel's catalogue. Choices that can't be interpreted show the V without them, with one plain line in the ready state's message, and the answer is degraded (prepared again next time). Test: the structural input equals the full catalogue's.
- **PIPE-81:** every page and search answer carries the catalogue's identity; an answer from another catalogue starts the panel again.
- **PIPE-82:** `slotChoices`, `RenderOverride`, the record's `choices` and `character.override` and their readers are gone; the record schema is `xfs/render-detail-7` (v6 is refused as retired, so a page and a host of different versions show the version-skew notice). The README describes the Piercings row instead of tried styles.
- **PIPE-83:** both endpoints refuse a declared `Content-Length` over the limit before reading, and count the body in bytes as it streams (`src/request-body.ts`); `deriveCharacter` keeps one choice per option (the last, in its place); switcher choices are indexed by their activated options (`CatalogueIndex.activating`), so matching and link following are no longer quadratic.
- **PREV-86:** the follower asks for the V's details only when the context's request changes (`src/character-follow.ts`), and `CharacterDetailActions.setCharacter` never prepares the same request again, even after a failure; `retry()` (through `character.retry`) is the one way to try again. Tests: five publishes after a failure send nothing; Try again sends exactly one request.
- **PREV-87:** the context's view is the one last writer of the face: its shape is applied only once the view answers the current state, and written again whenever the saved-V service changes the scene (a save applied or cleared). Test: the same shape is written again after the saved-V service wrote the face.
- **UI-67:** a row's choices are a `ChoiceList` (`src/studio-ui/panels/character-choices.ts`): selection and roving focus change attributes in place, a later page is appended (Off joins the front), another option or search rebuilds and returns focus to the same choice by position. Measured: Show more appended 14 choices to 240 in 62 ms with the first element and focus unchanged.
- **CORE-70:** a switcher choice's identity (the options it activates) travels with `character.setOption`; the context checks it, the host matches it (indexed), R5 takes the chosen position (`SwitcherPicks` in `cco-model.ts`), the view reports each value's `position`, and the list marks the chosen item by position. Tests: two same-named hairstyles, each turning on its own hair, from the page to R5 and in the list.
- **CORE-71:** `character.hideOwnMakeup` is an action of `CharacterContextActions` with a capability (loading, the view still on its way, every makeup row already Off); the host's projection marks the makeup section (`makeup`, `xfs/cc-panel-2`); the panel only dispatches, and the command palette has the command (with Reset every creator change).
- **CORE-72:** every catalogue follow is a session with a generation: a new one aborts the previous pages, searches and views, and an answer that arrives anyway is dropped. Test: a page in flight across a body change never lands under the new body's option.
- **CORE-73:** every action that changes choices (set, reset, reset all, keep changes, hide makeup) needs the catalogue of the shown V's body; a V change and Undo/Redo don't, so a V is never stuck behind a failed catalogue. `setOptions` changes are validated whole by the shared rule; the action catalogue says so.
- **CORE-74:** an earlier build's tried piercing style (the retired preview fields) becomes the matching Piercings choices once the catalogue is ready, if the context is untouched: the host finds the offered head switcher choice with that name activating an option that offers the definition (`legacyChoices`, `POST {kind:"legacy"}`), from the catalogue's data alone. Once the host has matched a loaded preset's choices, only the entries it couldn't match are kept.
- **UI-68:** one status line of fixed height (clamped to one line, full text in its tooltip) with Try again, Keep my changes and Details inline, keeping their place when not offered; the plain lines about what couldn't be used sit behind Details; a row reserves its detail line only when one of its options has a static detail, and "not shown in the 3D view" for a conditional option is a fixed-size marker in the row head.
- **UI-69:** "your save stays loaded" and "uses the creator default" are replaced by what happens ("Undo shows your V again"; a save's choice "can't be changed here"; a preset's choice "keeps the V's own look"); the device words every host refusal by its code and a network failure in one plain line, never the host's own text.
- **UI-70:** choices are a listbox: arrow keys, Home and End move focus without choosing (each choice prepares the V anew), Enter or Space chooses; provenance is each item's and each row's accessible description; **Reset all** dispatches `character.resetAll`. Documented in the style guide's Creator options pattern.
- **UI-71:** tests for the follower, `makeupOff`/`rowOption`, the panel DOM (light DOM harness), a failing build and names at the limits. The boundary scan reads the static parts of template literals and covers the composition root (`browser-head-attachment.ts`) and the new modules.
- **UI-72:** search runs on the host over every choice (`GET ?search=`, `searchChoices`); an open row whose name doesn't match lists only its matching choices (`choicePage` with a query).

## Fixed in claude/cc-panel

CC controls slice 2: the generated Character panel with live preview, plus findings CORE-50 to CORE-62, PIPE-46 to PIPE-49, PIPE-51 and UI-59 to UI-60 from the creator catalogue review, and PIPE-53, PIPE-56, PIPE-65, CORE-63 and CORE-64 from the prepare speed review. PIPE-50 is left to claude/cleanup-hosts3 (above). The look-history parity golden and the 0.1.0-alpha.1 reader tests are unchanged; the registry and spec-fields goldens were recaptured for the new family (the diff is the `characterContext` kinds, `savedV.clear` and the two preset file workflows, less `character.tryChoice`). Checked in the browser (`?verify=1`, reference MO2 profile, own port, disposable data) with the default V, the reference save (three legacy XF Eye Artistry layers, lipstick, blush) and save B; timings are in the [CC controls backlog](../backlog/cc-controls-and-presets.md#measured-in-the-browser-verify1-reference-mo2-profile-26-september).

- **CORE-58:** the character context owns every creator choice for the shown V: `CharacterContextActions` (family `characterContext`, registered in `STUDIO_OWNERS` with a `StudioApplication` handler) holds the V's source and the choices set; the host interprets them (`deriveCharacter`) for the view and the preparation (`xfs/character-request-4` carries the save's descriptors and the choices). `character.tryChoice`, `applyChoiceOverride` and `descriptorsFromSwitcher` are gone; the piercing try is the Piercings row. Tests: `tests/character-context.test.ts`, `tests/piercing-resolver.test.ts` (a piercing chosen in the context, several targets and a linked follower through R5), `tests/studio-application.test.ts`, `tests/studio-registry.test.ts`.
- **CORE-50, CORE-51:** only the choices a person set are stored, one per link family (the latest wins); followers and other controllers are derived at request time, Reset removes the family's choice so it recomputes from the base, and a preset writes only the choices set. Tests: reset of a skin tone on a save gives neck, skin type and body back their saved tone; one skin tone is one preset entry.
- **CORE-52:** `savedDescriptorsOf` validates a save whole before anything changes (the capability refuses it with a plain reason; `recoverSave` is a pure function over validated input).
- **CORE-53:** CNames with `/` and names up to 512 characters round-trip; the writer leaves out (and counts) an entry the reader would refuse instead of writing a file the Studio refuses whole (`presetEntryWritable`).
- **CORE-54:** unknown preset data is copied into prototype-free objects; a `__proto__` key is kept as data and written back.
- **CORE-55:** the 1 MB limit counts UTF-8 bytes; unknown data is bounded (depth 16, 20,000 nodes) with plain reasons; a file's JSON is read once (`readPresetJson`) and the context parses the preset once (the capability's parse is reused by dispatch).
- **CORE-56:** the host checks a preset before writing it with the repository's personal-data patterns (`src/private-data.ts`, which imports only the pattern keys of `tools/private-data.json`, so no bundle carries its test vectors): a name, kept entry or kept field naming a user folder or an address is left out and counted, and a text that still holds one is refused. Test: every vector of the shared file is flagged or passed as the repository scan does.
- **CORE-57:** R5's activation is exported from `cco-model` (`activeOptionNames`, `r5Roots`) and used by both R5 and the context's view.
- **CORE-59:** creator changes have their own Undo history (in memory, 100 steps): every change, the quick action, a save or preset load (one step each, with Keep my changes), and Undo across a V change shows the earlier V (`savedV.restore`, the new `savedV.clear`). `character.reset` requires its part and option; `character.resetAll` resets everything.
- **CORE-60:** the catalogue carries no coverage; the preview side projects it (`catalogueCoverage`) when the panel projection is built, and a `conditional` option settles from the parts the shown record draws (`status.assets.characterDetails.drawn`).
- **CORE-61 (hypothesis):** linked switchers take the choice activating the same options (`linkedChoice`); documented with in-game test ask 11 on the knowledge page.
- **CORE-62:** the catalogue copies every list it takes from the merged resource. Test: changing the catalogue's lists leaves the resource untouched.
- **CORE-63:** a change that can't be prepared keeps the V as it was shown and says why (`updateError`, the panel's status line) until the next change; asking again retries. Test: `tests/character-switch.test.ts`.
- **CORE-64:** the context family needs no scene: before the preview is ready, and while the catalogue loads, a change is refused as `not_ready`.
- **PIPE-46:** a base creator resource won by a mod archive names that mod on its options and choices.
- **PIPE-47:** the text table keys entries by primary key alone, as ArchiveXL does: a same-ID mod entry replaces the whole entry, a secondary-keyed mod entry is added under FNV-1a 32 and 64 of its key, the fallback language only fills in. The secondary-key lookup's tie-break is recorded as a hypothesis on the knowledge page.
- **PIPE-48:** `textPlan` and `gameLanguageOf` are pure and unit-tested.
- **PIPE-49:** every TweakDB count is bounded by the bytes that could hold it before anything is allocated or read.
- **PIPE-51:** a language whose game texts aren't installed falls back to English with the gap `texts-language-missing`; an unset or relative `LOCALAPPDATA` reads no settings file.
- **PIPE-53:** a preparation during which the fetcher's transient count grew, or WolvenKit failed on an archive, is degraded: everything it added to the preparation cache is forgotten (`CharacterPreparationCache.mark`/`forget`), and the host serves the answer once and prepares it again when asked again. Tests: `tests/cc-catalogue-service.test.ts`.
- **PIPE-56:** the planner builds slot labels and messages within the record's limits (`clampedList`: whole names, then "and N more"), and the reader cuts one that is still too long with a note instead of refusing the V. Test: `tests/render-detail-record.test.ts`.
- **PIPE-65:** one rule picks a switcher choice by name, preferring the one that turns an option on (`switcherChoiceNamed`), for R5 and the catalogue alike.
- **UI-59:** the panel gets a compact projection (`xfs/cc-panel-1`, 300 KB for 943 options and 131,856 choices on the reference installation) with choices paged per option, built once per installation in the long-lived host. Test: a 1,000-option, 130,000-choice creator stays under 600 KB.
- **UI-60:** a head option without a category is filed beside the rows around its position.

## Fixed in claude/cleanup-platform3

Findings CORE-45 to CORE-49, PIPE-44, PIPE-45 and UI-53 to UI-55 from the platform step 5 review at `4afea26`, and UI-52 in part. Stored workspace and library bytes of the committed fixtures, the look-history parity golden, the registry golden and the spec-fields golden are unchanged; the 0.1.0-alpha.1 reader tests pass. The reviewer's probes are now tests.

- **CORE-45:** `PartRegistry.readCollection` never trusts a `locked` key: a look is locked only when reading its parts (or, in a stored workspace, its memory) meets a newer build's data. In-memory drafts are validated again with `rereadCollection` (rename and preset edits), which keeps a look this session locked. Tests (`tests/locked-looks.test.ts`): an imported file carrying the key with a garbage body is refused with exactly the message it gets without the key, through the reader and the import request; a readable preset carrying the key opens editable, in a file and in a stored workspace; a look locked only by its newer Undo history stays locked through rename and collection rename, is stored without the key and locks again on restore.
- **CORE-46:** Redo validity also compares the other live features' revision (`AuthoringDocument.partRevision`); when it changed, the look's content key decides, so a change that records no step hides Redo and a selection does not. Test (`tests/look-transaction.test.ts`, from the probe): a `none`-policy content action after Undo removes Redo and keeps its value; a hair selection after Undo keeps Redo.
- **CORE-47:** `fitWorkspace` has two new stages: before dropping recovery drafts or removed presets it writes locked looks in them without their kept memory (`plan.kept: "outside"`), and as the last resort the current presets' locked looks too (`"everywhere"`). Their parts are always verbatim; a look locked by its parts is locked again on restore. Test: the probe's over-budget workspace (a 200-step newer history) now fits at half its size with the locked look's parts unchanged and writable on reload; a recovery draft's kept memory goes first, the draft and the current draft's memory stay.
- **CORE-48:** `app.transaction` refuses a thenable result (reverting what ran synchronously, no step, no Redo) with a plain message, and its type (`NotThenable<T>`) rejects an async body at compile time (`@ts-expect-error` in the test).
- **CORE-49:** the draft summary marks an oldest recoverable draft that holds a locked look, the consequence carries its ID and the lock, and the import/open confirmation then says the draft may be that look's only copy and offers **Export collection** for it. That exports the earlier draft unsaved (`exportCollection` takes an optional `draft`; a draft no longer queued is refused with `missing_target`). Tests: the summary, the consequence and the export of the earlier draft in `tests/locked-looks.test.ts`.
- **PIPE-44:** a locked look never counts as packageable; when nothing else is, Export plan, Check and Build are refused with a plain reason ("Every look in this collection was made with a newer version of XF Studio…", or, beside looks without eye makeup, that the looks with eye makeup are newer). Export collection stays available.
- **PIPE-45:** the Export plan's message names the looks it leaves out instead of saying they are exported as they came, and the plan file carries the view's `omitted` list. The schema stays `xfas/export-plan-1`: the field is optional and present only when something is left out, the package pipeline plans its packaged copy (which never carries it), and no reader checks for unknown fields, so every existing reader and every normal plan is unchanged.
- **UI-52 (part):** `featureStateOf` reads the editor document's feature instead of `"eye-makeup"`. The facade-only dispatch boundary test is deferred to the file moves (see the open findings).
- **UI-53:** facades expose `locked()` (the plain reason, or undefined), backed by `StudioApplication.featureLocked`; the Layers notice and the viewport hint read `Frame.locked`, and the unused `Frame.editable` is gone.
- **UI-54:** the shift strength, size variation and orientation spread sliders take their ranges from `rt.range`; field density is a percentage of the registered count range; the density/size refusal comes from the domain (`IRREGULAR_RANGE_MESSAGE`, built from the flake limits) and the panel shows it as it is; the finish catalogue's `stored` names (Satin's `satin`) replace the alias in `inspector.ts`, `layers.ts` and `target-menus.ts` (`StudioRuntime.finishOf`).
- **UI-55:** "Undo the last change (outside text fields)" and "There is no change to undo."; the style guide's Undo scope text; `feature-module-platform.md` and `ui-architecture-boundary.md` now describe the step-5 names as current. The parity golden, which records the old refusal text, is compared through a one-entry map of that intentional rename in its observer.
- **Browser (`?verify=1`, private port and data):** an imported file whose newer look carries a `locked` key and a damaged body was refused with the same message as without the key; a newer look imported locked showed the Layers notice with "Get the latest version" and the viewport hint; with only that look left, Export plan and Check were refused with the new reason while Export collection stayed available; after four more imports the import confirmation named the discarded draft's newer look and offered Export collection. No console errors.

## Fixed in claude/platform-step5

Step 5 of the [feature-module platform](feature-module-platform.md#step-5-status), without its file moves. Details, decisions, tests and measurements are in the design's step 5 status. Every golden except the registry snapshot is unchanged; that one differs by exactly the Undo-port rename (`history.undo`/`history.redo`, policy `part`).

- **CORE-03 (routing, presentation):** `port.features()`/`port.feature(id)` facades (the port's `editor` became eye makeup's `view()`); a live document per registered feature, generic feature handlers and `app.transaction(label, features, fn)`, tested with a synthetic second feature; view contributions for the panel catalogue, factory layouts, closed-panel homes, lazily repainted panels and activity sources (saved layouts restore unchanged; `<feature>.<panel>` IDs now accepted by the dock).
- **UI-10 (partly):** `units`, `limits` and `consequence` are action-spec fields (`action-limits.ts` retired), with a golden captured before the move.
- **CORE-27 (extended):** newer data in a collection draft or an imported collection file locks only its look, which is kept verbatim and written back exactly by every writer; the rest of the workspace stays editable. The loose editor, workspace-1 drafts and a newer workspace schema keep the read-only protection.

## Fixed in claude/cleanup-hosts3

Findings PIPE-52, PIPE-54, PIPE-55, PIPE-57 to PIPE-64, PIPE-66, PIPE-67 and PREV-73 to PREV-79 from the `88b5379` prepare speed and layered cleanup review, with the reviewer's probes turned into tests. Measured on the reference MO2 route with WolvenKit 9.0.1 in one process through the character-detail host (default V, reference save, save B, the default V again, styles 09, 12 and 14, then both saves again; warm caches, the second of two runs each; offline measurements, not game evidence): the first V in the process 3.06 → 2.99 s (it opens the route), the reference save 1.07 → 0.85 s, save B 0.45 → 0.38 s, the tried styles 0.12/0.42/0.13 → 0.08/0.37/0.09 s; stamp checks for the ten requests 13 → 8; heap after the three Vs 274 → 133 MB and after everything 318 → 136 MB (growth over the first V: +105 → +29 MB), with 44 MiB of JSON kept by the one graph. The resolver integration test (`XFS_RESOLVER_REFERENCE=1`, reference save) and the creator catalogue oracle pass on the real install. In the browser (`?verify=1`, own port, disposable data, reference profile): the default V, then styles 12 gold, 14 gold and 09 black tried in turn, then a forced WebGL context loss and restore; every layered chunk baked again after the restore with the same sizes, the resolved eye stayed shown, and the only console errors were the private idle assets this worktree doesn't stage.

- **PIPE-52:** a route dropped to make room for another keeps its watch list; `revalidate` compares it for a route that isn't open, and an open compares it after opening (a change made even during the open counts), so a change inside an MO2 mod folder while the route was closed moves the generation on. A route whose kept list is let go (more than eight) has its generation moved on. An unchanged route keeps its key. Tests: `tests/installation-lifetime.test.ts` (the reviewer's probe, with and without a check first).
- **PIPE-54:** a resource the archive lists but WolvenKit wrote nothing for, in launches that all finished cleanly (the `unbundle` step's exit is now judged too), gets a `not-written` marker counting batches; from the second (`NOT_WRITTEN_RUNS`) it is lasting, keyed like every marker by the archive's fingerprint and the WolvenKit identity, and removed once the resource is written. The fetch port says whether a null may not repeat (`transient`, per cache folder's lane, whichever fetcher answered); the graph counts only consumer reads (`retryableFailures`), so a prefetched resource nobody asked for no longer replaces the graph or forgets the merged creator resource. Tests: `tests/resolver-fetch-markers.test.ts` (the reviewer's probe: transient, then lasting, then no launch; an unclean launch doesn't count; another WolvenKit tries again), `tests/resource-graph-retention.test.ts`, `tests/installation-registry.test.ts`.
- **PIPE-55:** the graph keeps a mesh, morph target or `.app` as the shape its model reads (appearances, material entries and instances, render-chunk masks, targets, definitions) with only its type as the loaded root, and counts what it keeps (`retainedBytes`: the cache file's JSON length, or the shape's). Over the reference cache the new shapes build identical models to the old whole documents for 269 meshes, 334 morph targets and 349 `.app`s, keeping 21.9 MB where the documents are about 485 MB. The registry keeps the views' graphs of every route within `MAX_GRAPH_BYTES` (256 MiB) in total, giving the least recently used views fresh graphs first and the view being handed out last, and drops a route whose WolvenKit changed in place. On the reference route the graph keeps 44 MiB after the three Vs and three styles, so nothing is evicted in that use; the heap after them fell from 318 to 136 MB. Tests: `tests/resource-graph-retention.test.ts`, `tests/installation-lifetime.test.ts`.
- **PIPE-57:** the installation summary carries `readErrors` (unread archive indexes, unreadable folders, entries, mod list or settings file, unreadable `.xl`); an installation with any is opened again once it is `PROBLEM_TTL_MS` (60 s) old, and its generation moves on only if the errors changed. Test: `tests/installation-lifetime.test.ts`.
- **PIPE-58:** `volume-info.ts` reads the volume's file system (`GetVolumePathNameW`, `GetDriveTypeW`, `GetVolumeInformationW`, no process); folder times are trusted on NTFS and ReFS only, and elsewhere (FAT32, exFAT, network shares, an unidentified volume) source discovery stamps each scanned folder by a hash of the listing it read (`list|…`), which the registry's check reads again. POSIX volumes keep folder times. Test: a folder's time put back after adding an archive is missed by time stamps and caught by listing stamps (`tests/installation-lifetime.test.ts`). The development machine's volumes are all NTFS, so the listing route runs only in the test.
- **PIPE-59:** `GradingLutHost.refresh` revalidates its registry (the shared one, or its private one over the test seam), the LUT endpoint calls it before answering on both hosts, and the LUT key passes that registry to `installationFingerprint`. Test: `tests/installation-lifetime.test.ts`.
- **PIPE-60:** both extraction routes store and serve JSON without `Header.ArchiveFileName` (`withoutArchiveFileName`, also applied to older cache files when read). The opt-in oracle `tests/wolvenkit-uncook-oracle.test.ts` compares `uncook -u -s` with `unbundle` + `convert s` on seven vanilla resources (a material instance, a mesh, an `.app`, an `.ent`, a morph target, a layer setup and a texture) per accepted WolvenKit version; with the export time set aside it passed for 9.0.1 and 8.17.4 on the reference install.
- **PIPE-61:** `uncookPatterns` splits a launch's paths so the escaped pattern (counted as `depotPathRegex` writes it) stays within `MAX_PATTERN_CHARS` and the whole command line (the CLI, every archive, the output folder, the flags, Windows quoting) within 32,767 characters; a batch's archives are capped at `MAX_ARCHIVE_CHARS`. Test: 60 long archive paths and 900 escaped paths (`tests/resolver-fetch-markers.test.ts`).
- **PIPE-62:** a clean `revalidate` vouches for the next acquire of that route within `CHECK_FRESH_MS` (250 ms), provided nothing reused the installation since that check began; an acquire after an acquire always checks. A POST now reads each stamp once. Test: `tests/installation-lifetime.test.ts`. The synchronous reopen (about 1 s) still blocks the event loop; moving discovery off the loop was not in scope.
- **PIPE-63:** precedence ambiguities are recorded for consumer reads (`load`, `provenance`, and each memoised model's resource and patch sources on every ask), never for prefetched reads; `ResourceGraph.collect` gathers one resolution's, the merged creator resource carries its own, and `resolveCharacter` reports those instead of everything the long-lived graph ever met. Test: a cold cache (prefetching a template) and a warm one report the same ambiguities, and a later V on the graph reports its own (`tests/resource-graph-retention.test.ts`).
- **PIPE-64:** an `.app`'s part `.ent`s are no longer prefetched; the resolver reads the requested appearance's parts together (one batch); patch sources are read only once the target is provided. Tests: only appearance `b`'s two parts are read, two at once; a patch of an absent target reads nothing (`tests/resource-graph-retention.test.ts`); the prefetch chain test reads parts together as the resolver does.
- **PIPE-66:** a registered custom creator file an archive provides but that could not be read is the gap `custom-cco-unreadable`, worded from the graph's `loadErrors`; one no archive provides stays `custom-cco-missing`. Test: `tests/resource-graph-retention.test.ts`.
- **PIPE-67:** `chunkInScene` counts only a missing mask as drawn; an empty flag list, `0` or a value of another type is not. The reference cache has no empty mask. Test: `tests/resource-graph-retention.test.ts`.
- **PREV-73:** a bake while the renderer's context is lost stays pending (baked after the restore), and a bake that failed because the context was lost (`isContextLost`, or the draws' `CONTEXT_LOST_WEBGL`) is re-armed by `contextRestored`; any other failure stands. Test: `tests/layered-material.test.ts`.
- **PREV-74:** the scene publishes the placed V's limits whenever they change after placement (`onBakeLimits`: a hidden slot shown and baked, a re-bake after a context restore), the device words them per shown slot as `show` does (`onLimits`), and the character-detail service replaces the shown slots' codes (`withSlotLimits`). After a restore the core eye's visibility is recomputed from what baked again. Hook in the claude/cc-panel file `character-detail-actions.ts`: the optional port member `onLimits` and one constructor line. Tests: `tests/detail-limit-updates.test.ts`, the restore and visibility wiring in `tests/render-scheduler.test.ts`.
- **PREV-75:** the bake asks for the framebuffer status once per target (the two accumulation targets and the kept maps) and for WebGL errors once per pass (after the clear, after all layers, after the resolve), not after every layer: a 12-layer stack makes 3 status and 4 error queries instead of 13 and 14. An error from any layer is still caught (the flag stays set until read). Test: `tests/layered-material.test.ts`.
- **PREV-76:** the mesh-wide normal's intensity, UV scale and bias are clamped to the layers' ranges. A bottom layer whose template could not be read is drawn as a neutral stand-in (mid grey, fully rough, not metallic, no maps) instead of being left out (which baked black at zero roughness), with the limit `layered-base` ("The base finish of some of your V's piercings or layered parts couldn't be read, so a plain grey stands in for it."). Tests: `tests/layered-material.test.ts`, `tests/character-material-adapters.test.ts`.
- **PREV-77:** bakes are sized per side (`layeredBakeExtent`): a domain wider than tall gets a wider target (an eyeball spanning three tiles no longer gets a square one), and the material and evidence carry the height. Style 09 black's ring strip (a UV domain 0.99 wide and 0.15 tall) now bakes at 1024 × 256 instead of 256², the density along U the square bake fell short of by about 2.6 times; the style's kept maps go from 2.0 to 4.2 MiB. The other half of the hypothesis was checked and kept as it was: the texel density divides the part's world area by its summed UV area, which is right for mirrored or overlapping UVs (each copy of a texel lies on the surface at that density); dividing by the covered area would size a mirrored part up by √2 for no visible gain. Test: a mirrored part gets its unmirrored half's bake (`tests/layered-material.test.ts`).
- **PREV-78:** the previous V is released after the new one has baked (`setCharacterDetails`), so a try can share a bake the previous V held; shared maps are credited to their longest-standing user still holding them, so the evidence sums to what the GPU keeps as users come and go. Tests: `tests/layered-material.test.ts`, the order in `tests/render-scheduler.test.ts`.
- **PREV-79:** the loader's texture ledger key includes the resource's `isGamma` flag (`textureLedgerKey`). Test: `tests/character-material-adapters.test.ts`.

## Fixed in claude/cleanup-layered

Findings PIPE-40 to PIPE-43, PREV-62 to PREV-68 and UI-48 to UI-51 from the `90b8602` layered material review, PREV-69 and UI-56 from the `fc36eae` studio light rig review, plus two additions asked for during the work: host/page version skew (seen live after a rebuild) and fast piercing tries (the reference installation took 7.6–10.1 s a try with everything cached, over 20 s the first time). Checked in the browser (`?verify=1`, reference MO2 profile, own port, disposable data) with the default V, the reference save, save B, style 09 black, style 12 gold and silver, style 14, a forced heart eye design, and switching reference ↔ B; measured against a build of `4036d07` on another port with the same data.

- **PIPE-40:** one creator-name rule, `isChoiceName` (1–127 characters, no control characters or `\ / < > "`), used by the record reader, the request parser, the host's choice list and the workspace. The record reader (`render-detail-6`) keeps its frame strict but leaves out a part, a choice, a colour or the applied override that breaks a rule, with one "Left out of the prepared details" note, and a slot left with nothing becomes unavailable with a plain line; parts beyond 96 are capped the same way. The host drops unusable creator names with a note (`slotChoices`), cuts layer CNames to the record's length, and writes the reader's own output (`parseCharacterDetail` before the identity), so what it writes always parses. Tests: `tests/render-detail-record.test.ts` (`piercings_(ccxl)`, a 200-character definition, a 130-character CName, 196 parts, a bad override; parse idempotence), `tests/piercing-resolver.test.ts` (host-side filtering with notes), `tests/character-detail-service.test.ts` (the written record equals its re-parse).
- **Version skew (added):** a record of a retired or newer schema throws `RenderDetailVersionError`; the host names the record schema it writes in every state and answers an unknown request version with `409 unsupported_version`; a host refusing a request this page's reader accepts counts as skew too. The device turns each into `DetailVersionSkewError`, the service publishes the typed notice `version-skew`, and the presentation words it ("XF Studio was updated while it was running. Restart it to see your V's …"). Tests: `tests/character-switch.test.ts` (older host, newer host, newer record, refused request, plain failure without a notice, the host's 409), `tests/detail-limits.test.ts` (wording from the code).
- **PIPE-41:** the resolver reads each render chunk's `renderMask` (`chunkInScene`) and the planner keeps only `MCF_RenderInScene` chunks; `LAYERED_SLOTS` is gone, so a layered chunk draws on any slot that brings it. An explicit `lodMask` of 0 now means no level of detail (it was read as the top level); the reference save's CCXL hair brings a one-triangle proxy with in-scene chunks at `lodMask` 0, which stays out, and the hair stays shown. A part of a slot that can't be served no longer blanks the slot when others drew ("Some of your V's hair couldn't be read…"). Tests: `tests/character-detail-plan.test.ts` (shadow-only proxy out, in-scene layered chunk drawn on the hair slot, `lodMask` 0 out), `tests/character-detail-service.test.ts` (partial slot). `knowledge/head-cc-rendering.md` §4 and Q3 updated.
- **PIPE-42:** a tried choice is a switcher choice (its `localizedName`, the creator's choice identity) plus a definition of the option it drives (its link controller). `applyChoiceOverride` drops everything the slot's switchers can reach and derives the replacement with `descriptorsFromSwitcher` (the shared R5 rules rooted at the switcher, so every target and each linked follower's colour index resolve like the creator); `detailSlotOf` puts a switcher target on another creator slot into the switcher's detail slot. Request `xfs/character-request-3`. Test: several targets, a linked follower on its own slot, a replaced follower, in `tests/piercing-resolver.test.ts`.
- **PIPE-43:** `readSetup`/`readTemplate` clamp every number to `LAYER_RANGES` and cap override tables at 4096 entries (the largest vanilla table, a paint template's colours, holds 906; a first cap of 256 turned style 09's black plastic white in the browser check and was raised); `layerBakeParameters` clamps again. Each record has a decoded-texel budget (256 Mi texels; the host leaves textures beyond it out with a note, the loader refuses a part over it). Tests: `tests/layered-material.test.ts` (1e38 tiles, NaN, 50 layers, 5000-entry tables, a vanilla-sized table read whole).
- **PREV-62:** on `webglcontextrestored` the scene forgets the renderer's shared bakes, resets every layered handle (`contextRestored`: hidden, pending) and bakes again. Tests: a real loss and restore beside the plate's probe (`tests/webgl-plate-composite.test.ts`: without the restore path the part draws differently, with it within 0.2 %), the handle in `tests/layered-material.test.ts`, the wiring in `tests/render-scheduler.test.ts`.
- **PREV-63:** bakes are sized by texel density on the part's own surface (20 texels a millimetre from its triangles' world and UV areas; 256–2048), accumulate in RGBA16F, keep two packed 8-bit maps (sRGB colour with roughness in alpha, normal with metalness in alpha; the lit material reads the alphas), share one bake between chunks of identical stack, domain and size, and skip hidden slots until shown. Save B, style 09 black (5 layered chunks): kept maps 160 MiB (5 × 1024², three RGBA16F maps with mips) → 2.0 MiB (5 × 256², 3 distinct bakes, 0.67 MiB each); transient accumulation per bake 96 MiB → 3 MiB. With the heart eye design added: 288 MiB → 44.7 MiB (the eyeball stays 2048², as its UVs span several tiles: 128 → 42.7 MiB). `renderer.info.memory` for that V: 102 → 88 textures, 25 → 21 geometries. Parity: within one byte of the CPU reference for the constant stack and for a non-constant stack (tile 2, an offset, a mask differing by row and column, an sRGB colour map) at all 256 texels; a flat baked part lit beside a plain standard material of the same surface agrees within 6 %, and the same maps read the default way (roughness G, metalness B) differ by more than 25 % (this caught the packed-channel shader injection replacing text that is still an `#include` at compile time). Tests: `tests/webgl-display.test.ts`, `tests/layered-material.test.ts`, `tests/character-material-adapters.test.ts` (a 4 mm part bakes at 256²).
- **PREV-64:** researched in the resolver's component JSON: every vanilla and framework piercing component binds `parentTransform` (`entHardTransformBinding`) and `skinning` (`entSkinningBinding`) to the entity's `root` animated component, with no slot, so the data names no head bone to follow [resource]. The rigid part stays bound in place and now says so: limit `rigid-part` ("A piercing part stays in place while your V's head moves in the idle…"); how the engine moves an unskinned mesh in a skinned component is a hypothesis for an in-game check. Test: `tests/character-detail-geometry.test.ts`.
- **PREV-65:** after each bake draw the handle checks the program diagnostics, the framebuffer status and `gl.getError`; any failure reports `failed` with its reason and the slot's limit code. Test: a renderer stand-in in `tests/layered-material.test.ts` (unlinkable program, incomplete framebuffer, WebGL error, no half-float target).
- **PREV-66:** the clear, layer and resolve programs and the quad are made once per renderer (`bakeKit`). Test: three bakes draw with the same three materials.
- **PREV-67:** `stackProblems`: the `layered-mask` limit only for a mask the host could not read (no layer, or a layer inside the mask's count without its image); a layer whose `.mltemplate` could not be read is recorded as `templateUnreadable`, left out of the bake and noted. Tests: `tests/layered-material.test.ts`, `tests/character-material-adapters.test.ts`.
- **PREV-68 (widened):** the host keeps a `CharacterPreparationCache` per installation fingerprint (which includes the installation registry's generation). It is layered on the registry's long-lived `Installation` from `claude/prepare-speed` (the opened archives, the graph and the memoised merged creator resource stay the registry's), and holds only what preparations derive from it: each resolved descriptor, interpreted templates, setups, profiles and gradients, exports, and each served component by its plan; a reopened installation (new archives, `depot`) resets it, while the registry's per-acquire view objects and graph replacements do not. Going back to the V's own is also shown as on its way ("Going back to your V's own piercings…"). so a try re-plans from the resolved V and resolves and exports only the tried slot; it works with a long-lived `Installation` passed in. Exported files are hashed once per path, size and time. The browser keeps the V visible and interactive during a try (status stays `ready` with `trying`; the character panel says "Trying Style 14…"; the full "Preparing" line is for a V's first preparation), and the loader takes over every unchanged component (objects, materials, bakes, textures through a shared ledger) so only the changed slot loads. Measured on the reference profile: a cached try 0.27 s on the host and 0.3–0.7 s in the browser end to end (was 7.6–10.1 s); the first try of the framework's style 12, which needs six WolvenKit reads, 38.9 s (6.1 s resolving the framework's 128-slot appearance cold, 32.7 s exporting); a new V on the same installation reused 9 of 10 parts. Tests: `tests/piercing-resolver.test.ts` (one open, only the tried part exported, back to the V's own exports nothing and gives the same record), `tests/character-detail-geometry.test.ts` (reuse and ownership), `tests/character-switch.test.ts` (no overlay phase during a try).
- **UI-48:** `CharacterDetailActions` owns the tried choice with the typed `character.tryChoice` action (family `characterDetails`, separate from the CC work's `characterContext` family that shares the `character.*` namespace, as the action catalogue documents; its own capability check against the V's choices); the presentation dispatches it and reads `previewState().character`; `preview.setPiercingPreview`, the preview port's piercing callbacks and `PreviewConfig.piercingStyle` are gone, and the workspace stores the service's value. Tests: `tests/studio-application.test.ts`, `tests/studio-registry.test.ts` (golden updated), `tests/character-switch.test.ts`.
- **UI-49:** the host gives one label per style and per colour (`styleLabel`, shared with the slot label); the `1024` sentinel and the device's wording are gone.
- **UI-50:** `projects/xf-studio/README.md` and the public site describe piercings, brows, lashes and hair as rendered from the player's own files; the localhost host refuses `/assets/prc/` and `/assets/piercings/` (the desktop host serves only the derived core files), and the boundary test checks it. The ignored payloads in the main checkout were left in place.
- **UI-51:** the workspace keeps the tried style only when both names pass `isChoiceName`; the service reads it with the host's rules (`validOverride`), clears it once the V's choices show it is not offered (never re-sent), and clears it when another V is loaded: a tried style belongs to the V it was tried on (decision recorded in the service's header). Tests: `tests/character-switch.test.ts`.
- **PREV-69:** the studio rig's `setStudioLights` is part of `createBrowserScenePreviewPorts`; the head attachment no longer spreads it in. Test: `tests/trusted-preview-services.test.ts` (the port carries it, the attachment has no spread).
- **UI-56:** studio controls under the creator preset are refused as `incompatible_mode`. Test: `tests/studio-lighting.test.ts`.
- **Test gaps:** the GPU parity test with non-constant maps, the real exporter's `masks()` path (`tests/character-detail-service.test.ts`: layer folder, cache, by hash), and piercing identifiers in the boundary regex (`piercings_\d\d`, `earring__\d\d`, `i0_000_pwa`, `i1_000_pwa`).

## Fixed in claude/cleanup-render3

Findings PREV-57 to PREV-61 from the `4bf688a` plate blend review. Measurements: headless Chrome (ANGLE, D3D11, RTX 4070); in-app captures in `?verify=1` with disposable data, 732×788 viewport, fixed face camera, idle off, experiment 016's Board 1 and a Shimmer-over-Glossy look (Glossy 50 % with Shimmer fine 50 % over it on one lid, Shimmer fine 50 % alone on the other), both lighting presets, before and after. The default V's details were still loading in that workspace, so the plate lit with the standard light over the core head. Private captures stay in the ignored evidence tree.

- **PREV-57:** the composite pass moved to `src/engines/layered-makeup/render/plate-composite.ts` and now follows the export's faceted chain. The layers accumulate premultiplied into an accumulation target; a resolve pass writes the merged decal as `encodeSurface` stores it: the merged normal per texel, rounded to the export's bytes (flat where nothing covers), and that normal's squared length. The GPU builds that target's mips once (box means, which are `facetedMipChain`'s), and one draw per level writes the export's roughness chain (level 0 unwidened, lower levels (r⁴ + E[|n|²] − |E[n]|²)^¼, zero outside coverage) into a single-channel target the plate samples as the game samples the exported map. The normals are two-channel. Test: `tests/webgl-plate-composite.test.ts` (Shimmer at 30–100 % over Glossy at 50 % on a 16-texel grid through the stack's own layer materials, against `facetedMipChain` of the compiler's bytes for the same texels): half-float within 0.56 of a byte step for roughness and 0.51–0.57 for coverage at levels 0 and 2, 1.12 for the normal at level 0 (half-float sums rounding a tie one byte away) and 0.55 at level 2; at the Shimmer 50 % over Glossy 50 % texel the export's roughness is 0.353 and the composite's 0.354, where the earlier composite gave 0.444 at level 0. In the app, Board 1 (flat, no facets) is unchanged (max 3 levels, no pixel over 2); in the Shimmer-over-Glossy look 0.07 % (studio) and 0.08 % (creator) of the frame changes, by up to 10 and 22 levels, all in the Shimmer sparkle over Glossy, whose facets are no longer dulled at level 0; the Shimmer alone changes only faintly (bilinear filtering no longer widens between texel centres). GPU memory for the 469 × 153 half-float composite: 2.30 → 3.54 MB (the accumulation target and the roughness chain).
- **PREV-58:** `makeup.contextRestored()` marks the blend dirty. The rest of the frame changed because the studio environment's prefiltered target came back empty (every surface lost its image-based light): `src/studio-environment.ts` owns it and `restore()` prefilters again and copies into the same texture, so the creator preset's saved studio state keeps working. `scene.ts` calls both on `webglcontextrestored` (already a render trigger). Tests: a real context loss and restore in `tests/webgl-plate-composite.test.ts` (without the hooks the plate and the environment-lit skin read black; with them both return to their pixels exactly), `tests/plate-blend.test.ts` (the next frame redraws the composite), and the wiring in `tests/render-scheduler.test.ts`.
- **PREV-59:** either `EXT_color_buffer_float` or `EXT_color_buffer_half_float` makes half float renderable (Three.js enables both; Chrome offers the latter alone on some GPUs), so the linear path, the composite and the prefiltered environment use either. With neither, the creator preset renders into an 8-bit sRGB target (the GPU encodes on write and blends in linear light; values clip at one before the grade) and the studio environment becomes a light probe of `RoomEnvironment`'s spherical harmonics (`ROOM_ENVIRONMENT_SH`, recomputed on the GPU by the test; its diffuse light is 7.5 % below the prefiltered room's on white matte), so neither preset goes dark and no framebuffer is incomplete. The darkened stage was the prefilter's half-float target. The preset stays offered: it works. Tests: `tests/webgl-display.test.ts` reruns its page with both extensions hidden (no WebGL or shader errors; creator decal blends within one level of the half-float target's), `tests/lighting-preset.test.ts` (target type and colour space per extension set). In the app with both hidden, neither preset is dark: the creator frames differ from the half-float build's by 2–9 % mean luminance, within the run-to-run spread of when the colour grade arrives in a fresh workspace (two runs of the old build differed by 8 % on the same look), so the probe's measurement is the controlled one; the studio stage is 11 % lower in mean luminance than the linear path with the prefiltered room (drawn straight to the canvas, no sharp reflections).
- **PREV-60:** a later skin of the same plate is copied into the existing underlay attributes (`copyArray`, one upload each); a different vertex count frees the geometry's GPU buffers before replacing them; no skin leaves them in place, unread. Test: `tests/plate-blend.test.ts`.
- **PREV-61:** only the merged target asks for mips, so the renderer generates them once, after the resolve; the accumulation target has none and the roughness chain is drawn level by level. Mip generations per drawn frame during a one-layer opacity edit: Board 1 (6 layers) 19 → 4, the Shimmer-over-Glossy look (3 layers) 10 → 4 (3 for the composite, 1 for the edited mask's upload); 12 layers in the GPU test: 3 per update (was 36).
- **Tests the review asked for:** the merged slots equal `planPresetExport`'s included layers across seven presets mixing flat finishes, game-matched and earlier Glossy and Shimmer, Glitter, one- and two-pigment and earlier Colour-shifting, disabled and opacity-0 layers (`tests/plate-blend.test.ts`); the 8-bit composite path against the export (within 2 byte steps at levels 0 and 2). A V switch re-reading the skin under the plate stays covered by the stack's own test (a new underlay source is read once on the next frame, with its skin light) and the source check in `tests/render-scheduler.test.ts`; a behavioural test through `setCharacterDetails` needs the scene split (UI-11).

## Fixed in claude/cleanup-platform2

Findings CORE-38 to CORE-44 from the platform steps 3–4 review at `4bf688a`. Details, decisions and measurements are in the [design's cleanup after step 4](feature-module-platform.md#cleanup-after-step-4). The standard stored form of every fixture, the look-history parity golden, the registry golden and library bytes are unchanged; the 0.1.0-alpha.1 reader tests pass.

- **CORE-38:** Export collection and Export compiler plan save first only when the library takes the collection (`writeMinimal` gives `xfas/collection-1`); otherwise the draft snapshot is exported unsaved, and each path's message says plainly whether it was saved and why not. Test: `tests/alpha-library.test.ts` with the real `CollectionLibrary` (Save refused; both exports succeed, the collection file is `xfs/collection-2` with the hair part, the library and the draft's revision are unchanged; a storable collection is saved as revision 1 and exported as collection-1). It failed before the fix with the old message.
- **CORE-39:** `PartRegistry.writeMemory(memory, { lookLevel })`, threaded through `serializeWorkspace` and `writeCollectionWorkspace`; `WorkspacePlan.lookLevel`; `fitWorkspace` tries the look-level standard plan after the whole-part one and trims in that form (unless it is not smaller). `storesLookLevelHistory` lets the desktop store write a look-level workspace back in that form. Reviewer's heavy look at 2,000,000 units: 4 → 80 steps kept. Tests: `tests/look-history.test.ts` (standard → look-level → trimming order with both forms restoring the same histories; a 32-layer look whose whole-part floor does not fit keeps all 80 steps, and the pre-step-4 reader refuses that form as newer data; the reload gate now derives its budget from the look-level floor and checks that whole parts could not hold it), `tests/workspace-persistence.test.ts` (16 presets store look-level with nothing trimmed and status `saved`; a tighter budget trims and says so), `desktop/tests/workspace-store.test.ts` (a look-level post is written back look-level, less than half the whole-part size, with the same histories).
- **CORE-40:** `LookHistory.fromData` maps each stored chunk to its local address once and retains it for later references. Heavy look: `fromData` 24 → 1.8 ms, restore 24 → 2.8 ms, preset switch 35 → 13 ms.
- **CORE-41:** `fitWorkspace(state, model, budget, from)` gallops each stage from the previous fit's value; `WorkspacePersistence` passes its last plan. The desktop store parses the previous file once per save (not at all when it is the text it last wrote or checked) and the new text once, and reads the previous schema from that parse. Heavy look: over-budget autosave 57 → 37 ms (the remaining cost is mostly the whole-part standard encoding, kept so the older-build form is used whenever it fits); 32-layer large fixture: autosave 22.7 → 6.1 ms, desktop save 164 → 67 ms. Tests: a hinted search equals the cold one for every pairing of seven budgets; the desktop test counts `JSON.parse` calls per text.
- **CORE-42:** a labelled checkpoint equal to the top step relabels that step; a transaction without its own step names the top step at commit when its change is kept, and `cancel()` restores the start through `TransactionHost.revert(undefined)` without taking the step off (`AuthoringHistory.revertTransaction`). Tests: the platform transaction test (de-duplicated gesture cancelled, control committed and returned to start) and an app-level session: a gesture moved and back, a second gesture cancelled with Escape (the step stays), an opacity edit (undone as "Opacity"), Undo, and an Escape-cancelled gesture that leaves Redo available.
- **CORE-43:** the scan covers `export … from`, bare `import "…"`, dynamic `import("…")` and inline `import("…").T`, with its own test; it revealed no violation, so no exception was recorded.
- **CORE-44:** `RecipeActions.dispatch` and `capability` removed (their tests use `app.dispatch` or the registered apply); `applyRecipeAction` `field.add` and `editLayers` add/duplicate refuse a missing ID; `editPresets` takes `newId` in add and copy commands, filled by `CollectionSession` from its host's ID source, and capability refuses an ID in use. Tests: `tests/application-actions.test.ts` (deterministic `editPresets`, the session's ID source, the refusals), `tests/layer-stack.test.ts`.

## Fixed in claude/multilayer-piercings

- **UI-02 (completed):** piercings render from the resolver: the `piercings` slot (creator slot `piercings_color`, face groups), each drawing component with its chunk mask, `multilayered.mt` chunks through the new layered adapter, and any framework that replaces a style's `.app` by archive precedence alone (the reference profile's jewellery framework shows its filled slots). Removed: `piercing-preview.ts`, `piercing-palette.ts`, `tools/intake_prc.ts`, `tools/intake_piercings.ts`, their tests, the scene's manifest loading, `prcError`/`prcAvailable`/`piercingError` in the status and the PRC note in the preview panel, and `matchedPiercing`. The piercing style and colour controls now list the creator's options from the record and ask the host for the V with the tried choice. Tests: `tests/piercing-resolver.test.ts` (vanilla, framework replacement, Off, override, choices, requests), `tests/layered-material.test.ts` (bake maths), `tests/render-detail-record.test.ts` (v5 schema; the boundary test now covers piercings with no exception), `tests/character-switch.test.ts` (tried style keeps the V on screen), `tests/webgl-display.test.ts` (the bake on a real GPU against its CPU reference).
- **PIPE-11 (piercing part):** see the table.

## Fixed in claude/platform-step4

Step 4 of the [feature-module platform](feature-module-platform.md#step-4-status). Details, decisions and measurements are in the design's step 4 status.

- **CORE-02 (completed):** one Undo history per look (`LookHistory`: part and look steps, a content-addressed chunk store, Redo), in memory in every look's memory, so preset switches, draft copies and autosave captures no longer repeat each step's whole recipe (large fixture: in-memory draft 2,250,819 → 551,846 code units, draft snapshot 32 → 2.5 ms, preset switch 3.6 → 1.0 ms). `fitWorkspace` keeps the standard policy and trims in the design's order, keeping at least 10 steps of the selected look unless the looks alone leave no room (the old smallest level kept none); a quota refusal fits a smaller budget. The stored form is unchanged for every history this build makes (byte-identical on every fixture; the previous build's reader, vendored in `tests/fixtures/pre-step4/`, reads it), and a history only the look-level form holds opens older builds read-only. Tests: `tests/look-history.test.ts` (chunk store and collisions, part and look steps, limit, trimming, stored forms and the previous reader, damaged steps, the platform transaction, `fitWorkspace` order, the reload gate, the size benchmark), `tests/look-history-parity.test.ts` (golden from `0885ba6`: every fixture's histories, stored form and a scripted session).
- **CORE-11 (part):** gesture and form-control transactions share the platform's `HistoryTransaction` with their rules as policies; spec `label` and the gestures' `label` name Undo steps from the registry.
- **CORE-36:** library requests and file workflows are registered asynchronous families (`LIBRARY_FAMILY`, `FILES_FAMILY`; `AsyncSystemFamily`, `Registry.routeAsync`), bound by `StudioApplication` (`StudioOwnerRequests`) and routed through the registry by `requestCapability`/`execute` and `fileCapability`/`executeFile`, which the collection application and the presentation's `files` port now use; the request, gesture and file catalogues derive from the registry (the golden registry snapshot is unchanged). Gesture proposals are eye makeup's `gestures.descriptors`.
- **Not done (recorded):** the per-look "not editable in this version" state moves to step 5 with the presentation; the Undo policy keeps the name `recipe` until the port changes.

## Fixed in claude/cleanup-render2

Findings PREV-50 to PREV-56 from the `bb2cc22` rendering review. Measurements: headless Chrome `?verify=1` (ANGLE, D3D11, RTX 4070), 510×888 viewport, face camera, idle off; the default V, the reference save, save B and the reference save again, both lighting presets, dark and light themes.

- **PREV-50:** `src/linear-display.ts` replaces `creator-display.ts` and serves both presets: the scene renders into one half-float 4× MSAA target. For the Studio stage, one coverage pass sets alpha to one wherever a surface wrote depth, Three draws the stage backdrop on the canvas as before, and one output pass applies the renderer's own ACES tone mapping at the stage exposure and sRGB encoding, laid over the backdrop by coverage. The canvas has no MSAA or depth; without `EXT_color_buffer_float` the Studio stage draws straight to a multisampled canvas as before (`frameTiming().display.path` says which). Look parity against the previous build on the default V: forehead and cheek patches max 1 level (mean 0.05 to 0.09), the backdrop byte-identical, the creator preset identical for all four Vs (one pixel off by one level). The differences are the intended ones: blended passes (face decals, brows, eye shell, makeup-plate edges) now match the linear solve, and sub-pixel coverage (hair, lashes) resolves in linear light, so thin dark strands and the reference save's blush and lipstick read lighter. Idle: no frame drawn in 4 s with nothing changing. Test: `tests/webgl-display.test.ts` (dark liner over skin 98,70,57 against the solve's 97,70,57, where the old path showed 41,32,28; lipstick 189,127,115 exact, old 172,94,94; opaque surface within 1 level; backdrop identical) and `tests/lighting-preset.test.ts` (pass order and restored renderer state).
- **PREV-51:** the face-decal adapter returns how the skin under it was read (`decalSurface`), the loader keeps it on the decal entry, and the scene evidence reads it there; the scene's `WeakMap` is gone. Tests: the adapter keeps the evidence, and the evidence reader takes it from the entry.
- **PREV-52:** the underlay carries metalness (`xfsUnderMetalness`: the resolved skin's Roughness map G, zero on the core head and the flat fallback), and the shader mixes metalness from it by the surface share, as `forwardSurface` does. Tests: placement sampling, shader text.
- **PREV-53:** the host serves only the drawn chunks (`glb.ts` `keepGlbMeshes`: nodes kept, sparse POSITION/NORMAL morph deltas, no TANGENT deltas, target names unchanged), keyed in the store by the export's hash, the chunk list and a version (`storeChunkGeometry`); a file it can't read is served whole with a note. The loader parses each file once and clones objects and skeletons for a second component. Budget on the same Vs: default 78.6 → 58.2 MiB, reference 138.1 → 99.9, save B 86.8 → 63.4, and the scar and tattoo test V 151.2 → 72.9 MiB, its face slot 71.3 → 13.1 MiB (scar mesh 43.5 → 0.6 MiB). With the new files the creator-preset renders were identical to before (one pixel differed by one level). Tests: `tests/character-detail-geometry.test.ts`.
- **PREV-54:** `render-templates.ts` lists each template's `required` inputs (`decalRequired` for the face family); a chunk is dropped only when one of those is unreadable, and an optional or recorded-for-later input earns a note. Test: an eye without its `Normal` still draws; without `Albedo` the eyeball chunk is left out.
- **PREV-55:** the by-hash fallback runs only when the archive index lists the texture, and up to `BY_HASH_CONCURRENCY` (4) launches at a time, each into its own folder; the first failure stops new launches and surfaces after the others finish. Test with a fake WolvenKit: six launches peak at four and take two rounds; an unreadable index launches nothing.
- **PREV-56:** `tests/webgl-display.test.ts` bundles `tests/webgl-probe-page.ts` and runs it in headless Chrome through `tools/cdp.ts` (about 4 s; skipped without Chrome, failing under `XFS_REQUIRE_ORACLES=1`): every face-decal variant (plain, double diffuse, gradient recolour; with and without underlay and skin light), the brows, skin, eyeball, shell and display passes compile and draw with no shader error (51 programs).
- **Design note (not a finding):** the Studio's own makeup plate blended in linear light, so its soft edges read lighter than the game's square-root blend. Done in claude/plate-blend: `src/engines/layered-makeup/render/plate-blend.ts` gives every exportable layer the face decals' per-vertex skin underlay and forward solve, plus a UV-space target of the blended layers below it, so the preview lands on the export's merged decal over the skin (see [head CC rendering §3](../../knowledge/head-cc-rendering.md#3-the-head-decal-family)).

## Fixed in claude/cleanup-platform

Findings of the platform steps 1–3 review. Details, decisions and measurements are in the [design's cleanup section](feature-module-platform.md#cleanup-after-steps-13).

- **CORE-27:** `NewerDataError` (`platform/api/document.ts`) is thrown for a part schema or layer model this build does not know, wherever it sits (current draft, recovery drafts, removed presets, loose look, Undo histories), and is never treated as damage. `loadWorkspace` opens such a workspace read-only (`NewerPolicy` `omit`: its current draft without the newer entries, `writable: false`, a plain protected message), so nothing is written back. The desktop store loads a workspace whose current draft is readable, refuses to replace one holding newer data and never sets that draft aside; only an unreadable current draft is set aside by Start fresh. `writeMemory` writes each Undo history in the oldest part schema that holds every entry, and workspace looks use `minimalLook`; the step-2 code (`2dced39`) read the small, loose and large fixtures written this way as writable with the same content. Tests: `tests/newer-data.test.ts` (five placements, a newer current draft, damage still dropped, desktop load/save/Start fresh, minimal histories, a part-1-only registry as the step-2 read path).
- **CORE-28:** `lastPackageIsCurrent()` compares `[content, collection ID, saved revision, selected look, live revision]` with the key recorded at the request; `CollectionSession.snapshot()` no longer stashes into the draft. Test: `tests/package-freshness.test.ts` (large fixture: 75.2 ms before, 0.011 ms after, per repaint; stale after an edit, a preset switch and a rename; current after a new Check; the draft is unchanged by reads).
- **CORE-29:** `DocumentModel` (`collection-workspace.ts`) and `StudioComposition` (`trusted-authoring-core.ts`) are passed in by `studio-startup`, `server.ts` and `desktop/server.ts`; nothing else imports `compose/`. Eye makeup's types moved to `src/eye-makeup-model.ts`, the history and collection family types to their services, `StudioOwnerActions` to `StudioApplication`. The package pipeline reads eye-makeup parts with `parseEyeMakeupPart`. Boundary tests: only `compose/` and roots import `compose/`, and no module outside them reaches `features/` or `compose/` transitively.
- **CORE-30:** the design doc states the alpha's exact limit; the library refuses a save that would write `xfs/collection-2` (`COLLECTION_2_LIBRARY_MESSAGE`), and the desktop `.bak` is refreshed whenever a version-1 file is replaced. Tests: `tests/alpha-library.test.ts` with the release's `list()` vendored from `60a60e9` (`tests/fixtures/alpha-0.1.0/collection-list.ts`): it lists everything this build saves, and one collection-2 row makes it fail; the desktop `.bak` refresh test.
- **CORE-31:** form controls apply through the registered `apply` and the eye-makeup port; gesture frames through the module's registered `gestures` provider (`GestureProvider`, `applyRecipeGesture`) and the port. `AuthoringLayerActions` is deleted and its tests dispatch through `app.dispatch`. Test: `tests/registered-editing.test.ts` counts registered apply and gesture calls from dispatch, controls and gestures.
- **CORE-32:** eye-makeup dispatch records Undo by Undo policy; context-menu input options use `undoPolicyOf` for their variant. Tests: a content action registered with policy `none` records nothing; every option's policy equals its variant's.
- **CORE-33:** `field.add` carries `fieldId` and layer `add`/`duplicate` carry `newId`; the spec's `assignIds` fills them from the host's ID source (the port's `newId`), the registered apply refuses an action without them, and capability refuses an ID in use. The purity test covers all 29 kinds, deep-frozen, applied twice with identical results.
- **CORE-34:** package and build-plan capability need a look with eye makeup; the eye-makeup view lists looks without eye makeup and other features' parts in `omitted`, reported by Check, Build and the manifest (a new `part` omission kind; looks count in the original preset count; the packaged copy and hash never carry the list). Pipeline guide and its authoring diagram updated (visual review recorded). Test: `tests/package-omitted-looks.test.ts`.
- **CORE-35:** each stored part is parsed once on restore (`part()`/`canonicalParts` use in-memory bodies), sessions copy instead of re-parsing (a JSON copy), preset edits validate only the looks, and SQLite `save()` reads the previous revision number only. Large fixture: `CollectionService` construction 70 → 27 ms (604 parses → 0), draft snapshot 150 → 59 ms, stored restore 41 → 38 ms (169 → 137 parses), unchanged `save()` 11.6 → 8.1 ms.
- **CORE-36:** recorded as a design gap in [§4](feature-module-platform.md#4-routing-and-the-registry-derived-catalogue-core-03-core-08): library requests and file workflows are asynchronous and gesture proposals run inside a gesture session, so they join as families with the look history (step 4); gesture frames already apply through eye makeup's registered provider.
- **CORE-37:** `withPreferences` gives the workspace-1 fixtures schema'd UI preferences with a dock layout and tour progress (`tests/workspace-preferences.test.ts`: restore, every budget level and a second restore keep them); the CORE-27/30 tests above; the purity test covers 29 kinds; the browser-globals check catches `globalThis`, `sessionStorage`, `navigator` and `fetch(`, with its own test.
- **CORE-11:** closed (the routing tests landed in step 1). **CORE-05:** completed with CORE-28.

## Fixed in claude/cleanup-polish

Low findings from the `84cb39d` review, plus two render-review nits.

- **PREV-48:** `lighting-preset-stage.ts` notifies whenever the cube changes (`file` differs), not only when the source description does, so the scene's lighting subscription asks for a frame. Test in `tests/lighting-preset.test.ts`: an updated LUT file with an identical source notifies once; the same cube again stays quiet.
- **UI-45:** `render-scheduler.ts` keeps an in-frame flag: a request made while a frame runs (the controls' `change` inside `controls.update()`) only marks the scheduler dirty, and no longer resets `previous`, so the next dt covers the whole interval. Test: a frame that requests another 5 ms in has each dt equal to the time between frame starts (it failed before). Measured in headless Chrome with the idle playing and three orbit drags per window: the idle advanced at 0.966-0.976 × wall time before, 1.0015-1.0021 × after.
- **UI-46:** `src/device-pixel-ratio.ts` (`viewportPixelRatio`, capped at 2; `watchDevicePixelRatio`) re-arms a `(resolution: Ndppx)` query after each change. `scene.ts` applies the new ratio, resizes and invalidates, and releases the watch with the scene. Tests in `tests/device-pixel-ratio.test.ts`. Not confirmed in a browser: headless Chrome's device-metrics emulation fires no media-query `change` events (a plain listener stayed silent too).
- **UI-47:** `surface-editor.ts`'s `rehover` ends with `viewer.requestRender?.()`: the release is stopped before the viewport's own pointer trigger sees it. Test: `tests/surface-rehover.test.ts` (after a shape drag released over the interior, the hover highlight is gone without another move).
- **PREV-51, PREV-52, PREV-53, PREV-54, PREV-55, PREV-56:** Fixed in claude/cleanup-render2 (see below).
- **UI-41:** F6 and Shift+F6 include the visible `.guidance-callout` regions (tour card, onboarding offer), after the status bar; focus lands on the callout's primary action, never its close button. Checked in headless Chrome `?verify=1`: F6 from the status bar reached the tour card's Next, and the offer's "Show me around".
- **UI-42:** the controller reads the step index before awaiting `runCommand`, so `then: "next"` cannot advance a second time after a paint moved the tour on.
- **UI-43:** `guidance.startTour` ends a running tour through `end(…, "skipped")` first, so the outcome is recorded and readers see it end. The controller keys the shown step by tour and index, so a new tour starting at step 1 is announced. Test in `tests/guidance.test.ts`. In `?verify=1`, starting What's new on onboarding's second step recorded onboarding as skipped and announced What's new step 1 of 4.
- **UI-44:** the Help topic and the onboarding finish step carry tokens (`{{finishes.export}}`, `{{finishes.mod}}`) that `guidance/finish-text.ts` fills from the catalogue the port publishes (`rt.finishes`, from `authoring.finishCatalogue()`), through `helpTopicsFor` and `toursFor`; the presentation's value-import allowlist did not grow. Test: the data names no finish, the filled text has no token left, each finish appears once in the sentence of its export status, and moving Glitter to experimental changes the words.
- **Render-review nits:** `detail-limits.ts`'s comment no longer claims no device writes user sentences: it says the codes cover partial drawing only, and that the character-detail loader still words its load failures (the wording itself stays in the loader for now). The name-based "every scene mutator is wrapped" source test documents its limits (name prefixes, layout-dependent key reading, wrapping versus effect).

## Fixed in claude/platform-step3

- **CORE-09:** step 3 of the [feature-module platform](feature-module-platform.md#step-3-status). Eye makeup's part is `xfs/eye-makeup-part-2`, the in-memory recipe with no recipe-level schema; each layer's Glitter and game-matched optics models are validated by their own IDs in the model registry, which also carries each model's oldest recipe schema. `selectGlitterModel`, `applyRecipeAction`, gestures and shape transforms no longer touch a schema, and `requiredRecipeSchema` (with its "never go below" pinning) is gone: writers derive the oldest schema that holds the layers, for "Export recipe", library rows, collection-1 files and part-1 bodies, so 0.1.0-alpha.1 reads more files than before. Recipe files keep their per-schema gates (checked exhaustively against the step-2 rule). A newer part schema or unknown layer model stays refused on read (decision recorded in the design). Parity with the step-2 code: masks, preview keys, export plans and compiled maps of every recipe schema and 27 Glitter edits beside Glossy; collection and workspace-1 fixtures; no new revisions for unchanged presets; the four-preset Build's 47 compared files byte-identical from the original, minimal and part-2 inputs.

## Fixed in claude/cleanup-hosts2

- **PREV-45:** `GradingLutHost` (`src/grading-lut-host.ts`) keeps the installation it opened for a fingerprint, so a retry runs no second source discovery; the creator environment is read again through a fresh `ResourceGraph` over the same indexes only when it failed. A transient failure (time limit, missing .NET, a process that could not start, a disk error) is retried on a request after `GRADING_LUT_RETRY_MS`, doubling each time, at most `GRADING_LUT_MAX_RETRIES` (5) times; meanwhile the previous answer stands and the note says when XF Studio tries again. A WolvenKit exit code, an unhandled exception in its log, or output that cannot be read or decoded is lasting for that decoded-LUT key (archive fingerprint, entry, decoder, WolvenKit identity) for the host session. A damaged cached cube is a miss; the cube and its key are written atomically. Tests in `tests/grading-lut.test.ts` (retry timing, cap, one discovery, lasting exit code, WolvenKit update, failed cache write).
- **PREV-46, PIPE-04 (rest):** `WolvenKitFetcher` computes `wolvenKitIdentityKey` once; cache files are `<hash>-<archive fingerprint>-<identity tag>.json`, and `.failed` markers (version 3) record the identity and count only for it. Existing JSON cache entries are extracted again once. Tests in `tests/resolver-fetcher-concurrency.test.ts`.
- **PREV-47:** `readArchiveIndex` (`src/resolver-host.ts`) writes the `.u64` cache through `writeFileAtomic` (now shared from `derived-cache.ts`) and reads it back only when its length is 8 × the file count the archive's index declares (`readRdarIndexCount` reads the header and the first 20 bytes of the index); otherwise it deletes the file and reads the archive again. Test in `tests/rdar-index-fs.test.ts`.
- **PREV-49:** a fetcher cache write that fails still answers with the extracted document (recorded as "not cached"), and a failed marker write is advisory. The LUT host's comments now say that a superseded preparation's WolvenKit run stops at once while discovery and a resource load already under way finish first.
- **PIPE-36:** the eye plate cache status records `routeKey` (`eyePlateRouteKey`: route settings, head choice, and size and time of the game's mod folder, its `modlist.txt`, the MO2 profile's `modlist.txt`, MO2's overwrite folder and the manual mod folder). `cachedPlateReach` requires the same route key, game folder, content fingerprint, recipe and the current window rule; both hosts pass the key. Without such a plate Check returns `plateUv: null` with `notes: ["Whether each look reaches the eye area is checked when you Build."]`, shown in the result card and the status message. A committed vanilla footprint was considered and rejected: it is every plate vertex UV and triangle of the CDPR head's eye area, verbatim game mesh data rather than asset-free metadata, and it would not describe a modded head. Tests in `tests/eye-plate.test.ts` and `tests/package-server.test.ts`.
- **PIPE-37:** `loadCached` and `cachedPlateReach` recompute the window from the recorded bounds and treat a mismatch as a miss (derived again once); a change to how the footprint is read from the mesh changes its schema. The resource builder's mismatch throws `PlateFootprintChangedError`, which the CLI reports as `package_plate_stale`; localhost `runLocalPackage` and desktop `runDesktopBuild` read the CLI's `XFS_PACKAGE_ERROR` code (`packageErrorCode`), `discardCachedPlate` that entry and build once more (once only). Tests in `tests/eye-plate.test.ts`, `tests/package-build-service.test.ts`, `tests/package-server.test.ts` and desktop `tests/build.test.ts`.
- **PIPE-38:** `mappingStats` counts `drawn` (map ≥ 1/255) and `authored` (reference ≥ 2/255) samples, and `checkMapping` refuses a map that is empty at the plate where the authored makeup reaches it, however faint. Test in `tests/plate-reach.test.ts` (at 2 % opacity an empty map's mean error is under the limit; it is now refused).
- **PIPE-39:** `mappingOffset` returns null below 24 authored-content samples or an edge strength (spread of the total error over the coarse shifts) below 8 coverage units. On lossless lid bakes a preset reaching 13 samples, and 0.5-1 % opacity, gave spurious 0.2-0.5 texel estimates; presets with at least 100 content samples and edge 24 estimate exactly 0. Tests in `tests/plate-reach.test.ts`.

## Fixed in claude/platform-step2

- **CORE-03 (data), CORE-05:** step 2 of the [feature-module platform](feature-module-platform.md#step-2-status). New `platform/api/document.ts` (part, editor and memory codecs, looks, `FeatureState`/`FeatureResult`), `platform/core/document.ts` (`PartRegistry`: collection-1/-2 reading, minimal writing, canonical comparison, per-feature memory), the eye-makeup part-1 codec and pure core in `features/eye-makeup/`, and `STUDIO_PARTS` in `compose/`. `CollectionSession`, `CollectionActions`, `CollectionService` and the SQLite store work on looks; `save()` and the service baselines compare canonically, which also fixes the step-1 store giving every migrated preset a new revision on its next save. The workspace is `xfs/workspace-2` (per-feature memory; workspace-1 read losslessly; desktop `.bak`). Library rows and exported files use `xfas/collection-1` whenever it holds the looks exactly, so 0.1.0-alpha.1 still reads them (checked by running that release's readers). CORE-05: `targetCapability`, `contextFor`/`contextQuery`, `requestCapability` and the Glitter-model preset lookup no longer clone or stash the draft (`targetCapability(preset)` 240 ms → 1 µs, `contextQuery(preset)` 1.56 s → 6 µs on a 6 × 16-layer draft with 80-step histories). Tests: `tests/look-model.test.ts` (golden workspace-1 restores from the step-1 code, recipe and collection parity at 512/1K/2K and compiled maps, a step-1 SQLite library, unregistered features, downgrade, pure apply, CORE-05), `desktop/tests/workspace-store.test.ts`; the four-preset Build's archive members are byte-identical before and after.
- **CORE-14 (partly):** the Glitter-model preset lookup no longer calls `workspaceSnapshot()` (which stashed and copied the whole draft on every Glitter-model or finish change). The shared mutable `glitterChoices` object remains, now as eye makeup's feature memory.

## Fixed in claude/platform-step1

- **CORE-08, CORE-15 (reason codes), part of CORE-03:** step 1 of the [feature-module platform](feature-module-platform.md#8-migration-plan). `STUDIO_REGISTRY` holds six system families (history, collection, preview, motion, quality, saved V) and the eye-makeup module. Each registers its existing descriptors as-is through a table keyed by its action union, so a missing or doubly owned kind fails to compile or to construct. `StudioApplication` reads kinds, descriptors, Undo policy, scene gating (`needsScene`) and the code for a failure after the gate (`thrown`) from the registry, and binds one handler per owner through a mapped type plus a constructor check.
